Step 1 had NO born-red state; the golden is what replaced it.** **CONTENT: `forward_with_kv_context_impl`, `build_and_realize_first_decode_token` and `forward_with_kv_context_persistent_inv_freq` are GONE from `LlamaModel` — 12-line delegations now — with the bodies in `persistent_decode.rs` as one `build_decode_graph` plus two tails, `DataConsts::{Baked,Rebindable}` the only D1/D2 difference inside the graph half.** A `DecodeBackbone` trait carries `decode_family` (threaded into every shared error, answering the diagnostics-lie consequence), `decode_dims` (with `rope_width` as a VALUE — Glm4's entire divergence is that one `usize`), `decode_shape_key`, `decode_mask_plan`, and the per-family hooks. **`MaskPlan` VALIDATES AT BUILD TIME — an out-of-range variant index is refused at construction rather than surfacing as a panic in the layer loop or, worse, as a silently mis-masked layer.** **N=1 BYTE-IDENTITY IS ASSERTED, NOT CLAIMED (`mask_variants_match_the_dense_builder_when_uniform`, 4 shapes) — AND IT MATTERED MORE THAN EXPECTED: the lane also repointed LLAMA'S REBIND-SIDE mask at the same builder, so had it not been byte-identical, EVERY EXISTING DECODE CARRIER WOULD HAVE CHANGED NUMERICS UNDER A REFACTOR ADVERTISED AS PRESERVING THEM.** **⚠️ ONE BEHAVIOUR DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED, AND THE REASONING IS THE POINT: old D2 did not check cache-vs-config KV geometry while D1 did. THE ASYMMETRY WAS PRESERVED, mode-gated, with a comment — because making D2 stricter would be A SEMANTIC FIX SMUGGLED INTO A BEHAVIOUR-PRESERVING REFACTOR, blunting the only oracle it has. Flagged as a real follow-up rather than silently inherited.** **SABOTAGE A DEFERRED TO AFTER STEP 2 WITH A STATED REASON — its question is *"do BOTH families route through the shared path"* and only one is on it yet — and it is ALSO what will establish that the golden DISCRIMINATES. Written into the golden's own doc: A GOLDEN NOBODY HAS SEEN FAIL IS A CONSTANT, NOT AN ORACLE. That red is owed.**
| GAP-031 | `fuel-ir` `BackendCapabilities`; `fuel-dispatch` ranker/placement; FKC | B | **The capability vocabulary has NO extent dimension — a kernel whose validity depends on a SHAPE rather than a dtype is unexpressible, so the optimizer cannot route around it.** `BackendCapabilities` keys support on `(OpKind, DType)` and carries alignment/granularity/transfer/substrate/throughput — nothing shape-valued. Consequence: a backend can advertise "I support Q4_0 matmul producing F32" but **not** "…only when M=1", so placement hands it work it must then reject at realize time, and with `pipelined.rs:798` fail-fast by contract that rejection is terminal. **This is the root cause of GAP-007** (CUDA `matmul_q4_0`/`matmul_q4_km`, verified broken above batch 1) and of **at least one sibling three lines away** (`k >= 64` on the same path) — so it is a class, not an instance, and fixing GAP-007 alone just moves the comment. **Fix shape (not yet ruled):** widen `BackendCapabilities` with an extent predicate, **or** let FKC carry an extent predicate the ranker consults — the latter is likely better aligned, since FKC already owns per-kernel contract claims and the ranker already reads it. **Sequencing note:** Vulkan already solves this *inside* one backend by dispatching `qmatvec_q4_0 (M=1)` vs `matmul_q4_0_tiled (M>1)`; that is the workaround shape, and it works only because the choice is internal to a backend that supports both. It cannot express "this backend can't do it at all — place elsewhere," which is what the optimizer needs. | OPEN |
| GAP-032 | cross-project: KISS `spec/classify.md` + `contract.md`; Unpopped `OperandDesc` | A | **Operand-invariance declaration + `prepare`/`step` split-kernel contract — HELD pending evidence, and now SPLIT IN TWO.** Follows the 2026-08-07 decisions-log entry declining runtime value memoization: what stays open is invariance **declared** and hoisted at build time, which a kernel generator can target because a generator emits structure, not cache policy. **(A) An operand-invariance hint on `OperandDesc`** — argued NOT a `structure_key` event, since a kernel that ignores the hint is still correct, so it is not an admissibility fact; additive, sits beside the existing `quant: Option<QuantFacts>` / `symbolic: Option<SymExtent>` precedent; Unpopped's lane; can ride a 0.2 breaking batch alone. **(B) A `prepare(invariant) -> state` / `step(state, varying)` split** — Unpopped suspects this IS a `structure_key` event; Fuel argues it is a **KISS-Contract** fact (invocation protocol) rather than KISS-Classify, because several kernels may accept the same cell and selection is the consumer's job. **Fuel's confidence here is explicitly limited** (reasoned from a partial read of `spec/classify.md`) and Unpopped was invited to push back. **Sequencing consequence: (A) is useful WITHOUT (B)** — a consumer can hoist a declared-invariant operand whether or not any kernel offers a split protocol — so the cheap half must not be held hostage to the expensive one, and only (B) needs KISS process. **Window facts (verified with Unpopped 2026-08-07, correcting a stale README read):** the generator carve is **COMPLETE**, not in progress — `unpopped` + `unpopped-vocab` are published at 0.1.0 and Baracuda's dep flip landed at `415f5c85`. So the "add it while the IR is being defined" urgency argument was **wrong**. Two later windows exist: a 0.2 breaking batch, and a larger restructure (Unpopped becomes a standard with a normative reference emitter per target; emitters move to sub-crates). **Unpopped's own position: no rush, get the evidence right** — they would rather wait than receive a proposal resting on an unfinished measurement. **DESIGN INPUT FROM GAP-014, 2026-08-07 — four findings, each of which would have broken the naive proposal** (obtained by asking the decode owner for GAP-014's lessons *before* writing anything up): **(i) A generation counter would have been ACTIVELY WRONG, not merely insufficient.** GAP-014 was **substitution** of a different same-shaped object, which a generation cannot see; and in Fuel mutation-in-place is **intended** (the held plan writes its own KV buffers every token), so generation-based invalidation would fire every token and destroy the reuse the mechanism exists for. **State is welded to a BUFFER, not to its contents.** A contract specifying generation-based invalidation would be "correct and useless". **(ii) "Enforced by construction" is weaker than the phrase suggests.** It was achievable in only 1 of 3 carriers (`DeviceKvPool`), and its proof is **the absence of an API** — absences are not enforced; nothing fails if someone later adds `replace_layer`. Flagged by its own author as a known weakness in what shipped, **not a pattern to copy**. Across a project boundary it is worse: the producer can add that API in a point release and the consumer's proof evaporates with no signal. **(iii) The cross-project asymmetry is the hard half and it is NEW.** Inside Fuel one can enumerate "everything the plan bakes" by reading the code; a consumer **cannot**, and the baked set changes on the **producer's** release cadence. So the contract needs a **producer-declared `prepare_key`** that changes whenever the prepared-from set changes, with the consumer obliged to treat an unrecognised *or* changed key as invalidating — **the producer's obligation to bump, because only the producer knows**. Without it this is GAP-014 with version skew instead of a pool slot: silent, full-speed, and *harder* to find because both sides look individually correct. **(iv) Conformance MUST require a negative control.** GAP-014's own first tests compared graph `NodeId`s, which are graph-local and re-minted on rebuild — so the check could not distinguish reuse from rebuild **in either direction** and one test was **passing vacuously**. A cross-project conformance test carrying that defect certifies nothing while looking green (GAP-144's shape). **UNPOPPED IMPLEMENTED THE ARTIFACT-VALIDITY RULE AND SHIPPED IT - `188d9c9` (2026-08-07).** They added `Provenance { structure_key, backend, provider, generator }` to `GeneratedKernel`/`Variant`, which previously named **no cell at all**, while their `jit.rs` documented a `Compiler` seam held open "so a pre-built-variant cache can slot in" - i.e. a cache whose key lived entirely *outside* the artifact. **Finding (iii) caught a real defect mid-edit, in another project.** They had written `Provenance { structure_key, backend, provider }` and were about to ship it. But **`structure_key` names what was ASKED FOR, not what was PRODUCED** - change a lowering rule and the same request yields different code under an unchanged token, so a consumer caching on the key alone serves a kernel from a generator that no longer exists, undetectably. That is precisely the producer-side version skew finding (iii) predicted. They added `generator: "unpopped <version>"`, **deliberately coarse so it over-invalidates**. **Findings (ii) and (iv) were applied too:** `provenance` is a **private field with a `pub(crate)` setter**, so a backend *cannot* stamp **at the type level** rather than being trusted not to - their direct answer to "absences are not enforced"; and every provenance test carries a **negative control** that a *different* cell yields a *different* stamp, with the vacuous-`NodeId` story written into the test module docs as the reason. **ROUTING (Unpopped's correction, to be honoured): this must NOT arrive as an Unpopped proposal.** It is a **KISS-Contract clause**, KISS owns that process, and their standing instruction is that KISS changes go through proper channels. **When Fuel takes it to KISS, cite `188d9c9` as PRIOR ART - do not name Unpopped a co-proposer.** **Posture is materially improved: two projects with implementations beats one project with a draft.** Fuel has the invariant at `14-lifecycle` v0.10 with four closed violations; Unpopped has an independent implementation with a cost report. Neither is a draft - which is also exactly what KISS's freeze gate (umbrella section 5.3) asks for. **Honest limit to carry into any write-up (Unpopped's own statement):** the stamp asserts *"produced in response to this request by this generator"*, **NOT** *"implements this cell"* - a backend returning a kernel built for another plan still gets the requested key stamped. **Verification remains the oracle's job.** Stated by them rather than left for consumers to over-trust. **Blocked on:** a constant-foldability measurement on a quantized graph, which is itself blocked on GAP-029 (no quantized decode surface) — see the decisions-log entry for why the originally-planned census cannot run. | HELD |
| GAP-033 | Fuel `pub struct`s on the sk4-touching identity/wire surface (`fuel-dispatch/src/fkc/**`, `revhash.rs`, `plan.rs`, telemetry) | A | **Reciprocal extensibility sweep — does Fuel have the growth-reservation hole Unpopped just found in themselves?** Origin: a schedule-only timing question to Unpopped (GAP-032) incidentally prompted them to sweep their published wire-format crate — 27 public structs with public fields, none reserved for growth, **while sk4 is an in-flight schema event touching identity types** — and they shipped `#[non_exhaustive]` on `StructureKey` + `OperandDesc` (`aaea608`). sk4 touches Fuel's identity types too, so the same question applies here. **METHOD — the naive sweep produces a WRONG to-do list, and both failure modes are recorded because both were nearly committed.** **(1) `#[non_exhaustive]` is NOT blanket-appliable.** Unpopped reserved only the two types with **real construction paths**; they explicitly could **not** reserve `ContractionKey`, `OperandKey`, `QuantFacts`, `SymExtent`, because those have **no public constructor at all** — marking them would make them **unconstructable by downstream code**, i.e. a functional break dressed as a compatibility fix. So the sweep has **two axes per struct**: (a) is it reserved, and (b) does it have a public constructor. Only "needs reservation **and** is constructible" is actionable. **(2) The architect's initial signal was a FILE count, not a struct count** (`non_exhaustive` in 2 files vs `pub struct` in 18 files, `fuel-dispatch/src/fkc`) — a file holds many structs, so that number supports **no conclusion in either direction** and must not be quoted as one. The real enumeration is **per-struct on both axes**. **(3) THIRD AXIS, added 2026-08-07 after GAP-049 — and this one guards against the sweep EXPANDING wrongly rather than listing wrongly.** This row is scoped to **`pub struct`s that are CARRIED** (identity/wire types forwarded without per-variant interpretation) — Unpopped's `StructureKey`/`OperandDesc` are the model. When GAP-049 broke the build, the natural next thought was "so mark the enums too". **That is wrong and would have made GAP-049 worse.** `#[non_exhaustive]` on an enum forces downstream **wildcard arms**, converting a compile error into a runtime fallthrough. For an enum that is **INTERPRETED exhaustively** — every variant demanding a distinct semantic action, no meaningful default — the exhaustive match *is* the enforcement mechanism, and the build break is the feature. `Scalar` is squarely that case. **Discriminating question before adding anything to this sweep: can a wildcard arm do something CORRECT?** If no, the type does not belong here regardless of how well it otherwise fits. **This row now has three ways to produce a wrong list — unreserved-but-unconstructible, file-count-as-struct-count, and carried-vs-interpreted — which is itself the argument for doing it deliberately.** Worth roughly an hour given sk4 is in flight — but the hour must produce a **correct** list rather than a plausible one, which is the whole reason this row carries the method and not just the task. | OPEN |
| GAP-034 | `fuel-ir` `BackendCapabilities`; `fuel-core` `decode_shape` validity keys; and any sibling "what is permissible" type | — | **GENERALIZATION of GAP-031 + the GAP-028 residency finding: Fuel has types whose job is to describe what is PERMISSIBLE, and the facts people actually need to state keep turning out not to be in their vocabulary.** Two instances found the same day, independently: (1) `BackendCapabilities` cannot express an **extent** constraint (`m>1`, `k>=64`) → GAP-031, whose downstream consequence is GAP-007's hard realize-time failure; (2) the decode validity key could not express **residency** — shape was already implied by existing fields, but *device* was not expressible at all → the GAP-028 design result. **Why it is one pattern:** these types are **enumerations of dimensions somebody chose up front**, and each of these bugs is a dimension that was not chosen. In both cases the workaround had the same shape — a comment, or a construction-time guarantee, standing in for a mechanism — and **in both cases the failure is silent, because a vocabulary gap cannot produce a type error.** **The question worth asking of any such type: what facts about validity exist that this type cannot express?** **⚠️ Stated limit, from the finder: two instances, both in code they were already reading for other reasons — that is a suggestive sample, NOT a survey.** A third lane examining a type nobody involved has touched is what would establish whether this is general or merely true of the two things that happened to be open. **THIRD INSTANCE FOUND 2026-08-07, and it partly discharges that limit — see GAP-048.** The type is Fuel's **op vocabulary** (`OpKind` / `Op`), which nobody in this row's lineage had touched, and it was reached by a **different route**: auditing Fuel against KISS-Ops section 6.15 rather than reading Fuel code. Same shape as the first two — an enumeration of dimensions chosen up front, where the facts callers need to state (truncated-remainder semantics; NaN-suppressing max/min) are **not in the vocabulary**. **The route matters as much as the instance:** the first two were found by someone already inside the code, which is exactly the sampling bias this limit warned about; an *external* standard supplied the discriminating question for free, because it enumerates distinctions somebody else paid to learn. **Honest qualification — the failure mode DIFFERS from the first two and the row should not flatten that.** There the gap was **silent**. Here `RemElementwise`'s doc comment explicitly names the C99/Rust divergence, so the gap is **documented but still inexpressible** — the reader is warned and still has no way to ask for the other behavior. That is a *milder* variant, and counting it as identical would overstate the pattern. **FOURTH INSTANCE 2026-08-08, and it is the strongest yet because the missing dimension makes a model UNDECODABLE rather than merely mis-described: `DecodeModel` cannot express HETEROGENEOUS PER-LAYER STATE** (GAP-098). Its geometry methods describe one uniform KV shape replicated across all layers; LFM2 needs a conv ring buffer for some layers, so the allocated state is not just oversized but **wrong**. **Fourth route as well** — found while *scoping a port*, not by reading code (instances 1-2), not by auditing against an external standard (instance 3). **The tally now: four instances, three distinct discovery routes, and in every case the type was an enumeration of dimensions somebody chose up front with the needed dimension not among them.** **Verdict: three instances, two routes, one of them adversarial — no longer "merely true of what happened to be open", but the survey this row asks for still does not exist.** | OPEN |
| GAP-035 | `docs/specs/kernel-contract-format.md` - new Semantics section | A | **FKC carries no op SEMANTICS; it names an op and stops.** KISS-Contract section 6.4 makes semantics a **mandatory, resolvable op DAG** down to a primitive floor. Fuel's contract carries `op_kind`/`fused_op` and the meaning lives in Fuel's own registry - sufficient inside Fuel, **insufficient across a vendor boundary, and Fuel has already paid for it**: baracuda's `rope_apply` is INTERLEAVED, Fuel's `FusedOps::ROPE` is ROTATE-HALF, same name, different function, caught only by a numeric oracle after a fused registration had to be reverted. A semantics DAG makes that a **contract-read-time** mismatch. Fuel already has the material (`decompose`/`pattern` recipes, base map, build-time-closed primitive basis, `base_map_hash` recipe identity) - it is simply not in the contract. **Ranked #1 of the convergence work: it unblocks real Baracuda/Unpopped/Vulkane interop.** See [fkc-kiss-convergence.md](fkc-kiss-convergence.md) section 3.1. | OPEN |
| GAP-036 | FKC spec + `fuel-dispatch/src/fkc/` | A | **Adopt KISS's clause-ID discipline: atomic numbered clauses, 1:1 mapped to conformance tests, build FAILS on an untested MUST.** This is structurally the countermeasure to Fuel's most expensive recurring defect - **existence != enforcement**. Evidence it is needed, all from this codebase: the FKC audit found ~24 gaps, mostly correctly-named validators nobody invoked; GAP-016 records ~612 `panic!` sites against a hard never-panic rule that had **never been counted**; GAP-141's scoping found the registry's own founding case sits in a class its first increment does not check. **Fuel enforces by vigilance; KISS enforces by construction.** **Highest-return item in the convergence set, and its return is INTERNAL** - it pays for itself even if no cross-project interop ever happens. Pairs with GAP-141. **SHARPENED 2026-08-07 by reading KISS-Conform section 6.1/6.2 FIRSTHAND (previously second-hand from the umbrella) - and it is materially stronger than "clause IDs mapped to tests".** Four properties Fuel does not have: **(1) BIDIRECTIONAL totality, both gated** - clause->test (every normative clause resolves to >=1 test citing it; an untested MUST is a build-fail, `6.1-0002`) AND test->clause (every test cites >=1 *live* clause; an orphan test is a build-fail, `6.1-0003`). **(2) The matrix MUST be DERIVED, NOT HAND-AUTHORED (`6.1-0004`)** - a lint parses the machine-readable sidecar for the authoritative clause set and cross-references `#[cite(...)]`-style annotations on the tests, and an implementation **MUST NOT accept a hand-maintained matrix as authoritative**, explicitly because a hand-maintained one can silently diverge. This is the same derived-not-authored principle as `audited_status` (GAP-037), applied to coverage itself. **(3) The gate is COMPILE/GENERATE-time, not run time (`6.2-0005`)** - a suite that does not fully cover its declared normative surface **MUST NOT be producible**, so it cannot be run or claimed; deferring the structural checks to run time is explicitly forbidden. Note **Fuel's GAP-141 gates are run-time `cargo test` checks of a structural property** - they work, but they are the weaker shape. **(4) Structural gate and conformance VERDICT are separated and MUST NOT be conflated (`6.2-0006`)** - build-time asks "does the mapping exist", run-time asks "do the mapped tests pass". **Worked example of why this matters, from this very session:** GAP-141 inc-2 shipped a gate enforcing 14 patterns while registry rule 1 still named 5, and the drift was closed by a **hand-coordinated companion commit** with a minutes-long window where the two disagreed. KISS-Conform `6.2-0004` makes exactly that a **build failure** - drift is detected by deterministic clause-ID-token match (never a semantic prose parse): every clause-ID token in prose must appear in the sidecar and every sidecar clause must appear in prose. **Fuel solved that instance with coordination; KISS solves the class with a gate.** That is the whole argument for this row in one example. See [fkc-kiss-convergence.md](fkc-kiss-convergence.md) section 3.2. **INCREMENT 1 SHIPPED 2026-08-08 @ `661333f9`** (`fuel-dispatch/tests/fkc_clause_coverage.rs`). **Clause anchor is the `FkcError` variant set, NOT numbered spec prose — a deliberate profile deviation.** Reason: the FKC spec's normative statements are **not atomic** (its own numbered validator rules bundle several independent checks per paragraph — rule 5 carries four), so prose clause IDs are real work, deferred to increment 2. The variant set is the better anchor for *this* defect class: it is **derived from the code that enforces**, so it cannot drift from enforcement the way a prose list can, and it is already atomic + machine-readable. **Stated tradeoff:** MUSTs that bind something other than the validator (e.g. "the planner MUST insert `Op::Contiguize`", §4.3) produce no `FkcError` and are INVISIBLE to this gate — its green says nothing about them. **3 of the 4 properties adopted:** (1) bidirectional totality — clause→test AND citation→clause (an exemption naming a deleted variant is a hard failure, because a stale one silently pre-authorises a future variant of the same name to skip coverage); (2) DERIVED not hand-authored — the clause set is parsed out of `error.rs`, so there is no matrix to maintain and none to drift; (4) structural gate kept separate from conformance verdict. **(3) generate-time gating DECLINED, with reasoning** (per the ruling that a reasoned deviation beats a grudging adoption): it needs a `build.rs` in a crate that has none, and would re-run the scan on every consumer build of fuel-dispatch. Revisit if a second such gate appears — one build.rs amortises across several. **WHAT IT FOUND IMMEDIATELY — 5 of 47 clauses had no test, in THREE different situations a pass/fail gate would have flattened:** (a) **`PassthroughNamesOptionalOperand`** — constructed on a live path (`lower.rs:766`) with nothing proving that path reachable; **CLOSED** by a new provoking test that carries its own positive control (same contract passing through the REQUIRED operand must lower cleanly), so it asserts *this rule fired* rather than *an error happened*; (b) **`UnauditedPrecision`** — appears NOWHERE outside `error.rs`: a declared error no code can emit (the precision half of V-FKC-9, a known wiring decision); (c) **`BlurbMismatch`** reserved by design, **`Io`/`Yaml`** infrastructure rather than contract rules. The four remaining are EXEMPT with written reasons; a placeholder reason is rejected. **ANTI-VACUITY is the part that earns the green, and it is sabotage-verified in BOTH directions.** A coverage scanner that goes blind reports FULL COVERAGE — indistinguishable from success. The first version of this scanner did exactly that (disarmed its `#[cfg(test)]` tracker on the arming line, before the opening brace was counted) and reported **0 of 47 covered** when a hand count found 41 citations in one file; a positive control caught it, reading the code did not. The gate therefore controls itself against *blind* (`MUST_DETECT_AS_COVERED`) AND against *over-inclusive* (`exemptions_are_still_necessary_…`). The second was added after noticing the first set **could not** catch it, and sabotage proves the point: forcing the scanner to treat production code as test code makes the new control FAIL with `["Io","Yaml"]` while `every_clause_is_cited_by_a_test` still **PASSES** — the coverage-direction assert is provably blind to over-inclusion. That control doubles as a **staleness ratchet**: an exemption that stops being necessary fails the build instead of accumulating. Gate: `--lib` 734/0, `--test fkc_clause_coverage` 4/0, `--all-targets` exit 0; the gate went red→green across the change (1 uncovered → 0), so it is born-red on a real gap rather than shipped already-satisfied. **ARCHITECT RULING on the clause-anchor deviation (asked for explicitly; answered rather than waved through): the deviation is CORRECT for increment 1 — and for a reason slightly different from the one offered.** The builder argued the `FkcError` variant set is the better anchor because it is *derived from the enforcing code* and therefore cannot drift from enforcement. True, and it is also **more conformant with KISS-Conform §6.1-0004 than a prose list would be** — that clause requires the matrix be **derived, not hand-authored**, and a hand-maintained prose-clause list is precisely what it forbids. So this is not a cheaper substitute for the standard; on that clause it is a closer match. **But the two anchors answer DIFFERENT QUESTIONS and neither subsumes the other, so increment 2 is NOT optional.** Error-variant anchor: *"is every ENFORCED rule tested?"* — and it uniquely catches the **validator that exists but is never invoked**, which is Fuel's documented recurring defect (*existence != enforcement*, the FKC audit's central finding). Prose-clause anchor: *"is every NORMATIVE CLAIM tested?"* — and it uniquely catches a **MUST that binds something other than the validator**, e.g. §4.3's *"the planner MUST insert `Op::Contiguize`"*, which emits no `FkcError` at all. **The class invisible to increment 1 is the MOST dangerous one: a MUST that nothing enforces AND nothing tests.** It is invisible to the gate, and — worse — invisible to a reader who takes the gate's green as coverage. KISS-Conform §6.2-0004's drift detection is specifically prose-clause-ID-to-sidecar, so **full §6.1/§6.2 parity requires both anchors**; increment 1 is half the shape, and the honest half. **DO NOT let increment 1's green be read as GAP-036 done.** The builder flagged this risk themselves and the row states the scope so a later reader cannot over-read it — which is the same discipline as GAP-028's vacuous test whose *name* promised coverage it never had. **Deferral of (3) generate-time gating ACCEPTED with its reasoning:** it needs a `build.rs` in a crate that has none, and would re-run the scan on every consumer build of `fuel-dispatch`. Revisit if a **second** such gate appears — one `build.rs` amortises across several, one does not. A reasoned decline beats a grudging adoption (GAP-046 precedent). **THE FINDING WORTH MORE THAN THE GATE — and it is `ep0587yw`'s lesson applied by a different lane, on a different artifact, the same night.** The first scanner **reported 0 of 47 covered** while a hand count found 41 citations in one file: it had disarmed its `#[cfg(test)]` tracker on the arming line, before the module's opening brace was counted. **A coverage scanner that goes blind reports FULL COVERAGE — its failure mode is indistinguishable from success.** A positive control caught it; reading the code did not. **And the second control exists because the builder checked whether the FIRST set could catch it, and they could not** — sabotage-verified with recompilation confirmed: forcing the scanner to count production code as test code makes `exemptions_are_still_necessary_…` **FAIL** while `every_clause_is_cited_by_a_test` still **PASSES**, proving the coverage-direction assert is blind to over-inclusion. **That is a falsifiable claim about the instrument, not an assurance about it** — the standard this registry should hold gates to. **INC 2 SHIPPED @ `582eaedb`; the §10.10 DIVERGENCE it found is CLOSED @ `55bf0a2b` — the whole cycle took about half an hour.** **The amendment describes BOTH paths, which was the load-bearing instruction:** writing only the importer's `?`-propagation would have replaced one inaccuracy with a tidier one. The clause now states the two callers of `finalize()` are **deliberately different and MUST NOT be collapsed** — **import MUST propagate** (a provider contract is *untrusted input*; a duplicate is a rejectable contract, never a crash), **static-table init MAY fail fast** (a duplicate in a checked-in table is a programmer error in Fuel's own source, detectable only at init). **Collapsing is wrong in EITHER direction:** propagating from init invents a recovery path that cannot exist; expecting on import crashes on third-party input. **Superseded text kept INLINE rather than rewritten** — the drift *is* the defect, and a later reader needs to see the clause still read "the actual de-panic lands with the importer implementation" **after the importer had landed**. `FKC-10.10-0001` is no longer a divergence; it describes what the code does and moved into the normal clause->test path, cited by the two tests proving each half. **THE `DIVERGENCES` TABLE STAYS IN PLACE AND EMPTY, WITH A COMMENT SAYING WHY — the epistemically interesting part.** An empty table asserts **"checked, none outstanding"**, a *different claim* from **"we do not track that"**, and both are indistinguishable from a **missing** table. **Complement of GAP-149:** there an emptiness result had to be distinguished from a broken instrument; here an empty result is engineered to **assert** something. **Recorded because someone will later propose deleting an empty table:** increment 2 shipped, found a real divergence, and closed it inside one cycle. **The prose anchor justified itself faster than either the architect or the builder predicted** — the category going empty is evidence the mechanism works, **not** evidence it was a one-off. **⚠️ SCOPE NOTE ON THE INC-1 GATE, found 2026-08-08 by the successor of the person who built it — state this wherever "the coverage gate is green" is cited.** `FkcError::UnauditedPrecision` (`error.rs:367`) is **never constructed**, *and* `tests/fkc_clause_coverage.rs:74` asserts **the variant exists**. So **the gate certifies that a clause has a CITATION, not that the clause has an EFFECT** — and a dead variant therefore reads as covered. **That is *existence != enforcement* applied to the enforcement mechanism itself.** It does not invalidate the gate, which does exactly what it was designed to do; it pins the gate's boundary, which is narrower than "the coverage gate is green" sounds. **INCREMENT 2 SCOPED 2026-08-08 — and the architect's premise INVERTED.** The architect worried the spec's **non-atomic** rules (rule 5 carrying four independent checks) were the real work. They are the real work *of numbering* — but they are **validator** rules, which increment 1 already anchors via `FkcError` variants. **Re-numbering them would DOUBLE-ANCHOR a surface that already has one anchor**; one surface, one anchor. Increment 2 must leave validator prose alone with a pointer to the variant-anchored gate. **The genuine surface is the MUSTs binding something OTHER than the validator — ~6-8 claims, hand-classified (a keyword heuristic over-flagged and was not trusted):** §4.3 (planner), §4.8 (corpus; enforced + tested), §12.5 (graph registry; separately gated), §10.10 (a Rust API shape; **DIVERGED**), remainder already covered. A **numbering pass over a small surface**, not a design pass. **NON-FINDING, RECORDED SO NOBODY RE-DERIVES THE SUSPICION: §4.3's planner MUST is ENFORCED.** `awkward_layout_strategy` appears only in the FKC validator and never in the planner, which reads as a declared-but-unconsumed field. It is consumed one hop away: `caps_map` projects the per-operand layout tri-states onto `KernelCaps.strided_input`, and **validator rule 5 enforces coherence between the strategy and those flags** — `requires_contiguous` => `contiguous: required` => `strided_input:false` => planner inserts; `contiguize_internally` => `strided: accepted` => it does not. The tri-state reaches the planner **through** the flags it is validated against. **§10.10 IS A REAL DIVERGENCE, and it upgrades increment 2 from hypothetical to EVIDENCED.** The spec says `register_full_with_source` **"MUST become `Result`"**, calls it a **"firm prerequisite of the FKC import path - not merely a future cleanup"**, marks it RESOLVED 2026-06-17, and says the de-panic "lands with the importer implementation." **The importer landed; both `register_full_with_source` and `_generic` still return `()`.** **Correctly NOT called a never-panic violation by its finder:** registration is append-only and infallible by design and the duplicate-`KernelRef` case surfaces at `finalize()`, which returns `Result`. **The never-panic goal was met — by a different route than the spec mandates.** So it is **doc-vs-code drift**, which CLAUDE.md already calls a defect, and **nothing detects it**. **Note the CLASS: not an unimplemented MUST, but a MUST the implementation satisfied a DIFFERENT WAY without updating the spec.** Neither anchor was designed to catch that and only the prose side can. Invisible to increment 1 exactly as predicted, so the prediction is now evidence rather than argument. **RULED (architect): AMEND THE SPEC, record as the first divergence entry — do NOT change the signature.** Forcing `Result` on an operation that cannot fail makes every caller handle an impossible error, and **a `Result` that is always `Ok` is a false signal that trains people to stop reading Results.** Same reasoning as GAP-049 from the other side: there a wildcard could not do anything correct so the compile break was right; here a `Result` arm cannot carry anything real so it is noise. **⚠️ ONE VERIFICATION COULD FLIP THIS RULING, and the builder was asked to check it rather than take the architect's reading: GAP-015 records that `finalize()` had an `.expect()`.** If the duplicate case panics on that path rather than returning, then registration is **not** safely infallible, the never-panic goal was **not** met by another route, and the ruling flips to "change the signature". **HARD REQUIREMENT on the build, raised by the builder against their OWN proposal:** clause IDs on prose reintroduce exactly what KISS-Conform §6.1-0004 forbids — a hand-authored list that can silently diverge — **unless the sidecar is GENERATED by parsing the spec**, never written alongside it, with the gate failing on prose-vs-sidecar mismatch by deterministic token match. **If that generation proves infeasible, STOP rather than ship a hand-authored sidecar: one that LOOKS derived is worse than none, because it carries the mechanism's authority without its property.** The only genuine design work in the increment. **INCREMENT 2 SHIPPED 2026-08-08 @ `582eaedb`** (`fuel-dispatch/tests/fkc_prose_clause_coverage.rs`). **RESOLVED, the hard requirement — there is NO SIDECAR FILE AT ALL**, which is stronger than generating one: the clause set is obtained by **parsing the spec at test time**, so there is no second copy that could drift and nothing to regenerate. Delete a clause from the prose and its stale citations surface immediately. **The scoping premise INVERTED.** The non-atomic validator rules (rule 5 bundling four checks) were expected to be the work; they are exactly the rules to **leave alone**, being already anchored on `FkcError` and gated by inc 1. **One surface, one anchor** — numbering them twice is how a coverage matrix starts disagreeing with itself. Five genuinely non-validator clauses numbered: `FKC-4.3-0001/0002` (bind the PLANNER), `FKC-4.8-0001` (binds the CORPUS), `FKC-12.5-0001` (binds the GRAPH REGISTRY — enforced in fuel-graph/fuel-core, recorded WITH its location so "not cited here" never reads as "unenforced"), `FKC-10.10-0001` (a DIVERGENCE). **FIRST DIVERGENCE ENTRY — `FKC-10.10-0001`.** Spec says `register_full_with_source` MUST become `Result`; code returns `()`. **Ruled: amend the SPEC.** **VERIFIED against the code rather than inherited from the ruling, exactly as the ruling asked:** `finalize()` returns `Result<()>` and the FKC importer maps it to `FkcError::DuplicateKernelRef` and propagates with `?` ("never-panic on the import path"), so registration IS safely infallible there and **the ruling does NOT flip**. **Scope limit stated because the claim is narrower than it reads:** never-panic holds for the IMPORT path; the hand-written static-table init path still `.expect()`s on `finalize()` at two sites — a deliberate init-boundary fail-fast on a programmer error in checked-in tables. **The defect is the DRIFT, not the signature.** This is the class that justifies inc 2 and it is now **evidenced rather than predicted**: not an unimplemented MUST, but a MUST the implementation satisfied a DIFFERENT WAY without updating the spec — which neither anchor was designed to catch, and only the prose side can. **NON-FINDING recorded so nobody re-derives the suspicion:** §4.3's `awkward_layout_strategy` appears only in the validator, never in the planner — it is consumed *one hop away* (`caps_map` projects the layout tri-states to `KernelCaps.strided_input`; validator rule 5 enforces strategy/flag coherence), so the strategy reaches the planner THROUGH the flags it is validated against. Not a gap. Gate: prose 3/0, inc-1 4/0 (unchanged), `--lib` 734/0. **Sabotage-verified, recompilation confirmed:** deleting a clause ID from the spec fails `no_orphan_citations` on the orphaned citation AND trips the minimum-clause control — proving the parse is LIVE, not cached. | **BOTH INCREMENTS SHIPPED** |
| GAP-037 | FKC section 4.8 precision `audited`; FKC `provenance` field | A | **Two small changes, each closing a proven failure.** (a) **Make `audited_status` DERIVED, not authored** - KISS-Contract 6.8-0008/-0009/-0010 derives it with a single home; Fuel shipped **85 CUDA kernels carrying `audited: false`**, never verified, unnoticed for months. A derived status cannot be fabricated by an author in a hurry. (b) **Rename FKC `provenance` -> `cost_provenance`** - KISS uses `provenance` for *who produced the kernel* (a supply-chain claim) and `cost_provenance` for the cost origin. Fuel uses the bare word for the cost meaning. **One word, two concepts, in documents intended to converge** is a guaranteed misread; KISS already spells it the unambiguous way (6.8-0006). Note both projects independently arrived at `declared`/`measured` - Fuel spells it `judge_measured`; reconcile the spelling too. **SCOPING NOTE from GAP-036's builder (2026-08-08), and it likely changes how this is sized: `audited_status`-must-be-derived is the SAME SHAPE as the coverage gate shipped at `661333f9` — derive from the enforcing code, never hand-author.** The two may share a **mechanism** rather than being two separate jobs, and KISS-Conform §6.1-0004 is the same principle applied to coverage. **SCOPED 2026-08-08, and the answer INVERTS the guess above: the PRINCIPLE generalises perfectly, the MECHANISM does not, and (a) is much BIGGER than this row reads, not smaller.** The GAP-036 gate derives from `error.rs` — an artifact that is **always present and always complete**, because the enum *is* the clause set. `audited_status` must derive from a **verification ledger covering 2.6% of the corpus**. Same principle ("derive from the enforcing artifact, never hand-author"); one has its artifact and the other does not. **A shared principle does not amortise implementation the way a shared mechanism would** — that distinction is the reusable part. **(a) IS BLOCKED ON EVIDENCE, NOT MACHINERY, and the evidence gap is itself a correctness finding — carved out to GAP-058.** **(b) RULED: HOLD, and do NOT start the 1237-line corpus edit.** Reasons, in order. **(i)** The internal cost of today's name is **ambiguity, not wrongness** — no correctness risk, no user-visible effect. That is the cheapest kind of debt to carry and the worst kind to pay twice. **(ii)** [KISS#125](https://github.com/ThinkersJournal/KISS/issues/125) is a *proposal* touching **pinned** §6.7 text with freeze close; "not now" is an explicitly legitimate outcome, and the architect additionally offered KISS an *optional sibling field* alternative that could carry a different spelling. Renaming on a guess about a proposal's outcome is exactly the shape of error this registry keeps recording. **(iii)** A 1237-line mechanical edit is a poor use of a session while **GAP-036 increment 2 is open and ruled NON-optional** and is worth strictly more. **WHEN it goes, take option (3): key AND values together** (`provenance`->`cost_provenance` *and* `judge_measured`->whatever spelling is settled). Both edits touch the same 1237 authored lines; doing them separately pays twice for one traversal. **RECORDED RISK, raised and then DISPROVED by the same person, which is the standard:** `revhash.rs:118` pushes the literal `"cost.provenance"` into the canonical block feeding `kernel_revision_hash` (§4.7, the cache-invalidation primitive), so a rename that also caught that literal would change **every** kernel revision hash — invalidating all 749 ledger entries and any artifact keyed on the hash — **while looking like a tidy find-and-replace**. It is already pinned: `revision_hash_frozen_value_regression` builds its fixture from the **real** `add_f32` contract (which carries `provenance: judge_measured`), so the push genuinely executes and the frozen value covers that key; a careless rename fails loudly. **The credit belongs to whoever wrote that test against a real contract instead of a synthetic stub** — a hand-rolled fixture with no cost block would have left the key unpinned and the trap live. **GAP-038 stays INDEPENDENT** — it shares no artifact with either half here, and per the same principle-vs-mechanism distinction, principles do not amortise implementation. | OPEN |
| GAP-038 | FKC section 11 versioning | A | **Adopt KISS's TWO version axes.** KISS mandates **wire/ABI schema version** and **published-crate semver** as independent axes with a per-change bump-rule table (umbrella 5.1). FKC has a single `fkc_version`, conflating "the bytes changed" with "the code changed" - so a pure refactor and a layout change are indistinguishable to a consumer. Cheap, mechanical, prevents a class of skew. | OPEN |
| GAP-039 | KISS RFC (external) - **GO-AHEAD GIVEN by CireSnave 2026-08-08; FIRST RFC OPENED: [ThinkersJournal/KISS#124](https://github.com/ThinkersJournal/KISS/issues/124)** | A | **RFC 1 of 4 OPENED 2026-08-08 — the verify-before-adopt ledger, KISS#124.** Chosen first because it is the only candidate that is **already in KISS's own required-work list**: umbrella §10.5 item 6 names it a *"normative clause that does not yet exist"*, names Fuel's ledger-with-downgrade-to-UNAUDITED as the pattern, and calls it *"the only thing in this list that catches a WRONG kernel rather than a MALFORMED one"*. So the RFC supplies a proposal KISS was already waiting for rather than asking them to want something new. Framed with no wire change, no new oracle, no Fuel vocabulary imposed (the demoted-state token is the editor's), and **Fuel's one-implementation limit stated up front** rather than left to surface at the freeze gate. **THE OTHER THREE ARE DELIBERATELY HELD, and two of the reasons are corrections to this row's own text.** **(a) The five-flag layout candidate is NOT a gap — this row said "KISS has no equivalent granularity" and that is WRONG.** A firsthand read of KISS-Contract §6.7-0001 shows the Capabilities section already carries `awkward_layout_strategy` **and** `in_place_eligible_variants`. The real claim is **coarser-vs-finer** (one strategy field vs five orthogonal flags), which is a much weaker and more honest ask. **An RFC asserting an absent capability would have been refuted by the spec's own text on first read** — the second time tonight a firsthand read overturned a second-hand claim about KISS. **(b) Precision-as-a-hard-pre-filter-before-cost has NOT been checked against the Dispatch sub-standard** and will not be proposed until it is. **(c) Cost-as-a-vector + the provenance flip is verified and ready** (§6.7-0006 pins cost as class + expressions over launch-scalar symbols, and is the single authoritative home for magnitude; Fuel's vector + provenance-flip is a genuine delta) — **held only for RATE, not readiness.** **RATE: the architect raised a constraint against his own RFC, and the KISS steward DISSOLVED it and corrected the premise (2026-08-08).** The concern was that adding normative surface to a spec whose coverage had stalled makes the gap worse. **Ruling: gate the LANDING, not the DISCUSSION.** Opening and discussing an RFC costs ~zero coverage — it is design, not ratified surface. **What grew the untested count is clauses landing WITHOUT tests**, and umbrella §10.5's own header already mandates the fix: each item lands as a clause **plus** a mapped test **plus** removal from `conformance/UNBACKED.tsv`, atomically. Because #124 was scoped to reuse the existing oracle + ULP machinery, **its test is writable against the current harness, so it can land already-backed and add ZERO to the untested count.** The tension was real but self-inflicted by scoping it as a queueing question instead of a landing-discipline one. **PREMISE CORRECTION — the architect's 702 figure was STALE.** Burndown has moved **702 -> 567**, and the structural-blocker framing is also out of date: SYNTH and EMIT are no longer 0-backed (SYNTH 99 untested, EMIT 35), and **OPS (109) is now the single largest untested block.** True at the restart, partially cleared since. Cited from KISS#91 without re-reading it — exactly the *audit-age-is-decay-not-warranty* failure this registry records elsewhere. **BACKED vs FROZEN — a distinction Fuel did not have and should use.** The architect stated Fuel's one-implementation limit up front on #124; the steward's refinement is that a profile's conformance test can be written against the **neutral oracle**, independent of Fuel's ledger, so the suite can **BACK** a clause on one implementation's machinery. **FREEZING** it as settled surface still wants a second dissimilar implementer. So the honest target is **land backed-but-explicitly-unfrozen** — the state most of the suite is already in. (Steward's own caveat, worth keeping: KISS's sibling implementations share one comprehension lineage, so the >=2-codebases bar already overstates independence.) **TRIAGE OF THE REMAINING THREE, from the steward, and it re-ordered the architect's plan:** **(1) cost-as-a-vector + provenance flip -> OPENED as [KISS#125](https://github.com/ThinkersJournal/KISS/issues/125).** *"The one real RFC of the three."* **It CONFLICTS with PINNED text** — KISS-CONTRACT-6.7-0006 pins cost as class + expression and the single authoritative home for magnitude — so it is **not additive surface** and wants the maintainer's eyes. Filed with the conflict leading, per instruction, and with the strongest objection against Fuel's own position stated in the RFC (**a vector is harder to PRODUCE than a scalar**; if providers leave every axis but `compute` absent it buys little and costs schema). Evidence offered is Fuel's measured paged-attention round-trip: **compute ~10.4x vs host-ward bytes ~186x** on the same kernel pair — axes diverging by more than an order of magnitude, which no single scalar can express without silently baking a weighting that belongs to the consumer. Limit stated in the RFC: one workload, one target; it shows the axes CAN diverge, not how often. **(2) finer layout granularity -> NOT a standalone issue.** Folded into the steward's Fuel<->KISS vocabulary-alignment program (their task #30), same species as the dtype/MX granularity items. Lowest urgency. Consistent with the architect having already self-corrected this from "gap" to "coarser-vs-finer". **(3) precision-as-hard-pre-filter -> HELD, and the hold was endorsed.** Still unverified against the Dispatch sub-standard, and it likely intersects the alignment program's precision item (same-hw/any-hw ExactByte split + rel/abs tolerance) — verify there first rather than filing. **Upstream the parts where FUEL is better, as KISS extensions on the promotion path.** Candidates, each with a reason KISS lacks it: **five-flag layout capability set** (KISS has no equivalent granularity; Fuel's exists so a planner can price contiguize-vs-strided-vs-materialize honestly); **cost as a vector + the provenance-FLIP** (KISS has cost class + expression; Fuel's vector contribution and its Judge bootstrap that *flips* provenance on refinement are genuinely novel); **precision as a hard pre-filter applied BEFORE cost ranking** (an ordering commitment KISS does not make, and the correct one); **awkward-layout fixups are themselves contracts** (so a contract can never hide a decision behind "handled internally"); **the verify-before-adopt ledger** - KISS 10.5 item 6 **explicitly invites this**, naming Fuel's ledger-with-downgrade-to-UNAUDITED as the pattern that should be a named optional profile rather than folklore. **Sequenced LAST deliberately:** KISS's promotion path requires >=2 dissimilar implementations plus a conformance test, so Fuel should arrive with the extensions implemented and its own house in order. **Also carries the GAP-032 artifact-validity clause** - routed through Fuel to KISS, citing Unpopped `188d9c9` as **prior art**, not as a co-proposer (their explicit instruction). | OPEN |
| GAP-046 | fuel-core `rebind_and_realize_paged_prebuilt` (the PAGED half of GAP-028) | — | **DECLINED, deliberately and with reason — carved out of GAP-028 so the omission reads as a DECISION rather than an oversight. Proposed by `ep0587yw` (GAP-028's owner), ruled by the architect 2026-08-08.** **The argument, and it is the right one:** in paged serving **one pool is shared across many sequences** — that is the entire point of paging. Per-request variation rides the `block_table`, which **already re-binds per token**. A *pool* swap therefore means the server replaced its whole KV arena: a **lifecycle event, not an admission-path event.** So the paged guard buys ~nothing on the path GAP-028 exists to accelerate, while adding a **second load-bearing guard whose failure mode is silent cross-request contamination** — the exact hazard GAP-014 was filed for. **Why the architect agrees rather than merely accepting:** GAP-028's own re-classification says it **trades a proof-free guarantee for a proof obligation**, and that trade is only worth making where there is a win to pay for it. Here there is no win, so it would be **pure obligation** — taking on a correctness proof to buy nothing. Declining is the consistent application of GAP-028's own reasoning, not an exception to it. **Re-open if** paged serving ever gains a path where the pool is swapped on the admission path rather than at lifecycle boundaries; that would supply the missing win and change the trade. | **DECLINED** — with reason, not omitted |
| GAP-047 | `fuel-graph/src/registry/qmatmul.rs:59` + `fuel-graph/src/lib.rs:9588` (`_ => panic!`) | B | **Op-id <-> `FusedOpParams` pairing is safe only by registry-construction CONVENTION, not by type.** Both sites carry a `_ => panic!` arm on a mismatched (op-id, params) combination. Nothing at the type level prevents the mismatch: **a new `FusedOpParams` variant, or a caller pairing an existing one with the wrong op id, would compile** and reach the panic. Found by the GAP-016 census; held as **bucket 3 on the conservative reading**, which is the correct one — the discriminating question is not "is it reachable today?" but *"what would have to change for it to become reachable, and would anything stop that change?"*, and here **nothing would**. Tier B rather than A because reaching it requires a future code change (contrast GAP-018, reachable now), and because the fix is architectural rather than local. **Fix:** type-pin the id<->params pairing (so an invalid pair is unrepresentable), or a build-time exhaustiveness check that fails when a new variant is added without its pairing. Either makes the `_` arm dead by construction, which is the standard the constitution asks for. | OPEN |
| GAP-030 | `fuel-inference` session lifecycle; mirrors Lightbulb `EngineModel::step_batch` | C | **"Absent ⇒ dead" vs "absent ⇒ not scheduled this round" is an ambiguity, not a fact — concept-level row, two independent citations.** A scheduler that steps a *subset* of live requests makes session-absence-from-a-batch indistinguishable from session-death, so a sweep keyed on absence silently frees a still-live KV cache: no error, full speed, wrong KV. Same failure family as GAP-014 (a validity key that cannot see the state it depends on), reached from the opposite direction — GAP-014 was a held plan blind to a cache *swap*; this is a sweep blind to a *deferral*. Filed after Lightbulb reported their `step_batch` doc already carries this warning independently (2026-08-07): two projects walked into the same ambiguity from opposite sides, which is the signal that it is structural rather than incidental. **Fuel's obligation under the §15 consumer seam is mechanism, not policy** — the scheduler's stepping policy is the consumer's, but Fuel must not offer a sweep whose only liveness signal is absence. Determine whether Fuel's own session sweep has this shape before Lightbulb builds real batching (they currently run `batch = vec![ctx]`, one request per batch, so neither side is exposed *today*). | OPEN |

| GAP-045 | fuel-ir/src/dtype.rs (F8E6M2); was GAP-002 Task 3 | A | **F8E6M2 has no bit-level encoding, and there is nothing to cite for one — DEFERRED by decision, not blocked (architect, 2026-08-07).** F8E6M2 ships **token-only** (`is_float`, 1 byte); real decode needs bias, reserved codes and subnormal semantics pinned. The sk4 RFC pins only its *shape* — unsigned, `6 exp + 2 mantissa`, `6+2=8` closes the scale self-check, scale category — and §6 carries kiss-ref's anti-invention rule verbatim: *"MX float semantics MUST be pinned by citing OCP-MX, not by analogy… No new numeric semantics are invented in this RFC."* **The trap:** for `f8e8m0` an external spec exists to cite; for `f8e6m2` there is none. The RFC's own language marks the difference — OCP-derived formats carry a citation, `f8e6m2` is "maintainer-ruled 2026-08-06" — so it is a Fuel-local invention and the encoding cannot be *resolved* by pinning, only **authored**. Fuel's code agrees: `dtype.rs:335-338` calls it "a non-OCP-standard token with no safetensors representation". Deferred because authoring a novel float encoding with **no consumer** is the wrong trade (CLAUDE.md: "no consumer" doesn't justify skipping a capability, but does justify sequencing it behind ones that have consumers). Nothing regresses — token-only is the shipped state. **Un-defer when:** a consumer needs real F8E6M2 arithmetic. At that point the bit-spec is a **maintainer (Eric) decision**, not a worker's, and the lane-A worker was right to refuse to let one be fabricated. GAP-002 completes on T1/T2/T4/T5/T6 without it. |

Fuel CONFORMS on 2 of the 4 load-bearing distinctions and is missing the SECOND HALF of the other 2.** KISS-Ops section 6.15 pins four pairs that an implementation MUST NOT merge, alias or substitute. **PASS - `6.15-0002` (relu NaN-propagating + `-0.0`-preserving, MUST NOT be `max(x,0)`):** Fuel conforms *and pins it*, `relu_f32_propagates_nan` + `relu_f32_preserves_negative_zero` in `fuel-cpu-backend/src/byte_kernels.rs`. **PASS - `6.15-0004` (`gelu` erf-based vs `gelu_tanh` distinct ops):** Fuel has `Op::Gelu` (tanh) and `Op::GeluErf` (exact) as separate variants with an explicit `assert_ne!` pin at `fuel-graph/src/jit.rs:382`. **GAP - `6.15-0003` (`rem_floor` vs `rem_trunc`):** Fuel has **one** `RemElementwise`, floored / PyTorch convention (sign of the *divisor*). Its doc comment honestly flags the divergence from C99 `fmod` and Rust `%` (both truncated, sign of the *dividend*) - **but there is no op a caller can ask for to GET truncated semantics.** **GAP - `6.15-0001` (`max_prop`/`min_prop` vs `fmax_ieee`/`fmin_ieee` - four distinct ops):** Fuel has **two**, both NaN-propagating (pinned deliberately for torch parity, 2026-07-08). No NaN-suppressing sibling exists. **IMPORTANT - this is NOT a live violation and must not be reported as one:** Fuel does not *merge* or *substitute* these, it simply cannot express one side. Nothing computes a wrong answer today. It becomes a **hard conformance blocker the moment Fuel declares KISS-Ops conformance**, which is on the GAP-035/039 path. **Latent landmine, verified and DOWNGRADED from my own first reading:** `fuel-core/src/mkl.rs:415-418` and `fuel-cpu-backend/src/mkl.rs` bind Fuel `max`/`min` to MKL **`vsFmax`/`vsFmin`** - the IEEE NaN-*suppressing* functions - and `accelerate.rs:473` does the same via `vDSP_vmax`. I initially wrote this up as "Maximum silently flips semantics under `--features mkl`". **That consequence is NOT supported and I withdrew it:** a positive-controlled search for callers of `vs_max`/`vd_max` outside the defining files returns **only the 4 `binary_op!` definition lines themselves** - the bindings are **dead code behind a feature flag**. The FFI binding is the fact; "the op flips" was a consequence I attached without its own evidence. Prior memory had already classified it correctly as a latent landmine; I was re-deriving an escalation the record had already declined. It matters only if someone wires the MKL path up - at which point it would be exactly the substitution `6.15-0001` forbids. Ties to **GAP-016(a)** (mkl wrapper, verification-blocked: DLLs absent on this box). **Fix:** add `RemTruncElementwise` and an `Fmax`/`Fmin` (IEEE-suppressing) pair, OR consciously declare them out of scope and record the profile deviation. Either is cheap; the point is that it be a **decision** rather than an absence. | OPEN |
| GAP-049 | `fuel-dispatch/src/pipelined.rs` `scalar_to_bytes`; `fuel-cuda-backend/src/storage.rs` `push_scalar_arg` | B | **`main` was RED on a DEFAULT build for an unknown window, and the guard shipped to catch exactly this class did not fire. FIXED @ `7b3abafd`; the open item is the RULING, not the code.** `feff38ed` (GAP-002 T2) added `Scalar::F8E8M0(u8)` to `fuel-ir` and left two exhaustive consumer matches un-updated. `fuel-ir` itself stayed green — **the entire break lived in consumers**, one on the default build (`fuel-dispatch`, a default member, so a root `cargo check` was red too) and one only under `--features cuda`. Found by `bzfevnl6` incidentally while building GAP-015; not found by whoever last ran a default build, because nobody had. **EVIDENCE, SHA-pinned so it is re-runnable:** `git show dccad436:fuel-dispatch/src/pipelined.rs \| grep -c F8E8M0` -> **0**; the same query at `7b3abafd` -> **1**. **BOTH arms verified by builds that ACTUALLY RAN, with positive artifacts rather than exit codes:** `pipelined.rs` went exit 101 -> exit 0 with rustc E0004 naming `fuel_ir::Scalar::F8E8M0(_)` before the fix; `storage.rs` was compiled by the `--features cuda` GAP-015 gate, artifacts `[vcvarsall.bat] Environment initialized for: 'x64'` + `Compiling fuel-cuda-backend`. **⚠️ The COMMIT MESSAGE ON `7b3abafd` UNDER-CLAIMS: it says the cuda arm was fixed blind.** That was true when written and was superseded minutes later by the cuda build. Recorded here because **the commit is the durable artifact** and a later reader will trust it over this row. An under-claim is the safe direction to be wrong, but it is still wrong. **THE REUSABLE SENTENCE, and it is the best thing to come out of this:** `DType::ALL` exhaustiveness shipped **in the very same commit**, added specifically to catch this class of break — and it missed, because it was aimed at the **DType** axis and the break was on the **Scalar-consumer** axis. **A guard only covers the axis it is aimed at.** **RULED 2026-08-07 by the architect; `d27pmruh` (GAP-002's IMPLEMENTER, not its design authority — they correctly declined to rule on registry scope, authority they do not hold) recommended and concurred.** They identified the property that predicts recurrence rather than the one that explains the instance — `Scalar` is not `#[non_exhaustive]`, so its extension model is undeclared. **The architect pushed back on the obvious remedy and the pushback is recorded here so it is not silently reversed:** marking `Scalar` `#[non_exhaustive]` would have made this WORSE. `scalar_to_bytes` returns `Vec<u8>`, not `Result`; its forced wildcard arm could only panic (violating never-panic) or emit the wrong encoding (silent corruption in a MaskedFill kernel). **The compile error was the GOOD outcome** — build-time validation doing what the constitution requires, stopping a wrong encoding from existing. Discriminating question: **can a wildcard arm do something correct?** For `Scalar` encoding, no. See GAP-033 for the carried-vs-interpreted split this forced. **THE RULING, three parts.** **(1)** GAP-033 does **not** absorb `Scalar`; it stays scoped to CARRIED types. **(2)** The compile break was the **correct outcome** — the defect is that nobody ran a default build between `feff38ed` and `bzfevnl6` stumbling on it, not that the build broke. **(3)** A per-enum extension model becomes an **explicit declaration** — *exhaustive-by-design* vs *extensible* — recorded at the definition site rather than defaulted either way. **Two concrete mechanisms, both from `d27pmruh` and both better than what the architect had:** a **marker doc-line at the enum definition** (e.g. `// EXHAUSTIVE-BY-DESIGN — variant addition is breaking; gate every consumer`) so the next variant-adder is warned **where they are standing** rather than having to remember a procedure; and the paired **gate rule — adding a variant to such an enum means workspace-scope, never `-p <the-defining-crate>`**. The second is the one that would actually have caught this, since `-p fuel-ir` was green throughout. **INDEPENDENTLY RE-VERIFIED, both arms, by a second party from a PINNED SHA (2026-08-07).** `d27pmruh` re-checked the default arm from a **clean detached worktree at `7b3abafd`** — not the shared checkout — `Checking fuel-dispatch v0.10.3` -> `Finished dev profile in 46.34s`, **0 `error[`, 0 bare `error:`**, 36 pre-existing unrelated warnings; full log retained. They moved the cuda arm UNVERIFIED -> verified only after getting `bzfevnl6`'s gate artifacts **first-hand rather than relayed through the architect** — the correct handling, and worth noting since routing evidence through a coordinator is exactly how provenance gets laundered. The detached-worktree read is GAP-145's rule being applied the same day it was written. **ATTRIBUTION — resolved as TWO LEDGERS, and this is the durable part of the whole incident.** The architect pushed back on `d27pmruh` calling the regression theirs, arguing it is structural: `DType::ALL` exhaustiveness shipped **in that same commit** aimed at this very class and missed because it was pointed one axis off. `d27pmruh` accepted that **for the registry** and declined it **for their own memory**, on the grounds that they had `gate-must-match-change-kind` written down *before* this commit and still chose `-p fuel-ir` — reclassifying it as purely structural would erase the personal-accountability signal that makes such a note work at all. **Both are right because the ledgers answer different questions: the registry records the structural fact that predicts recurrence for EVERYONE; a personal memory records the procedure-miss that predicts recurrence for THAT AGENT.** Same incident, two ledgers, no conflict — and collapsing them would damage whichever one lost. Registry position: **gate-design gap, not implementer error.** **RESUME SCOPE for mechanism (3), handed over by `d27pmruh` on banking (2026-08-07) — read this before starting.** **The marker doc-line is trivial; the entire value is WHICH ENUMS QUALIFY, and that is a read-the-consumers call.** `Scalar` is certain (wildcard-arm test applied end-to-end). **`DType` and `Op`/`OpKind` each need the same test applied by actually reading their match sites** — does any consumer have a *sound* wildcard default, or does every variant demand bespoke handling? `d27pmruh` explicitly declined to assert this from memory, on the grounds that **guessing it IS the "guard aimed one axis off" failure this row is about** — the same error, one level up. Doing only-`Scalar` now would leave the interesting part dangling while *reading* as decided rather than not-yet-looked-at. **Sequence:** per-enum qualification pass -> markers -> **a marker-integrity guard test that a marked enum is never also `#[non_exhaustive]`** (their idea, and the best part: it makes the two classes mutually exclusive *by test* rather than by discipline — seams beat vigilance) -> the workspace-scope-on-variant-addition rule into `CLAUDE.md`. **MECHANISMS (3) SHIPPED @ `da7fd8e5`** (pushed + reachability-confirmed before citing, per GAP-145). 5 files, +404, all additive: per-enum markers, the guard test, and the `CLAUDE.md` rule. **QUALIFICATION VERDICT — preserved because the METHOD is the reusable part, not the outcome. Marked: `Scalar`, `DType`, `Op`. Out: `OpKind`.** **`DType` — IN, and the decisive argument was a COUNTERFACTUAL, not a count.** ~30 concrete-per-variant matches (`size_in_bytes`, `as_str`, host-buffer tables, scalar ctors, dlpack tags) are exhaustive; ~111 *other* consumers wildcard but **decline loudly** (`Err`/`bail!`/`None`/`panic!`/`unreachable!`) — which is **sound**, because "unknown dtype => unsupported" *is* a correct answer. What settled it: **`#[non_exhaustive]` would force wildcards onto the CROSS-CRATE exhaustive tables** (`fuel-cpu-backend` host storage, the dlpack feature), so a new dtype would **silently route to a wrong buffer or a wrong dlpack tag**. **`Op` — IN, on a DIFFERENT argument, and the difference is the lesson.** The codebase **already** separates the closed vocabulary from the extensible one — `Op` is closed ("adding a new op means teaching every backend"), while `OpKind`, the dispatch key, is deliberately `#[non_exhaustive]` so persisted profiles parse forward. **Nuance kept rather than smoothed:** `Op`'s forcing functions live **in-crate** (`op_short_name`, ~125 arms; `backward` — both verified exhaustive firsthand), so `-p fuel-graph` already catches a variant-add and `#[non_exhaustive]` would not even break them; backends never match `fuel_graph::Op` at all (verified — they route through the `OpKind` binding table, and every backend `Op::` hit is a comment), so its 37 downstream wildcards are sound partial-maps gated by `op_to_op_kind -> None`. **So `Op`'s marker documents an existing architectural decision where the next op-adder stands; it does not add a guarantee.** **METHOD LESSON, and it is the transferable one: the marker text is PER-ENUM because the GUARANTEE differs.** Copying `Scalar`'s "no wildcard can be correct" line onto `Op` would have been **false**. **A marker that overstates is worse than no marker**, because it is trusted. `Op`'s text instead names its forcing functions, states that downstream partial-matches are fine, **and names its own exception** — `destructive_input`'s unsound `_ => None` (GAP-076). A marker that says where it does not hold beats one implying uniformity. **`OpKind` — OUT, and repurposed as the standing NEGATIVE CONTROL** in the guard test: it is the extensible class by design, so it must never be marked. **GUARD TEST** (`fuel-ir/tests/exhaustive_by_design_marker.rs`) — two assertions, **both watched RED under sabotage before the green was trusted**: *reach/non-vacuity* (reddens with the marker absent) and *invariant* (marked => never `#[non_exhaustive]`; reddened by temporarily giving `Scalar` `#[non_exhaustive]`). **Coverage is STATED, not inferred** — `found 3 marked enum(s): {"DType","Op","Scalar"}` — which is GAP-149 applied on the day it was written. Re-run on the rebased tree before pushing, because the guard whole-workspace-scans and 15 newly-merged commits were in range. | **CLOSED** — mechanisms shipped; follow-ups carved to GAP-059/075/076/148 |
