| GAP-058 | `docs/kernel-contracts/**` (`audited:` claims) vs `fuel-dispatch/src/fkc/verify/` ledger | A | **794 contract sections declare `audited: true` with NO verification-ledger entry AT ALL. Fuel's precision-based dispatch pre-filter consumes those claims. So the filter is currently trusting 794 assertions nothing has ever checked.** Measured by `bzfevnl6` while scoping GAP-037, and carved out because it is a **correctness-relevant gap in its own right**, not a sizing detail of a rename. **The numbers:** 1162 contract kernel sections parsed; **804** distinct kernel names; **21** distinct `kernel_ref`s in the verified ledger (**2.6%**); **853** sections author `audited: true`, of which **794 have no ledger entry**. **The ledger is TASK-SEEDED, not a corpus audit** — `task-4.5b/seed_cpu_ledger` (77), `capturedrun-4b/seed_cuda_ledger` (141), `v-fkc-9/seed_vulkan_ledger` (482), `task-4.6/rope_apply_harness` (1), unattributed (48). **And two of the 21 `kernel_ref`s are `baracuda` and `vulkan-slang` — PROVIDER-level rows, not kernels**, so even 2.6% flatters it. **Why this is the registry's own central pattern at corpus scale: `audited: true` EXISTS as a claim and nothing MAKES it true.** *existence != enforcement*, applied to 794 rows a production pre-filter believes. Contrast the FKC audit's original finding (a correctly-named validator never invoked): same shape, larger blast radius. **MEASURED 2026-08-08 — and the ARCHITECT'S FRAMING OF THIS ROW WAS OVERSTATED. Corrected here rather than quietly softened.** This row said the precision pre-filter "is currently trusting 794 assertions nothing has ever checked", implying a live operational defect. **`audited` is INERT for dispatch except through `bit_stable`:** `precision_digits()` reads only `max_relative` / `max_ulp` / `bit_stable_on_same_hardware`, never `notes`, and `PrecisionGuarantee::none(reason)` and `UNAUDITED` are field-identical apart from `notes`. **So the 794 were never causing wrong dispatch decisions. The risk was EPISTEMIC — people trusting the flag — with one narrow operational edge.** Re-read this row as a **trust defect**, not a live pre-filter defect. **THE MEASUREMENT WAS WRONG TWICE BEFORE IT WAS RIGHT, and the second catch is the finding.** The first pass concluded *zero* dispatch impact — the cheapest possible answer. Then the measurer's own instrument caught them: they had reported **"794 of 794 carry a numeric bound"**, a suspiciously perfect number *of the same shape as the 47-of-47 that had burned them hours earlier*. The control — **does the regex actually discriminate?** — showed it did not: **1162 of 1162** matched, because `max_ulp: ~` is YAML **null** and the `[^\s#]` pattern matched the `~`. **NULL-AWARE RE-MEASUREMENT — the real population is 578, not 794:** of the 794 unbacked `audited: true`, **139** carry a real numeric bound (**no effect** — the bound branch returns before `audited` is consulted); **578** have no bound but `bit_stable: true` (**real effect: `precision_digits` 9 -> 0**, because `Some(false) => UNAUDITED` drops the bit-stable claim); **77** have no bound and are not bit-stable (**no effect** — `none(reason)` and `UNAUDITED` both score 0). **ANSWER TO "DOES ANYTHING BECOME UNRUNNABLE": NO, not on the default path.** `PrecisionFloorFilter` short-circuits when the requirement `is_unconstrained()` — no bit-stable demand, no ulp/relative/absolute floor — and its own comment says *"most realizes don't set a floor"*. Unconstrained => every candidate admitted => **no eligibility change, no op loses a candidate.** The 578 change **ranking order only**: bit-stable kernels would stop outranking unaudited ones. **WHERE IT DOES BITE, AND IT COLLIDES WITH A CLAUSE WE NUMBERED HOURS EARLIER:** a caller that explicitly sets `require_bit_stable` would lose 578 candidates — and **`FKC-4.8-0001`** (GAP-036 inc 2) is the always-built commitment that `fuel-cpu-backend` declares **>=1 bit-stable kernel per primitive op**. **If any of the 578 is the ONLY bit-stable contract for its op, the honest flip breaks a clause that became normative the same night.** **Two paths, and choosing is a DECISION with real behaviour change, not mechanics:** **(1) no-evidence => unaudited** — honest, correct by KISS's derived-not-authored logic, and it flips ~794 sections, with **downstream effect on precision-based dispatch filtering** that must be measured before it lands, not after; **(2) extend verification to the corpus first** — a program, not a row. **Neither is a doc change.** **⚠️ RULING REVERSED THE SAME NIGHT — THE ARCHITECT'S "FLIP THE 216 NOW, FREE HONESTY, NO RISK" WAS WRONG, and the flip was ATTEMPTED, GATED, AND REVERTED. Nothing landed; tree clean, 734/0 restored.** The superseded ruling read: *flip the 139 bounded + 77 unbounded-not-bit-stable, provably zero-effect on ranking and eligibility, 27% off the untrue-claim population at no risk.* **The word "provably" was doing work it had not earned: the proof was about `precision_digits`, and two other constraints live elsewhere.** **CONSTRAINT 1 — the population is not 216, and the boundary is PRINCIPLED, not mechanical.** The selector matched **185, not 216**. The 31 stragglers all carry trailing comments of the form `audited: true  # PrecisionGuarantee::none(reason) — audited, no static bound applies`. The split is exact: **0 of the 185 carry a comment; all 31 do.** So the population divides into **bare unbacked flag** vs **documented human audit conclusion**, and flipping the latter would contradict the sentence written on the same line. **This also reconnects to GAP-037's own finding, established hours earlier and then not applied here: the ledger was NEVER a corpus-wide audit record, so "absent from the ledger" != "never audited". Those 31 are the visible proof** — a recorded audit conclusion with no ledger row. **GAP-058's population is therefore smaller than 216 and its true size is now UNKNOWN.** **CONSTRAINT 2 — a validator rule couples `audited` to DETERMINISM, entirely outside the precision mapping that was analysed.** With the 185 flipped, `ci_lint_corpus_parse_lower_validate` failed with **41 hard failures**: FKC §10.6 (**V-FKC-6**) requires `determinism: nondeterministic` to carry `audited: true` with a `none(reason)` precision — ***no silent unaudited nondeterminism***. The rule exists precisely so nondeterminism cannot ship unexamined. Flipping those breaks it. **WHY THE MEASURER REVERTED INSTEAD OF REFINING THE SELECTOR A THIRD TIME — and this is the judgement worth copying:** two attempts produced two previously-unknown constraints. **That is not bad luck; it means the model of the flag's consumers is incomplete, and a third refinement would be ASSUMING the second constraint was the last one.** **THEIR OWN DIAGNOSIS, which is the uncomfortable part: this is the GAP-015 wrinkle again.** *"I enumerated what the flag means for ONE CONSUMER and treated that as what it MEANS."* The lesson they wrote hours earlier — **when replacing something, enumerate what callers were getting from it incidentally** — they applied to a **lock** and did not apply to a **flag**. **REVISED RULING (architect, adopting the reversal): NO flip is currently safe to land, including the "provably zero-effect" subset. The prerequisite is an ENUMERATION OF `audited`'s CONSUMERS — not a better measurement of its POPULATION.** Known consumers so far: `precision.rs` (guarantee mapping), `validate.rs` (**V-FKC-6** determinism coherence), the bit-stable coverage lint — **and the count of two-found-by-accident is the reason to assume more.** Moved into **GAP-077** as its first step, because building the coverage harness without it would only relocate the surprise. **(2) HOLD THE 578** pending the coverage harness below — they are the only ones with a live consequence, and that consequence is a clause we just made normative. **CARVED OUT: the bit-stable coverage harness (GAP-077).** The measurer **stopped rather than estimated**, exactly as instructed, because answering *"does any op lose its LAST bit-stable candidate?"* needs the **populated binding table under a simulated flip**, keyed by `(op, dtypes, backend)`. **A corpus scan cannot answer it** — contract sections do not map 1:1 to binding keys (chassis sections fan out over dtypes), so counting `.fkc.md` text would over- or under-count **in ways they could not bound**. That is a build, not a measurement. **MEASURED VERDICT (GAP-077 @ `c81fcfab`): on the live CPU slice — where `FKC-4.8-0001` binds — THE FLIP IS PROVABLY INERT ON THE BINDING TABLE.** Zero of 601 CPU entries are bit-stable at the contract->table boundary either side of the flip, because `gate_precision` has already erased every unbacked claim. Measured, not reasoned. Off-CPU remains reasoned-only. **⚠️ CORRECTION TO THIS ROW'S REASONING, which was WRONG even though its verdict survives.** This row said the 578 would change "**ranking order only** — no eligibility change". That analysed **filter 1 of a 3-filter chain**. **`BitStablePreferenceFilter`** (`ranker/filters/bit_stable_pref.rs:31`) is a **soft** filter with `min_remaining: 1`, sits in `default_chain` (`filters/mod.rs:45`), is live at `plan.rs:1542`, and is **not gated on the precision requirement**. Per `chain.rs:54-70`, a soft filter whose kept-set meets `min_remaining` **APPLIES** — so on the **default path**, whenever ≥1 bit-stable candidate exists, **every non-bit-stable candidate is DROPPED**. That is candidate *elimination*, not preference ordering, and the name understates it. **The verdict is unchanged (measured), but the stated reasoning would have misled the next flip** — and the finder said so explicitly rather than letting a correction that cost them nothing look load-bearing. **⚠️ INHERITED HAZARD — pin this BEFORE computing the first verdict (predicted by `bzfevnl6`, see GAP-149).** This row derives a verdict from a ledger covering **2.6%** of the corpus, which is precisely the shape whose natural bug is a **silent full pass**: *found no evidence => nothing flagged* produces the same output as *everything is fine*. The loud direction (*no evidence => everything unaudited*) is survivable; the silent one ships. **Assert a minimum-evidence threshold before the first verdict is computed**, and make the gate fail rather than pass when the ledger query returns less than it should. **⚠️ FIGURE CORRECTION carried into GAP-037: its "85 CUDA kernels carrying `audited: false`" is WRONG.** `audited: false` appears **346** times corpus-wide and **CUDA is not the worst**: cpu 167, dispatch 69, cuda 44, quantized 32, metal 16, vulkan 14, reference 4. Either the 85 was partly closed or it counted kernels where this counts occurrences — **re-measure with a stated method rather than inheriting it** (GAP-145). | OPEN |
| GAP-059 | `fuel-ir/src/dlpack/convert.rs:44` (`dtype_to_fdx`) + `:109` (`dl_dtype`) | A | **THE `--features dlpack` BUILD IS BROKEN ON MAIN — artifact-confirmed, not inferred.** `cargo check -p fuel-ir --features dlpack` fails **E0004 x2: `DType::F8E6M2` not covered** in both exhaustive dlpack consumers. `F8E6M2` was added without updating them; the feature is **non-default**, so main's default build stayed green and nobody saw it. Found by `d27pmruh` while sweeping DType for GAP-049(3). **THIS IS THE SECOND INSTANCE OF THE IDENTICAL PATTERN IN ONE NIGHT, AND THAT CHANGES ITS WEIGHT.** GAP-049 was `Scalar::F8E8M0` added without updating its exhaustive consumers, one of which was reachable only under `--features cuda`. This is `DType::F8E6M2` added without updating its exhaustive consumers, reachable only under `--features dlpack`. **Different enums, different features, different authors, same failure: a variant added, exhaustive consumers missed, the break parked behind a gate nobody ran.** Two independent instances is no longer anecdote — **it is the empirical case for the workspace-scope-on-variant-addition rule being written into `CLAUDE.md` under GAP-049(3), and both should be cited in it.** **Correctly NOT fixed as a drive-by:** the fix is entangled with **GAP-045** (F8E6M2's deferred encoding), so choosing what these two arms should *do* is a real decision, not a mechanical arm-add. **FIXED 2026-08-08 @ `4cc0cb75`.** Ruling applied: **typed decline, not fabrication.** Both functions return `Result` and yield `UnsupportedDTypeForOp` naming GAP-045. **Why declining beats a placeholder, and it is stronger than "we don't know yet":** an FDX code and a `DLDataType{code,bits,lanes}` are a **stable WIRE contract read by another process** — a placeholder is not a local inaccuracy, a foreign runtime **strides over memory** with it, so the failure surfaces as silently mis-decoded tensor data rather than an error. And the guess would look *right* in review (`bits: 8`, by analogy with the other fp8s) while **nothing in the codebase can distinguish "correct width" from "width nobody has decided yet"** — no test could catch it. **This did NOT wait on GAP-045 and did not pre-empt it:** declining is sound whatever the encoding turns out to be; when it is authored, the arm becomes a mapping and neither signature changes again. **Propagation was compiler-driven, not grep-driven:** `SType::to_fdx` → `Result<Option<FDXQuant>>` (`Ok(None)` still means "projects to no quant block" — the two outcomes kept **distinct** so a decline can never be read as "nothing to describe"), then `fuel-memory`'s `dtype_to_fdx_code`/`view()` via `?`. **The tests PIN the decline rather than skipping it:** three corpus-wide `ALL_DTYPES` loops would otherwise have needed an exclusion, and **a skip silently starts passing the moment the decline is replaced by an invented code** — exactly the regression being guarded; two now assert `is_err()` explicitly and the duplicate-code scan `flatten()`s + counts what it collected, so it stays exact instead of comparing against a stale variant count. **GATED WITH THE FEATURE ACTUALLY ENABLED — the gate nobody ran:** `-p fuel-ir --features dlpack --all-targets` 101→0; `-p fuel-memory --features dlpack --all-targets` 101→0; `cargo test --features dlpack` green on both (fuel-ir 161/11/9/1, fuel-memory 26/1); **default builds of both crates `--all-targets` still 0**, so the fix introduces no default-path regression. | **FIXED** |

From a bare filename it reads exactly like `C:\Projects\baracuda`, which **is** a real sibling requiring propose-first (`CLAUDE.md`). **Pausing to check was correct and should not be trained out by the outcome** — the check costs a message; skipping it costs a cross-project incident. Full paths are given above so the next reader does not face the ambiguity. **Wildcards that do not DECLINE on an unknown dtype but FABRICATE an answer — the mirror image of GAP-049's marker intent, and nastier than the build break it was found beside.** The GAP-049(3) discriminator asks *can a wildcard arm do something correct?* These are the arms that answer **wrongly** rather than loudly: `fuel-vulkan-backend/src/lib.rs:10421` `_ => 4` (a fabricated byte size); five baracuda size helpers `_ => 0` (`elementwise.rs:623`, `reduce.rs:230`, `norm.rs:389`, `binary.rs:471`, `softmax.rs:211`); correctness-fixtures `lib.rs:266` `_ => Ok(())` (**validation that passes what it cannot check**); plus two borderline — `fuel-memory/src/dlpack_view.rs:252` and `fuel-graph/src/lib.rs:5606` (`masked_fill`'s reject-set **defaults to allow**). **SHARPENED 2026-08-08 by GAP-059's fix, and this is the strongest form of the rule: FABRICATION ACROSS A PROCESS BOUNDARY IS UNFALSIFIABLE IN-REPO.** An FDX code or a `DLDataType{code,bits,lanes}` is a **wire contract read by another process**, so a fabricated value is not a local inaccuracy — **a foreign runtime strides over memory with it**, and the failure surfaces as silently mis-decoded tensor data far from the guess. Two properties make it uniquely dangerous: **the guess looks RIGHT in review** (`bits: 8` by analogy with the other fp8s), and **nothing in this codebase can distinguish "the correct width" from "a width nobody has decided yet"** — so **no test could ever have caught it.** **The in-repo cost of a wrong cross-boundary guess is ZERO, which is exactly why it gets made.** Sites in this row that cross a boundary (dlpack/FDX views, anything feeding a foreign runtime) should be triaged FIRST for that reason, ahead of the purely internal size helpers. **SITE 1 LANDED @ `2eca1666`, AND IT WAS WORSE THAN THIS ROW PREDICTED — the detail is the finding.** The row called the correctness-fixture `_ => Ok(())` *"validation that passes what it cannot check."* In fact the compiler-forced consumer (`max_rel_err_vs_fixture`) mapped that false `Ok` to **`0.0` rel_err — "perfect agreement."** **An unvalidatable dtype did not merely skip the check; it scored as the BEST POSSIBLE RESULT**, and would have won any ranking or gate keyed on that number. A silently-skipped validation is bad; **one that reports perfection manufactures confidence nothing earned.** Now declines (`CorrectnessDrift::UnvalidatableDType`) and maps to `f32::INFINITY`. **SITE 2 LANDED @ `0589a95e`** — `sub_byte_bits_and_packing`'s `_ => (8, PACKING_BYTE_ALIGNED)`, a fabricated **FDX wire width** reachable by any future sub-byte dtype (`size == 0` routes there via `is_sub_byte`); a foreign runtime would stride over packed bytes at the wrong element width. Triaged first per the boundary-crossing rule and fixed to mirror GAP-059's decline pattern immediately below it. **REUSABLE METHOD — how to BORN-RED a guard against something that does not exist yet.** Both sites are unfalsifiable in-repo for their real trigger (a future dtype nobody can construct). The technique: **use the OLD FABRICATION, wrapped in `Ok`, as the red state.** That proves the test discriminates **decline-vs-fabricate** rather than merely passing — a genuine born-red where a regression lock would otherwise have been the honest ceiling. **DISCRIMINATION WORTH COPYING:** `physical_elem_bytes`'s `size == 0 => 1` was **left alone** — it is documented FDX §3.3 physical width, not a fabrication. **A sweep that converts everything SHAPED like the defect is worse than one that converts what IS the defect.** **OPEN QUESTION ON SITE 3 (five baracuda `dtype_byte_size` helpers, positive-controlled as DEAD — zero callers): convert, or DELETE?** A fabricating helper with no callers is a landmine; converting it to a declining `Option` **keeps the landmine and makes it safe if someone picks it up**, while deleting removes it — a future caller would write one against the dtype set of their day rather than inheriting a `_ => 0` that was made "safe" years earlier. Convert only if these are a **deliberately staged API about to be wired**; otherwise delete. **Both need the same CUDA build to verify, so the cost is identical and only the end state differs.** Site 4 (vulkan `_ => 4`) has **7 real callers** and carries no such question. **Why this ranks above a broken build: a broken build is loud and self-announcing. These are silent and produce a WRONG ANSWER** — a future dtype gets a wrong byte size, or passes validation it should have failed. **Declining loudly on an unknown dtype is sound (~111 DType consumers do exactly that and are fine); fabricating is not.** That distinction is the whole content of the GAP-049 ruling, and these sites are on the wrong side of it. **⚠️ MOST OF THESE SITES ARE GPU-GATED, which changes who can take this row and when (established 2026-08-08).** The vulkan `dtype_size` and the five baracuda `_ => 0` helpers **need `--features vulkan` / `--features cuda` to even compile**, so verifying any fix means `gpu-run` plus the `vcvarsall` CUDA recipe. **This row must not be picked up at the tail of a long session** — a GPU-gated multi-crate change under a tired reader is precisely where `CLAUDE.md`'s documented GPU-build-trap misdiagnoses happen (a red gate that says nothing about the code, blamed on the change under test). **CPU-only subset, separable if someone wants a smaller bite:** `fuel-correctness-fixtures` `lib.rs:266` `_ => Ok(())`, `fuel-memory/src/dlpack_view.rs:252`, and `fuel-graph/src/lib.rs:5606` (`masked_fill` reject-set polarity). **⚠️ THE `masked_fill` SITE WAS RE-SPECIFIED 2026-08-08 — the row's "polarity flip" framing was the WEAKER fix and the architect's was the anticipation that got corrected.** **Consumer sweep result: exactly ONE real consumer** (`fuel-core/src/lazy.rs:4686`, the `LazyTensor::masked_fill` wrapper; attention-style masking, float dtypes) plus one test. **The "unknown consumers" caution this row carried was correctly raised and is now correctly retired by measurement.** **Current shape is a DENYLIST** — `if matches!(dtype, F4\|F6E2M3\|F6E3M2\|F8E8M0\|F8E6M2) { reject }`, everything else implicitly allowed (5 rejected, 11 allowed). The defect is that **a future dtype defaults to ALLOW**. **RULED: take the EXHAUSTIVE MATCH, not the polarity flip.** An allowlist (runtime default-reject) *manages* the blast radius and can silently start rejecting a currently-valid dtype if the list is wrong. **An exhaustive match with no wildcard, preserving every current verdict exactly, has ZERO blast radius — no current consumer can break because no current verdict changes — and a future dtype becomes a COMPILE ERROR forcing conscious classification.** In the implementer's words: **it does not flip a default, it removes the concept of a default.** Composes with `DType` already being EXHAUSTIVE-BY-DESIGN (GAP-049) — the same marker doing its job at a second site. **AND GAP-097 MAKES THIS URGENT RATHER THAN PRUDENT: up to ELEVEN KISS dtypes are scheduled to arrive** (`e5m2`, `c32`, `c64`, two reserved `fnuz` spellings, `u16`, `u64`, `bool`, `s4`, `u4`, `b1`). **Under the denylist all eleven would silently become permitted for `masked_fill`** — including sub-byte packed formats and complex, where the operation's meaning is genuinely unobvious. Under the exhaustive match each forces a decision **by the person adding it**, who is the only one positioned to make it. **FIRST FIRING OF THE MECHANISM, 2026-08-08 — and it worked exactly as designed.** Adding `DType::F8E5M2` (GAP-097) hit the exhaustive match as a compile error, **forcing a conscious classification from the person adding the dtype.** Verdict: **ALLOW**, decided by the adder, not pre-settled by the architect — who ruled the *mechanism* and explicitly declined to rule the *verdict*. **The reasoning recorded at the site, and it is sharper than the architect's version: the reject set is NOT "exotic dtypes" — it is TWO distinct NON-VALUE categories.** `F8E8M0`/`F8E6M2` are **scales** that ride as sibling operands and are not element value dtypes at all; `F4`/`F6E2M3`/`F6E3M2` are **block-scoped elements with no standalone byte layout** — a single element is not addressable without its block. **Neither category has a well-defined "fill one element with this value" operation, which is what `masked_fill` needs.** `F8E5M2` is a value dtype in exactly the way `F8E4M3` (already allowed) is. **So ALLOW continues the classification; REJECT would be the exception needing justification.** **⚠️ THE COUNTER-ARGUMENT WAS STATED AND WEIGHED RATHER THAN MISSED — and its resolution is a DURABLE PRINCIPLE worth reusing: `F8E5M2` has no `HostBuffer` variant**, so after this lands `masked_fill` build-time-**accepts** a tensor that **cannot be allocated** (`fuel-cpu-backend` declines it at `cpu_zeros`, `cpu_alloc_uninit`, and both `as_host_buffer_ref` maps). **It still loses, because THE GUARD IS SEMANTIC AND THE GAP IS CAPABILITY, AND THEY ARE DIFFERENT QUESTIONS.** Writing REJECT would encode the claim *"E5M2 has no representable fill value"* — **which is false** — and **a falsehood in a guard outlives the temporary condition that motivated it: long after storage lands, the arm still lies.** The capability gap is enforced where it actually lives, as a loud typed `UnsupportedDTypeForOp` at allocation. **Semantically-true guard upstream; honest capability decline downstream.** **Genuine residual, correctly deferred rather than conflated:** the graph guard cannot see *"has a host buffer"*. That is a build-time capability check worth having **when E5M2 storage lands** — not a reason to put a false statement in the fill guard now. **Lock requirement: the test must fail if a variant is added WITHOUT a verdict.** An assertion that merely counts 5-reject/11-allow would pass a future dtype landing in either bucket by accident — the compile error is the guard, and the test must protect the **classification**, not the tally. `d27pmruh` verified the vulkan and cuda-elementwise sites firsthand; the rest are from the sweep. **UNVERIFIED, from the sweep only: `metal` `storage.rs:105` omits `I8`** — check before citing (GAP-145). **COMPLETE — all 5 sites on `origin/main`, each verified by artifact (`2eca1666`, `0589a95e`, `0e848bdc`, `75945a22`, `02472432`, all reachability-confirmed). Net: 3 loud declines, 1 deletion, 1 exhaustive match — every fabricating dtype wildcard in this row is now a decline, gone, or compile-forced.** **SITE 4 IS THE ROW'S OWN JUSTIFICATION, AND IT IS STRONGER THAN THE ROW ARGUED.** The vulkan `_ => 4` had **7 live callers**; replacing it with a delegation to `size_in_bytes()` changed the answer for **four dtypes** — `F8E8M0`/`F8E6M2` **4 -> 1** and `F4`/`F6*` **4 -> 0**. **So the fabricated value was not merely "a guess that might be wrong": it was DEMONSTRABLY wrong for a third of the dtype set, on a path with real callers.** The row was filed on the principle; the fix produced the evidence. Born-red pinned it (`dtype_size(F8E8M0)` left:4 right:1). **SITE 3's DELETION DOUBLED AS THE POSITIVE CONTROL.** Five dead baracuda `dtype_byte_size` helpers were deleted rather than converted — **the compile then adjudicated the "zero callers" claim** that a grep had only asserted. **Converting would have left that absence claim resting on the grep forever.** Verified with `Checking fuel-cuda-backend` + `Finished`, capped at `BARACUDA_FORGE_THREADS=6`, solo run. **SITE 5 sabotage-validated by an `F4`<->`F32` SWAP** — both directions fail, then revert green. That tests the *classification*, not a tally, exactly as ruled. **⚠️ THE METAL `storage.rs` ENTRY IS ADJUDICATED OUT OF THIS ROW — it is NOT a fabricating wildcard.** The sweep flagged it unverified; it was then **read**, and it is **already an exhaustive match with an explicit `Err` arm** for the exotic dtypes. So an "omitted" `I8` would fall to a **loud decline**, which is the behaviour this row exists to produce. **A capability question (does Metal support `I8`?) is a different row from a fabrication question, and the unverified sweep entry conflated them.** Recorded rather than deleted, because the sweep's own caveat — *UNVERIFIED, from the sweep only* — is what made the re-read happen. | **FIXED** |
| GAP-076 | `fuel-graph/src/lib.rs:1188` (`Op::destructive_input`, `_ => None`) | A | **The one arguably-unsound `Op` wildcard, and its failure mode is a SILENT DATA RACE — the worst on the GAP-049(3) incidental list and the hardest to attribute back to its cause.** `destructive_input` returns `None` for any unmatched op, so **a future destructive / in-place op reads as NON-DESTRUCTIVE**, the scheduler omits the ordering edges that would serialize it against readers of the same buffer, and the result is a race that reproduces intermittently and points nowhere near this line. Verified firsthand by `d27pmruh` during the GAP-049(3) qualification sweep. **Note where it sits: INSIDE `Op`, the enum GAP-049(3) is marking EXHAUSTIVE-BY-DESIGN.** The marker is honest — `Op`'s downstream partial-maps genuinely are sound — but this in-crate helper is the exception, and **`Op`'s marker text should say so**, because a generic "this enum is closed" note does not warn the next op-adder about the one default that is unsound. A marker that names its own exception is worth more than one that implies uniformity. **FIXED @ `e3074cd4`.** Primitive arms are now **exhaustive** — dropping the `_ => None` made the compiler enumerate every unclassified primitive, each was classified, zero E0004 remained. **`Op` being marked closed (GAP-049) does the work from here: a new primitive op will not compile until it is classified.** **THE FUSED HALF IS THE ARCHITECTURALLY INTERESTING PART.** `Op::Fused` is an **open registry**, so it cannot be enum-exhaustive, and it previously hardcoded `if id == INPLACE_AFFINE` — **a fact about a fused op, stored away from the fused op, kept in sync by memory.** Now a **mandatory `FusedOpEntry::destructive_input` field declared beside `decompose`/`pattern`** — the recipe principle extended to a third property, read via `default_registry().entry(id)`. **Adding the field forced all 28 construction sites at compile time**, which is the seam working rather than a discipline being followed. **AND THE "MOVED WILDCARD" TRAP — the architect's stated hazard — IS CLOSED BY CONSTRUCTION, NOT BY A DEFAULT.** The risk was that a registry lookup returning "unknown" would relocate the wildcard rather than remove it. It cannot: **runtime-registered fused ops are functional recipe regions, and the `PatternNode` grammar cannot EXPRESS a destructive op** — so they are non-destructive **by construction**, and there is no unknown case to default. **Fourth instance tonight of "make the bad state unrepresentable rather than police it"** (after GAP-015's copy-on-write, GAP-036's no-sidecar-at-all, and the mandatory declaration itself). **HONESTLY BILLED AS A REGRESSION LOCK, NOT A BORN-RED — and the distinction was checked, not assumed.** The suspects were examined firsthand (`ScatterIntoSlot` — fresh `Storage`, correctly `None`; `IndexAdd`/`ScatterAdd` — functional; `Copy`/`View` — non-writing) and **nothing was misclassified before the fix**. Stated in the test's own comment rather than dressed up. **`Op`'s EXHAUSTIVE-BY-DESIGN marker was updated in the same change so it stays honest**: `destructive_input` is now named as a **third in-crate forcing function** instead of the "unsound `_ => None`" hazard — the marker tracks the code rather than outliving it. Gates: `fuel-graph` lib **485/485**, all `derive_ordering` consumer tests green, `--all-targets` clean, GAP-049 marker guard still 7/7, **re-verified on the rebased tree** because the dlpack fix had moved `fuel-ir` underneath. | **FIXED** |
| GAP-077 | Enumerate `audited`'s consumers, THEN a bit-stable coverage harness over the populated binding table | B | **STEP 1 IS THE CONSUMER ENUMERATION, and it was promoted here 2026-08-08 after a flip attempt found TWO unknown constraints in two tries (GAP-058).** Whoever takes this: **start from the consumer enumeration, not from the ledger** — that instruction is from the person who just paid for it. Known consumers: `precision.rs` (guarantee mapping), `validate.rs` (V-FKC-6: `nondeterministic` requires `audited: true` + `none(reason)`), the bit-stable coverage lint. **Two of those three were discovered by a gate failing, not by the sweep — so treat the list as incomplete until a positive-controlled sweep says otherwise.** **Carved out of GAP-058 because it is a BUILD, not a measurement — and because its absence is the only thing blocking an otherwise-free honesty fix.** Question it must answer: **under a simulated flip of GAP-058's 578 unbacked `bit_stable: true` sections to unaudited, does any `(op, dtypes, backend)` binding key lose its LAST bit-stable candidate?** **Why a corpus scan CANNOT answer it (established, not assumed):** contract sections do not map 1:1 to binding keys — chassis sections **fan out over dtypes** — so counting `.fkc.md` text would over- or under-count **in ways the measurer could not bound**. They stopped rather than estimating, which is the behaviour this registry wants and the reason this row exists rather than a guess. **Shape:** load the binding table **twice** — once as-is, once with the 578 lowered — and diff bit-stable coverage per binding key. **The output is the decision input for GAP-058's remaining 578 and for whether `FKC-4.8-0001` survives an honest flip.** **GAP-149 applies to this harness too, both halves:** assert the loaded table is non-trivial **and** that the bit-stable predicate discriminates within it — a coverage differ that finds "no key lost its last candidate" is indistinguishable from one that loaded nothing or classified everything the same way. **ANSWERED + CLOSED @ `c81fcfab`** (`fuel-dispatch/tests/bit_stable_coverage_flip.rs`, test-only, no production source touched). **Does any `(op, dtypes, backend)` key lose its LAST bit-stable candidate under GAP-058's flip? NO — and the reason is not the reassuring one: NO KEY HAS ONE.** Measured: **601 CPU binding keys / 601 entries; bit-stable 0 of 601; UNAUDITED 601 of 601; 281 ledger downgrade warnings at import; 184 sections flipped; keys losing a last bit-stable candidate: 0; baseline and flipped coverage maps IDENTICAL** — not merely "no last candidate lost". `gate_precision` (V-FKC-9, `register.rs:389/398`) erases every unbacked claim **before the table sees it**, so the flip touches sections whose claim has already collapsed. **SCOPE LIMITS STATED RATHER THAN BURIED:** **CPU only** — `CudaLinkRegistry`/`VulkanLinkRegistry` are `#[cfg(feature=…)]` and unmeasurable on a default-feature run. The erasure argument applies *a fortiori* off-CPU (`gate_precision` runs identically and there is **no fill pass** to re-upgrade) — **but that is reasoning, not measurement, and was explicitly not banked.** Also **42 of 114 contract files are dormant** and cannot affect the table at all. **THE CONTROL THAT TAUGHT SOMETHING NEW — see GAP-149.** The sabotage arm **could not** serve as the discrimination control: with the corpus degenerate it strips 396 claims and still reports 0, **indistinguishable from a broken differ**. Discrimination was therefore established on **constructed** maps, three directions: a real loss is reported; self-vs-self reports nothing (catches stuck-at-yes); and a 2->1 reduction is **not** counted as losing the last (pins the exact boundary the clause asks about). **THE HARNESS CAUGHT ITS OWN DEFECT:** the first live-set derivation admitted **test-only** `include_str!`s (`cpu/quant-matmul.fkc.md`, embedded under `#[cfg(test)]`, never imported by `register_cpu_kernels`). It failed loudly here, but **in the general case it would have silently added binding keys that do not ship.** Narrowed to production registrars and cross-checked against the real table. **AND THE MANDATED "PREDICATE SELECTS NEITHER ALL NOR NONE" CONTROL FAILED — CORRECTLY.** Failing is what stopped a vacuous verdict being reported as a clean one. Kept as a change-detector that now fires if ledger backing ever appears. The zero is asserted **together with its cause** (281 downgrades > 0; 601/601 carrying the UNAUDITED sentinel) so nobody can misread it as "contracts never declared bit-stability" — a different world with a different answer. | **CLOSED — answered** |
| GAP-078 | `fuel-core/src/lazy_{gemma3,glm4,lfm2,phi3,qwen2,qwen3,qwen3_moe,smollm3}.rs` — the UNQUANTIZED models | B | **SUB-SCOPE of GAP-029 (not a carve-out — CireSnave scoped that row to all 11 families on 2026-08-08). Eight causal-LM families have no persistent-KV decode, and this is a DECODE-COVERAGE gap, not a quantized one — it would exist identically if quantization were deleted.** That reframe remains the correct headline for this work; it now describes a **later increment of one lane** rather than a separate decision. Whoever lands it is building persistent-KV decode on eight **unquantized** base models and doing nothing quantization-specific. Split out of GAP-029 on 2026-08-08 after its scoping pass established that the quantized wrappers are thin newtypes and the blocker is the base model. **MEASURED 2026-08-08 — and this row is a REFACTOR WITH EIGHT CONSUMERS, not eight independent ports. The distinction matters because those two framings attract different owners and different estimates.** The scoper checked whether the eight base models are LLaMA-shaped internally, hoping they wrapped `LlamaModel` and the work would be mechanical. **They do not.** `SmolLm3Model`, `Qwen2Model`, `Qwen3Model`, `Qwen3MoeModel`, `Glm4Model`, `Gemma3Model`, `Phi3Model`, `LFM2Model` each carry **their own `Config` + `Weights` types and their own forward.** **And the persistent-decode machinery is not portable as written:** `refresh_decode_session` / `build_and_realize_first_decode_token` / `rebind_and_realize_prebuilt` are **`LlamaModel` INHERENT METHODS keyed to `LlamaConfig`/`LlamaWeights`.** **So there are exactly two shapes, and only one is maintainable: eight copies of a subtle piece of machinery, or ONE generalisation of it over a model trait.** The generalisation must survive **real architectural variation, not naming differences** — Gemma3 (sliding window), Qwen3Moe (MoE FFN), LFM2 (conv layers). **THE SEAM IS NARROWER THAN FEARED — a trait generalisation is a real design, not a wishful one (measured 2026-08-08).** The whole `build_and_realize_first_decode_token` body touches only **six scalar dims** (`n_layers`, `dim`, `vocab_size`, `head_dim`, `n_kv_heads`, `norm_eps`), **four weight fields** (`token_embedding`, `output`, `layers`, `final_norm_gain`), and **three model methods**: `decode_shape_key()`, `build_token_rope_mask_arcs()`, `apply_layer_with_kv_writes()` — the last being the architecture hook. **AND THE DUPLICATION IS NOT HYPOTHETICAL — IT HAS ALREADY HAPPENED ONCE.** `PhiModel` carries **its own copy** of `rebind_and_realize_prebuilt` (`lazy.rs:12114`) and its own `decode_shape_key` (`:11514`), parallel to `LlamaModel`'s at `:9542`/`:8516`. **There are two copies of this machinery on main today; eight more ports would make it ten.** That is evidence for the refactor framing rather than aesthetics. **STRATIFIED — the eight are NOT uniform, and the three hard cases stress DIFFERENT parts of the seam:** **(i) SIX families bounded** (Qwen2, Qwen3, SmolLm3, Glm4, Phi3, **Qwen3Moe**) — the refactor plus per-architecture `apply_layer` work. **Qwen3Moe is NOT a hard case after all:** MoE routing lives inside the per-layer FFN, so it sits entirely within `apply_layer_with_kv_writes` and the existing hook already has the right granularity. **(ii) Gemma3 — forces the rope/mask hook to become PER-LAYER.** Layer `i` alternates: `(i+1) % sliding_window_pattern > 0` -> sliding-window mask **plus the LOCAL RoPE base**; else full-causal **plus the global base** (`lazy_gemma3.rs:110-112`, `:61-66`). `build_token_rope_mask_arcs` is built **once for the whole model** today, so it must take a layer index and return both a mask and a rope table. Awkward but tractable — a signature change, and the held decode graph bakes per-layer arcs anyway. **(iii) LFM2 -> CARVED OUT to GAP-098. It breaks the TRAIT, not its implementations.** **Design NOT yet proposed and explicitly NOT to be built before it is** — the scoper will bring it to the architect and CireSnave first. Each family still carries genuine per-architecture work on top: sliding window, MoE routing, hybrid conv, RoPE variants. **Not one task eight times — and not eight tasks either.** **SEPARABLE QUICK WIN, called out so it is not lost inside the eight:** `PhiModel` (`fuel-core/src/lazy.rs:11420`) **already has the inherent `forward_with_kv_context_persistent` method but NO `DecodeModel` impl.** Wiring the trait is close to free. **It does NOT serve GAP-029** — the quantized Phi wrapper wraps `Phi3Model` (`lazy_phi3.rs`), a *different* type — so do not count it toward that row. | OPEN |
| GAP-079 | `fuel-core/src/lazy_quantized_{t5,whisper}.rs` + the `DecodeModel` contract | C | **SUB-SCOPE of GAP-029 (not a carve-out — see that row's 2026-08-08 scope override). Two encoder-decoder families need a DIFFERENT CONTRACT, not more impls.** `DecodeModel`'s shape — `n_layers` / `n_kv_heads` / `head_dim` plus a single `KvCache` — **cannot model cross-attention KV**, which an encoder-decoder needs alongside its self-attention cache. **So this is a contract-design question**, and implementing it against the current trait would either misrepresent the cache or bolt on a second one informally. Filed separately so it reads as a **deferred design decision** rather than "T5 and Whisper were skipped" (GAP-046 precedent). Related: GAP-034 — a type whose vocabulary cannot express a fact somebody needs to state. | OPEN |
| GAP-098 | `DecodeModel` geometry contract (`n_layers`/`n_kv_heads`/`head_dim`) + `KvCache::with_capacity`; consumer `fuel-core/src/lazy_lfm2.rs` | B | **LFM2 CANNOT DECODE THROUGH THE `DecodeModel` SURFACE AT ALL — the trait cannot express heterogeneous per-layer state. Carved out of GAP-078 because it is a CONTRACT change, not a model-file change.** LFM2 interleaves **two block types**: GQA attention blocks, and **ShortConv "LIV" blocks** — depthwise causal conv with input/output gating (`lazy_lfm2.rs:4, 17-33, 61-63`). **The conv blocks write NO KV at all**; their decode state is a rolling window of `conv_kernel_size` (typically 4) past inputs. **`DecodeModel`'s three geometry methods describe ONE uniform KV geometry replicated across every layer**, and `KvCache::with_capacity(n_layers, n_kv_heads, head_dim, max_seq_len, …)` allocates exactly that. For LFM2 a fraction of those layers need a **conv ring buffer** instead. **Allocating full KV for them is not merely wasteful — it is the WRONG STATE, so the model cannot decode correctly through this surface.** **Constitution-level, not model-level:** it touches §14 lifecycle / the cache-geometry contract. Do not attempt it as part of a model port. **CARVED OUT ON GAP-142's OWN PRINCIPLE, and the finder cited it: a resolution inherits the scope of its FIX, not its TITLE.** Someone closing *"GAP-078: 8 families"* having done six-and-a-half would be reporting **accurately by title and wrongly by content** — the precise failure GAP-142 exists to prevent. **2026-08-12 — THIS ROW'S HAZARD IS NO LONGER HYPOTHETICAL, SEE GAP-166.** `DecodeModel`'s geometry methods already assert a KV shape that **MLA (`DeepSeek2Model`, shipped and decode-capable)** does not have, and they are **syntactically returnable** for it — so the *state-kind* mismatch this row anticipates for LFM2 exists on `main` today for a different model. **Consequence for sequencing: the vocabulary can be falsified BEFORE this row is worked, against a real consumer, instead of being designed for LFM2 alone and re-litigated when LFM2 lands.** | OPEN |
| GAP-099 | `PrecisionGuarantee` (`fuel-dispatch`, `fuel-mkl-cpu-backend`, `fuel-aocl-cpu-backend`) | D | **Make "audited, no static bound applies" STRUCTURALLY distinct from "never audited", so conflating them is unrepresentable rather than merely guarded.** Today `none(reason)` and `UNAUDITED` are **field-identical**, and a `&'static str` in `notes` is the load-bearing discriminator. GAP-095 shipped a shared-predicate **seam** that contains the blast radius and fixed the consumer disagreement; **this row is the cure the seam stands in for.** **PRICED, so nobody re-derives it: 64 literals across 3 crates** (39 production / 25 test; 31 production in `fused.rs`), and as a **field addition** it requires `--all-targets` per constructing crate — not `--lib` (GAP-143). **RANKED LOW DELIBERATELY, on measurement rather than taste:** GAP-077 measured **zero `none(reason)` CPU entries** in the live table, so this is **preventive maintenance on a state with no current instances**. **And GAP-095 lowered its price rather than duplicating it** — consumers now route through a method, so this becomes a change to the method body rather than to 64 call sites. **Re-rank upward if any `none(reason)` entry ever reaches the binding table**, which is the trigger worth watching. | OPEN — low, and cheaper than it was |

| GAP-095 | `fuel-dispatch/src/fkc/kernel.rs:1207` (`fill_unset_cpu_precision`), called `dispatch.rs:5859` | B | **A CPU audit that explicitly concluded "no static bound applies" is SILENTLY OVERWRITTEN with a `bit_stable_on_same_hardware: true` claim.** `fill_unset_cpu_precision` detects UNAUDITED **structurally** — all four value fields at defaults — and **never reads `notes`**. `PrecisionGuarantee::none(reason)` is **field-identical to UNAUDITED**, so it is caught by the same predicate and replaced with `PRIMITIVE_DETERMINISTIC_CPU`. **Narrowed deliberately, and the narrowing matters:** `FKC-4.8-0001` **sanctions** absent / `audited: false` -> family default, so the fill pass existing is *not* the defect. **It does NOT sanction `audited: true` + `none(reason)` -> default**, and that is the case being silently overwritten. The finder narrowed their own broader claim after reading the clause — the narrowing cut against their earlier position and **strengthened** the no-op prediction they had staked. **Why it is a real defect rather than a quirk: the entire `none(reason)` design (`fused.rs:198-206`) rests on `notes` being the discriminator between "audited, no bound applies" and "never audited", and the coverage lint HONOURS that distinction while this pass does not.** Two mechanisms in the same crate disagree about what distinguishes the two states. **FIXED @ `31f33f3d`** — `PrecisionGuarantee::is_audited_no_bound()` + `makes_no_static_claim()`, **one definition shared by both kinds of consumer**, so the *disagreement* between the fill pass and the coverage lint is what got fixed rather than just this instance of it. Gate: `fuel-dispatch --lib` 735/0, `--test bit_stable_coverage_flip` 2/0, `--all-targets` exit 0. CPU-only. **THE ARCHITECT ASKED FOR THE STRUCTURAL FIX TO BE PRICED FIRST, AND THIS TIME THE PRICE ARGUED THE OTHER WAY** — which is why it was asked. Structural (a provenance field on `PrecisionGuarantee`): **64 literals across 3 crates** (39 production / 25 test; 31 production in `fused.rs` alone; plus `fuel-mkl-cpu-backend` and `fuel-aocl-cpu-backend`), and being a field addition it needs `--all-targets` per constructing crate. **But COST is not what decided it — LIVENESS did.** GAP-077 measured **601/601** contract-derived CPU entries carrying the UNAUDITED sentinel, so **there are ZERO `none(reason)` CPU entries today: the defect is LATENT.** The harness was re-run after the fix and the fill's output is **byte-identical (636/636 bit-stable, 636 owed to the fill)**, so the change is provably behaviour-neutral. A 64-site preventive refactor across three crates, for a state that does not currently occur, is the wrong trade *now*. **⚠️ THE TRAP — AND IT IS THE MOST VALUABLE THING IN THIS ROW: THE CHEAPEST, MOST PRINCIPLED-LOOKING OPTION WAS THE CATASTROPHIC ONE.** A third layer was priced first — put the fact where it is known: `BindingEntry.precision_explicit`, set `true` by `register_with_precision` and `false` by `register`. **Only 8 construction sites — 8x cheaper — and it reads as the principled "store the fact at its source" fix.** **Rejected on CORRECTNESS, not cost:** a contract claim downgraded by the V-FKC-9 ledger gate **did** arrive with an explicit precision, so it would be flagged explicit and **excluded from the fill** — and per GAP-077 **every bit-stable entry in the CPU table arrives exactly that way.** **That fix would have stripped bit-stability from all 601 CPU entries and broken `FKC-4.8-0001` outright.** **Only GAP-077's measurement made that visible — a measurement taken to answer a DIFFERENT question.** Generalisable: *cheapest* and *most principled-looking* can coincide on the option that is **wrong**, and neither property is evidence about correctness. **NOT second-best — a stepping stone that REDUCES the future price.** Consumers now route through a method, so the structural version later changes **the method body, not the call sites**. Stated plainly in the commit as **a seam, not a cure**: a `&'static str` remains the load-bearing discriminator until the states are made structurally distinct. **REMAINING, deliberately not done, and flagged rather than silently skipped:** `tests/vulkan_dispatch_live.rs` **open-codes the same notes-comparison** and should route through the new method. It is a one-line change that **cannot be RUN here** — it needs `--features vulkan` and both other lanes held the GPU. **Changing code whose gate you cannot execute is exactly how "a clean rebase is not evidence of compatibility" happens** (GAP-143 family). **Natural pickup for whichever lane next holds a Vulkan build.** | **FIXED** — one Vulkan-gated pickup remains |
| GAP-096 | `FKC-4.8-0001` (`docs/specs/kernel-contract-format.md:1244`) vs `fill_unset_cpu_precision` | B | **`FKC-4.8-0001` HOLDS, and 100% of what satisfies it is a BULK FILL. Zero contract-declared bit-stable claims survive to the binding table.** Measured under GAP-077 @ `c81fcfab`: **636 CPU entries, 636 bit-stable — 636 owed to `fill_unset_cpu_precision`, 0 from a contract.** **The clause explicitly sanctions the fill, so this is CHARACTERISATION, not an accusation — but the shape deserves a name: `gate_precision` fires correctly on every claim (281 downgrades at import), and the property it was gating is then asserted WHOLESALE by a later pass that never consults it.** V-FKC-9's gate works, and is overwritten ~250 lines later. **The question this row exists to force: is the commitment "at least one CPU kernel per primitive op is bit-stable" meant to be DERIVED (from a contract, backed by a ledger entry) or ASSERTED (by convention, because CPU primitives are believed deterministic)?** Today it is entirely the second. If the intent was the first, the clause is satisfied nominally and the guarantee is hollow — a property declared rather than verified, which is the pattern this registry names *existence != enforcement*. If the intent was the second, the clause should say so plainly, because "the contract MUST declare" currently implies the first. **Related divergence:** the clause's own two sentences cannot both be satisfied by one contract (ruled a DIVERGENCES entry, GAP-036). | OPEN |
| GAP-097 | `fuel-ir/src/dtype.rs` `DType` vs KISS-Classify §6.1; and per-surface depth | A | **DTYPE CONFORMANCE AUDIT, requested by CireSnave 2026-08-08 ("Fuel should comprehensively support all KISS-specified dtypes throughout its code"). Measured against `origin/main`, not the stale shared checkout. RESULT: Fuel covers 11 of KISS's 22, and carries 5 tokens KISS does not recognise — non-conformant in BOTH directions.** **KISS-CLASSIFY-6.1-0001 pins a CLOSED set of exactly 22 tokens** and states an implementation **MUST NOT recognise a twenty-third and MUST NOT omit any**. Fuel declares **16**. **COVERED (11):** `f16`->F16, `bf16`->BF16, `f32`->F32, `f64`->F64, `s8`->I8, `s16`->I16, `u8`->U8, `i32`->I32, `i64`->I64, `u32`->U32, `e4m3fn`->F8E4M3. (Spellings differ; KISS-CLASSIFY-6.1-0004 requires its exact lowercase tokens **in a `structure_key` / operand descriptor**, which Fuel's deriver already emits correctly — the Rust variant names are not the wire form and are not a violation.) **MISSING (11), ranked by consequence rather than listed flat:** **(1) `e5m2` — ACTIVE, not reserved, and the sharpest gap.** Standard FP8 practice pairs **E4M3 (forward) with E5M2 (gradients/wider range)**; **Fuel has one half of the standard FP8 pair.** Positive-controlled: `DType::F8E4M3` appears in 32 files, `DType::F8E5M2`/`E5M2` in **0** — the two `E5M2` strings in `dtype.rs` are prose inside an error message. **(2) `c32` / `c64` — Fuel has NO complex dtype at all**, while **KISS-Ops §6.18 defines an entire complex-arithmetic op family**. This is a whole capability, not a token. **(3) `e4m3fnuz` / `e5m2fnuz` — RESERVED, and reserved is NOT the same as ignorable.** KISS-CLASSIFY-6.1-0001 requires a reader **recognise them and distinguish them from an unknown token**, answering with a **typed decline distinct from the unknown-token decline** (`reserved_fnuz_dtypes_typed_decline`). **Fuel cannot produce that distinction for a token it does not know** — so this is a conformance gap even though the dtypes have no compute semantics. **(4) `u16`, `u64`** — ordinary integer storage. **(5) `bool`** — a distinct KISS token even though its storage width equals `u8`. **(6) `s4`, `u4`, `b1`** — sub-byte int / bit, with pinned packing rules. **EXTRA (5), which KISS-CLASSIFY-6.1-0001 forbids recognising:** `F6E2M3`, `F6E3M2`, `F4`, `F8E8M0`, `F8E6M2` (MX / block-scale). **⚠️ THAT CLAIM WAS SECOND-HAND (read from issue TITLES) AND IS NOW CORRECTED BY READING KISS#32 ITSELF — the path covers FOUR of the five, and reveals TWO of Fuel's variants are ill-formed by KISS's own reasoning.** **Covered by KISS#32** (which explicitly scopes *"all the sub-byte / block-scaled FLOAT formats together"*): `F6E2M3` -> `e2m3`, `F6E3M2` -> `e3m2`, `F4` -> `e2m1`, plus the MX wrappers (MXFP8/MXFP6/MXFP4/NVFP4) — so `F8E8M0`, the MX block **scale**, is very likely covered as part of the block format, **though as a scale rather than an element format; unconfirmed, do not cite as settled.** **NOT covered: `F8E6M2`.** #32 enumerates `e2m1`/`e2m3`/`e3m2` plus the already-pinned `e4m3`/`e5m2`; **`F8E6M2` is absent because it is not a standard format at all** — Fuel's own `dtype.rs` calls it *"a non-OCP-standard token"*. **That is why it typed-declines** (GAP-045/GAP-059): its encoding is *unauthored*, so there is nothing to upstream. **AND THE FINDING THAT CHANGES THE PLAN: KISS#32 records that a BARE 6-bit float CANNOT BE A STORAGE DTYPE.** *"6 bits don't tile a byte"* — every other sub-byte KISS dtype has a clean convention (`s4`/`u4` nibble-pair, `b1` 8/byte) and 6-bit has none; `e2m3`/`e3m2` exist in practice **only inside MXFP6 blocks, where the block defines the packing**. Since §6.1's rule is *a dtype pins byte layout only*, **a bare 6-bit token has no byte layout to pin.** KISS tried the bare-storage-dtype approach in **PRs #33/#34 and CLOSED them** after a verify pass caught exactly this. **So Fuel's `F6E2M3`/`F6E3M2` are bare 6-bit storage dtypes in a form KISS has ALREADY DECIDED cannot exist.** The direction for those two is **not** *upstream them* but **restructure them as block-scoped** — materially larger than a token addition, and it belongs with whatever Fuel does about MX block formats generally. **`F4` is unaffected** (4 bits nibble-pair like `s4`/`u4`). **⚠️ SUPERSEDED SAME DAY BY THE sk4 SCOPE — a THIRD layer on this claim, and the direction reversed again.** KISS announced (2026-08-08) that the **sk4 spec target now in authoring** includes **MX additive dtypes: `f6e2m3`/`f6e3m2`/`f4` as ELEMENTS and `f8e8m0`/`f8e6m2` as SCALES**, OCP-MX cited. **So `f8e6m2` gets exactly the home it lacked — as a SCALE, which is precisely what Fuel's own `dtype.rs` says it is** (*"a finer-granularity sibling of F8E8M0 … so a scale type, not a signed value dtype"*). **All five of Fuel's non-conformant extras therefore have a path under sk4.** **RULED BY ERIC 2026-08-08: `f6`/`f4` are BLOCK-SCOPED ELEMENT FORMATS, not bare §6.1 dtypes. The architect's read is CONFIRMED — Fuel's bare `F6E2M3`/`F6E3M2` must RESTRUCTURE, not rename (carved to GAP-153, materially larger).** `f8e6m2` lands in §6.1 **as a scale**. MX is **vocab-binding now, kernels fast-follow**. **SUPERSEDED — this was the open question:** are `f6e2m3`/`f6e3m2` landing as **bare** storage dtypes or **only block-scoped**? KISS#32 argued at length that a bare 6-bit float cannot be a §6.1 storage dtype and closed PRs #33/#34 over it. If sk4 adds them bare, something changed; if block-scoped, Fuel's current **bare** `F6E2M3`/`F6E3M2` need **restructuring, not renaming** — materially larger. **Asked of KISS; do not plan either way until answered.** **sk4 ALSO CHANGES SPELLINGS FUEL MUST TARGET:** `f8`-prefix with a **mandatory variant suffix only where >1 variant exists** (`f8e4m3fn` suffixed, **`f8e5m2` NOT**), and a **complex meaning-flip `c32`/`c64` -> `c64`/`c128`** (naming by TOTAL bits). **Fuel has no complex dtype at all, so the flip costs nothing today — which is exactly why it must be recorded before complex is implemented, or the OLD names get built in six months.** **Method note: this correction cost one `gh issue view`, and the original claim came from issue TITLES** — the same class as reading a count instead of an attribution: the title named the right subject while the body said something materially different about the shape. **DEPTH — the second half of the question, and coverage is steeply uneven even within Fuel's own 16.** CPU kernel dtype suffixes in `fuel-cpu-backend/src/byte_kernels.rs`: `f32` **1174**, `f64` 270, `bf16` 152, `f16` 139, `u8` 51, `i8` 31, `i64` 26, `u32` 20, `f8e4m3` 17, `i32` 15, `i16` 15 — and **ZERO for all five MX/block-scale formats** (`f6e2m3`, `f6e3m2`, `f4`, `f8e8m0`, `f8e6m2`). So **5 of Fuel's 16 declared dtypes have no CPU kernel at all**, and the integer tail is thin. **⚠️ INSTRUMENT NOTE, recorded because the first measurement was wrong:** grepping `DType::` returned **0** for `byte_kernels.rs` — not because it lacks dtype support but because that file names dtypes in **function suffixes**, not `DType::` matches. **A per-surface audit needs a per-surface instrument**; a single pattern across dissimilar files produces false zeros (GAP-149). **THE SCOPING METHOD IS ALREADY BUILT, AND IT IS TONIGHT'S TWO BUILD BREAKS TURNED INTO A TOOL.** `DType` is now **EXHAUSTIVE-BY-DESIGN** (GAP-049), so **adding a variant makes the compiler enumerate every site that must classify it.** Do not hand-scope this by grepping — **add the variant, read the `E0004` list, classify each arm.** That is precisely what happened to `Scalar::F8E8M0` (GAP-049) and `DType::F8E6M2` (GAP-059), except those were discovered as *breaks* rather than used as *enumeration*. **The same mechanism that cost two red builds today is the one that makes this program mechanical.** **⚠️ The enumeration MUST run workspace-scope AND all features**, per the rule GAP-049(3) put into `CLAUDE.md`. Both of today's breaks hid behind a non-default feature (`--features cuda`, `--features dlpack`); a default-only `E0004` list will under-report exactly the arms that bit us. **Blast-radius proxy for the first item:** `DType::F8E4M3` — `e5m2`'s sibling FP8 — appears in **18 files** (fuel-ir 4, fuel-dispatch 9, fuel-cpu-backend 3, fuel-core 2, fuel-graph 0, fuel-memory 0). **Small, which supports taking `e5m2` first.** Treat that as an *estimate of shape, not a work list* — the compiler's list is authoritative and the two differ (mentions include tests and comments; exhaustive arms are the real set). **WAVES 1-3 APPLIED (2026-08-08) — 13 sites across 3 crates, FAR smaller than the 46-file proxy, and every decision collapsed to "decline or forward" exactly as the scope ruling predicted.** Wave 1 `fuel-ir` **8 sites** (`as_str`, `FromStr`, `size_in_bytes`, `is_int`/`is_float`, safetensors both directions, plus `Scalar::F8E5M2` with `zero`/`one`/`from_f64`/`dtype()`/`to_f64()`); wave 2 `fuel-correctness-fixtures` **1 site** (pure forward); wave 3 `fuel-cpu-backend` **4 sites, all DECLINING**. **A THIRD per-dtype enum exists: `HostBuffer`/`HostBufferRef`** (after `DType` and `Scalar`). Under the scope ruling E5M2 joins `UnsupportedDTypeForOp` there rather than expanding a third enum. **THE TWO DECLINE KINDS ARE MARKED DISTINCTLY AT EACH SITE, per the architect's instruction:** every E5M2 decline says it is a **DELIBERATE UNDER-SHIPMENT** — OCP-standard, `float8::F8E5M2` exists, real DLPack representation, declining only for want of a `HostBuffer` variant — **explicitly contrasted with `F8E6M2` beside it, which declines because its encoding is UNAUTHORED.** A reader in six months gets the difference at the site rather than from this row. **⚠️ A THIRD HOLE, AND IT IS A DIFFERENT KIND: STRUCTURALLY INVISIBLE. `telemetry` is a NON-DEFAULT feature of `fuel-dispatch`, so `telemetry/structure_key_derive.rs` — an exhaustive `DType` match, and the sk4 wire codec — was NEVER COMPILED by any e5m2 verification leg.** It was a **20th** `E0004` site. CUDA is *unrun*; Metal is *unrunnable*; **this was a leg nobody could see existed.** `--all-targets` does not imply `--all-features`. **Corrected e5m2 status: 20 sites / 7 crates**, with `--features telemetry --lib` 798/0 now green. **Caught only because a test filter printed `running 0 tests; 737 filtered out` and the worker refused to read it as a pass** — otherwise sk4 code that had never been compiled would have shipped behind a green-looking run. **GAP-149's shape, caught by its own discipline.** **FEATURE SPACE ENUMERATED (architect, so a sixth is not discovered by accident):** `fuel-dispatch` telemetry/jit/jit-synth/cuda/vulkan/metal; `fuel-ir` dlpack/ug/serde; `fuel-memory` cuda/vulkan/metal/dlpack; `fuel-cpu-backend` mkl/accelerate; `fuel-core` telemetry/cuda/cudnn/nccl/mkl/aocl/onemkl/accelerate/metal/vulkan/ug; `fuel-correctness-fixtures` serde/capture. **`mkl`/`accelerate`/`aocl`/`onemkl` are BOTH invisible AND unbuildable here (GAP-016) — a fourth category.** **Negative result kept: `serde` is NOT a hidden site — `dtype.rs:27` uses a `derive`, which absorbs a new variant silently; that is a wire-compatibility concern, not an enumeration gap.** **TWO TRAPS FROM THE REGEN, both recorded in its commit.** **(1) `revhash.rs` holds a frozen hash pin whose INPUT STRING is literally `"git:sk3-pin"`.** A global `sk3`->`sk4` replace rewrites the *input*, moves the hash, and **the natural fix — updating the frozen constant — destroys the guard that exists to prove the byte contract did NOT move during a regen.** The most dangerous edit in the whole change, and **it looks like a typo fix.** **(2) Retired-spelling guards need DELIMITERS, not substrings:** `f8e4m3fn` **contains** `e4m3fn`, so a bare `!contains("e4m3fn")` rejects the *correct* sk4 token. Guards assert `/e4m3fn/` and `\|e4m3fn\|`. **And a self-inflicted one, reported unprompted:** a blanket `sk3\|`->`sk4\|` replace corrupted a *historical* line describing the **sk2->sk3** delta into `sk2\| -> sk4\|`. Caught on read-back. **A global replace is exactly as unsafe in prose as in code.** **⚠️ METAL IS A KNOWN BLIND SPOT AND MUST BE STATED WITH ANY COMPLETION CLAIM.** `fuel-metal-backend/src/storage.rs` matches on `F8E4M3`, so this change almost certainly breaks a Metal site **that cannot be seen or gated on this machine** — it needs a Mac or `--target aarch64-apple-darwin`. See GAP-149(5): `--workspace` aborts on `objc2` here and the abort reports `E0004: 0`. **⚠️ THE "NON-CONFORMANT IN BOTH DIRECTIONS" HEADLINE IS TOO STRONG — corrected 2026-08-08 while pre-scoping the sk4 regen. AT THE WIRE LEVEL FUEL IS ALREADY CONFORMANT.** `structure_key_derive.rs:317` `dtype_token()` emits **only §6.1 tokens** (`f16`, `bf16`, `f32`, `f64`, `s8`, `s16`, `u8`, `u32`, `i32`, `i64`, `e4m3fn`) and **`return None` for all five MX/extra formats**, with a comment citing the pending RFC. **So the decline-don't-fabricate principle is already applied on the surface KISS actually governs.** KISS-CLASSIFY-6.1-0001 forbids *recognising a twenty-third **token***; a private Rust enum variant is not a token, and Fuel neither emits nor accepts one. **The non-conformance is in the INTERNAL enum, which KISS does not govern — the OMISSIONS (11 of 22 missing) remain real and are the substance of this row.** **sk4 REGEN PRE-SCOPED so the lane starts cold-free (PR-1 delta, `structure_key_derive.rs`):** **(1)** `e4m3fn` -> **`f8e4m3fn`** (f8-prefix; the `fn` suffix is already there) — one line; **(2)** add `DType::F8E5M2 => "f8e5m2"` **unsuffixed** — one line, gated on GAP-097 increment 1 landing the variant; **(3)** `sk3\|` -> `sk4\|` in the format string; **(4)** SCHEMA_VERSION 3 -> 4; **(5)** the **(acc+mp) trailing field** for non-contraction cells — **the only substantive piece, and it is already four-party-assented (GAP-148) with a precedent in the SAME FILE** (the contraction field's optional-trailing-slot shape). **Complex is a no-op for Fuel** (no variants exist, so the flip cannot be emitted wrongly). **CORRECTED against PR #131 itself: `f8e8m0` and `f8e6m2` are NOW §6.1 TOKENS (new at sk4, additive) — so Fuel must EMIT them, not decline them.** The architect's pre-scoping said *"MX stays declining"*; that was right for the **elements** and wrong for the **scales**. **Only `f6e2m3`/`f6e3m2`/`f4` keep declining** (block-scoped, PR-2, GAP-153). **⚠️ FIFTH DRIFT, AND THE ONLY ONE IN FUEL'S OWN EMISSION PATH — the architect's delta MISSED IT.** sk4 renames the signed integers (`i8` <- sk3 `s8`, `i16` <- sk3 `s16`, `i4` <- sk3 `s4`), and **Fuel emits `s8`/`s16` today** (`structure_key_derive.rs:323-324`). Implementing the delta as written would ship **`sk4\|bin\|s16\|…`** — an sk4-prefixed token carrying a retired sk3 spelling, which **§6.1-0004 forbids outright** (*"MUST NOT substitute a synonym"*). **ROOT CAUSE IS A RELAYED CHARACTERISATION THE ARCHITECT DID NOT CHECK.** KISS said *"you're already i-prefixed"*; that is true of `i32`/`i64` and **false of the signed bytes**. It went into the delta unverified. **AND IT WOULD HAVE SHIPPED SILENTLY, for the same reason as the `c64` defect in the spec: it rides through under cover of the part that gets scrutinised.** All attention is on the FP8 work; those tests get rewritten and go green and the change looks done. **Nobody re-reads the integer arms because nothing in the delta pointed at them** — and `s16` is not *obviously* wrong the way `e4m3fn` is once you know about the `f8` prefix. (The architect's own `i4`-not-`s4` correction was this same rename, surfacing only because `i4` happened to be in a list being checked.) **Small to fix, expensive to miss:** `I8 => "i8"`, `I16 => "i16"`, plus one golden literal — the only `s8`/`s16` token in the tree — which updates with the `sk3\|`->`sk4\|` sweep. **In scope for the regen as conformance, not scope expansion.** **FINAL PR-1 DELTA for `dtype_token()`:** change `e4m3fn` -> **`f8e4m3fn`**; add **`f8e5m2`** (unsuffixed — only `fnuz` deviates), **`f8e8m0`**, **`f8e6m2`**; **narrow the `return None` arm from five dtypes to three**; `sk3\|` -> `sk4\|`; SCHEMA_VERSION 3 -> 4 + manifest v4; then the **(acc+mp)** field. **⚠️ §6.1 IS NOW 24 DTYPES, NOT 22 — SO THIS ROW'S HEADLINE COUNT IS STALE AND FUEL'S POSITION IMPROVES.** The 22 plus the two MX **scales**. Fuel already has both, so **Fuel covers 13 of 24**, and **two of the five "forbidden extras" become legitimate**. Remaining true omissions: `u16`, `u64`, `bool`, `f8e4m3fnuz`, `f8e5m2fnuz`, `i4`, `u4`, `b1`, `c64`, `c128` — and `f8e5m2`, which is being added now. **ALSO NOTE THE INTEGER SPELLING: sk4 uses `i4` (PR text: "packed-pair storage identical to `i4`"), not `s4`.** This row listed `s4`; use the PR's spelling. **AND THE COMPLEX FLIP IS A LOUD REINTERPRETATION, BY DESIGN:** `c64` at sk4 = pair-of-`f32` (**the sk3 `c32`**), `c128` = pair-of-`f64` (the sk3 `c64`). **The same token means different things across the version boundary**, and PR #131 states the version prefix is what makes that reinterpretation *loud, never silent*. **A no-op for Fuel today — which is exactly why it must be read before Fuel implements complex, or the sk3 meanings get built.** **⚠️ SPEC DEFECT FOUND IN KISS PR #131 AND RAISED BEFORE RATIFICATION (2026-08-08): §6.1-0002 and §6.1-0003 WERE NOT UPDATED FOR sk4.** The table (§6.1-0001) is respelled to 24 dtypes; the **bit-width** and **numeric-kind** clauses are still sk3. Four drifts and an omission: **(1)** FP8 unprefixed (`e4m3fn`/`e5m2` — the very spellings the PR retires); **(2)** `s8`/`s16`/`s4` where the table now says `i8`/`i16`/`i4`; **(3)** complex still sk3 (`c32`=64, `c64`=128); **(4)** **`f8e8m0` and `f8e6m2` are ABSENT from both clauses** — the two dtypes the PR *adds* have no stated bit width and no stated numeric kind. **THE SHARPEST FORM, and the reason to fix (3) first: §6.1-0002 states the sk3 meaning of `c64` INSIDE the document whose headline change is that `c64` means something else.** The PR's own argument for the flip is that **the version prefix makes the reinterpretation loud, never silent** — so **a clause carrying the old meaning under the new version prefix is the one place it goes silent**, and it is inside the spec that promises otherwise. **Why it is more than tidiness: 0002 and 0003 each NAME their own conformance test** (`test_classify_dtype_bit_widths`, `test_classify_dtype_numeric_kinds`). **Tests written against these clauses would assert sk3 spellings and would not cover the two new dtypes at all** — the suite goes green while the table it backs has moved. Exactly the drift KISS-Conform's derived-not-hand-authored discipline exists to prevent. **PROVENANCE CHAIN, kept because it is why the finding was usable.** The worker read `origin/feat/sk4-spec:spec/classify.md` and **flagged that a branch is not the PR head** — five sk4 branches exist and they could not tell which is #131. The architect then verified the load-bearing half directly: **`gh pr diff 131`, positive-controlled — 28,124 bytes, `6.1-0001` appears 10 times, `6.1-0002` and `6.1-0003` appear ZERO times.** **The PR does not touch either clause**, so what they said before is what ships, which makes the branch read valid for the PR's shipping text. **Flagging the corpus actually read is what let the gap be closed with one command** — this morning's stale-tree lesson applied *before* it cost anything. **THE DEFECT IS ~2x WHAT EITHER SIDE SCOPED, and the second half outranks the first.** The KISS architect swept all of `spec/`: retired sk3 spellings number **58 in classify.md and 52 in ops.md**, plus emit.md 3, grammar.md 1, umbrella.md 1 — **and PR #131 touches only four files, `spec/ops.md` NOT among them.** **Why ops.md outranks classify.md: §6.16 is titled "Self-contained dtype bit layouts" and states its purpose as letting a foreign reader holding ONLY that document evaluate every float clause.** So the respell violates §6.16's own guarantee, and **the exposed party is the genuinely-external implementor that umbrella §5.3 makes a FREEZE PRECONDITION** — someone who implements sk3 spellings and sk3 complex meanings **self-consistently** and diverges from all four byte-match parties **with nothing to warn them.** Internal parties have a byte-match to catch drift; that reader has none. It also carries the `c32`/`c64` family through five more normative sites — the same meaning-flip hazard, in a second file. **ROOT CAUSE — and it is THIS REGISTRY'S OWN PATTERN, hit for the FIFTH time today, in another project's spec rather than Fuel's code: THREE dtype tables exist (`dtype_manifest.json` as SSOT, classify.md §6.1, ops.md §6.16) AND THE LINT VALIDATES ONE.** Today's other four: GAP-141's gate enforcing 14 patterns while its rule named 5; GAP-058's 794 `audited: true` claims with no ledger row; GAP-076's fused destructiveness hardcoded in a match the registry didn't feed; GAP-152's doc comment asserting a wiring that never existed. **A fact stored in N places with one of them checked.** **FUEL'S CONTRIBUTION BACK, offered as a suggestion: linting all three is the VIGILANCE form; GENERATING the two spec tables FROM the manifest is the SEAM form** — drift becomes unrepresentable rather than detected. **And KISS already holds that principle: KISS-Conform §6.1-0004 requires the traceability matrix be DERIVED, NOT HAND-AUTHORED, explicitly because a hand-maintained one "can silently diverge" — which is exactly what happened, to KISS's own dtype tables, in triplicate.** Caveat sent with it: if only the tables generate and the surrounding prose stays hand-written, that closes the part that broke and leaves the part that did not — probably the right trade, but a real limit rather than a clean win. **FUEL IS NOT BLOCKED:** §6.1-0004 makes the table governing for emission (*"spelled by exactly its stable lowercase token in the table above"*), so the regen proceeds against §6.1-0001/0004. **⚠️ THE REGEN'S REAL TEST IS BEHAVIOURAL, NOT SPELLING — and a spelling-only test would pass while missing it.** `dtype_token(first.dtype)?` **aborts the WHOLE derivation**, so today **any cell whose FIRST operand is an MX/scale dtype emits no structure key at all.** Narrowing the decline set does not add a token to an existing field — **it turns previously-SILENT cells into EMITTING ones**, and telemetry rows that never existed start existing. **Assert that a previously-declining cell now produces a well-formed key.** **WHY SCALES ARE SAFE TO EMIT — the worker's answer, better than the architect's hypothesis.** The architect guessed "scales get a positional slot". The real reason is stronger: **only `operand[0]` and the gem `weight`/`acc`/`out` triple are dtype-bearing at all** — every other operand contributes layout only (`co/00/v4/d16/f`). So **a scale riding as a sibling operand can never reach a dtype coordinate, by construction rather than by convention** — an answer that stays true when someone adds an operand. **SEQUENCING (architect):** this is a program, not a row-sized fix. Do **not** open it as one task. The defensible order is `e5m2` first (active, half a standard pair, small), then the two **reserved** tokens (pure parse+decline, no compute, closes a conformance obligation cheaply), then `u16`/`u64`/`bool`, then sub-byte, and **`c32`/`c64` last and separately** because it is a capability with an op family behind it. **The 5 extras are an UPSTREAM action (KISS #9/#32), not a Fuel deletion.** **⚠️ FOURTH HOLE FOUND 2026-08-12 (lane A) — THE e5m2 SWEEP MISSED A 21st SITE, AND THIS ONE WAS INVISIBLE TO A STRICTLY STRONGER GATE THAN THE ONE THAT CAUGHT THE 20th.** `fuel-dispatch/src/telemetry/baracuda_provider.rs:116` `map_element_kind` is a wildcard-free exhaustive `match` covering **16 of `DType`'s 17** variants; `F8E5M2` was absent. **Confirmed by the compiler, not by reading** — the derived-by-construction argument (17 vs 16, no `_` arm) was treated as a HYPOTHESIS and put to the oracle: `error[E0004]: non-exhaustive patterns: fuel_ir::DType::F8E5M2 not covered` at `:117:16`, exit 101, with the positive artifacts required before believing an exit code of **either** polarity (`[vcvarsall.bat] Environment initialized for: 'x64'` plus `Checking fuel-dispatch`). **MECHANISM — a DOUBLE GATE, and that is the generalisable part, not the missing arm.** `mod telemetry` is `#[cfg(feature = "telemetry")]`; `mod baracuda_provider` is `#[cfg(feature = "cuda")]` **inside it**. The file is therefore parsed ONLY under the **combination** `--features telemetry,cuda`. **The 20th site above was caught by `--features telemetry`; that same gate cannot see this one.** Neither can the default build, nor `--all-targets`, nor the aarch64 gate. `--all-targets` is not `--all-features`, and **one feature is not two**. GAP-160/162's lesson in a third place: **code nothing compiles is code nothing checks.** **POPULATION — a LOWER BOUND, and labelled as one.** A syntactic scan for `#[cfg(feature=…)]` immediately preceding a `mod` finds exactly **one** gated-module-inside-a-gated-module in the workspace: this one. A second and larger class exists — **optional-dependency crates carrying their own inner feature gates** (`fuel-cuda-backend`'s `mod ug` needs `cuda`+`ug`; `fuel-ug`'s inline `cuda`/`metal` modules). The scan is a lower bound **by construction**: it sees gated *modules*, not `#[cfg(feature=…)]` on individual items or impls inside ungated modules — equally invisible, and where most exhaustive matches actually live. **Enumerating the gated modules is the answerable form of "what else can't we see"; grepping for dtypes is not.** **FIXED** as `DType::F8E5M2 => ElementKind::Fp8E5M2` — a real mapping, deliberately **NOT** another decline: Baracuda ships the matching kind with its own FP8 tensor-core operand tag (`.e5m2.e5m2.f32`) at the locked `baracuda-kernel-vocab 0.0.1-alpha.78`, and `dtype_token` already emits `f8e5m2`, so declining would have silently suppressed structure-key derivation for a **supported** FP8 family. Re-verified green under the same double gate (exit 0, both artifacts). **SEPARATE FINDING, FILED NOT FIXED — a decline whose stated reason has EXPIRED, on a live site (the GAP-161 shape):** `DType::U32` sits in that same arm under the comment "No faithful Baracuda ElementKind", but **`ElementKind::U32` EXISTS at alpha.78**. Left deliberately: a decline there aborts the WHOLE derivation via `?` at the call site, so un-declining it changes **behaviour**, not a message — previously-silent cells would start emitting keys — and that needs its own test and its own decision. | OPEN |
