| GAP-141 | A | CI check: fail on a non-test path lacking a `GAP(...)` ref. The enforcement that keeps this registry honest. **SCOPED + RATIFIED 2026-08-07.** *Measured magnitude:* `todo!()`/`unimplemented!()` = **36 sites / 7 files** (with `fuel-backend-contract/storage.rs` alone holding 30 — likely one trait stub); `panic!(` adds **~612 more** (648 total). A whole-tree day-one gate is therefore a non-starter — it would red-gate hundreds of legitimate invariant panics against zero annotated backlog. *Seam ratified:* a Rust `#[test]` that scans the source tree, **not** a new YAML job — the existing `test` job already runs cross-platform and the positive control becomes ordinary test data. **Increment 1 (BUILD THIS): `todo!()`/`unimplemented!()` on non-test paths must carry `// GAP(GAP-NNN)`.** 36 sites, annotate-or-file first, then a clean green gate. **Increment 2 (SCHEDULED, not "deferred"): prose hedges, via a baseline allowlist that may only shrink.** **⚠️ THE BILLING CORRECTION THAT MUST NOT BE LOST: increment 1 would NOT have caught GAP-001.** The scoping proposal described `todo!`/`unimplemented!` as "exactly the class that caused GAP-001". It is not. GAP-001's origin site (`fuel-dispatch/src/jit_cuda_load.rs`, module doc comment ~:51) contains **no `todo!`, no `unimplemented!`, no `panic!`** — it is a **prose hedge** inside a `//!` block: *"A robust version would read the FKC contract's schedule/`count_unit:` metadata instead of sniffing the symbol suffix; sniffing is today's honest shortcut (documented, not hidden)."* The founding case of this entire registry falls in **increment 2**. So: the uncomfortable truth is that the hardest class to detect (fuzzy, false-positive-prone) is also the one that matters most, and increment 1 is real value that must not be **billed** as closing the GAP-001 class. Increment 2 is scheduled precisely so "we did the easy one" cannot quietly become the finish line. **Positive control REQUIRED in the check's own tests** (architect constraint, accepted): a fixture with a known un-referenced `todo!()` the check MUST flag, **and** one carrying a ref it must pass. A lint that silently matches nothing reads exactly like a clean codebase — without this, the enforcer inherits the very false-green defect it exists to prevent. This is GAP-144's shape applied to the enforcer itself. | **INC-1 DONE @ `999f5b67`** (`fuel-ir/tests/gap_refs.rs`, runs under the existing `cargo test --workspace`, no new YAML job; 6/6 green; positive control observed RED as `flags_unreferenced_todo` against a stub — **and the other 4 assertions passed VACUOUSLY until sabotaged**, which is exactly why both directions were required; tally prints `36 occ -> 0 untracked, 1 tracked GAP-125, 30 #[cfg(test)], 3 doc-comment, 2 examples/`; module doc bills it as a prospective ratchet that caught nothing historical, and documents the `examples/` judgment call). **INC-2 DONE @ `5f878092`** (`gap_hedge_allowlist.txt` + shrink-only ratchet; 8/8 green incl. stale-key detection, verified post-rebase; baseline 648 files / 221 hedges / 216-key allowlist / 0 new / 0 stale). **Per-phrase tally at adoption** (authoritative, emitted by the check): `not yet` 69, `TODO` 36, `naive` 25, `would need` 24, `for now` 21, `shortcut` 17, `bails` 15, `temporary` 5, `doesn't yet` 4, `workaround` 4, `stopgap` 1, `FIXME`/`XXX`/`HACK` 0. **Both increments now closed; inc-2 covers GAP-001's founding class.** *Recorded because it will be re-litigated:* the architect's pre-build estimate put `XXX` at 7 and `HACK` at 2; the check finds **0**, because the estimate ran uppercase markers through a case-INSENSITIVE grep and matched lowercase `xxx` in fixtures and the word "hack" in prose. The markers stay in the set (zero cost, pre-empt future ones) but **not for the reason originally given** — a measurement inherits its instrument's flaws. **SCANNER LEAK FIXED @ `1b98c3bf` — a correctness regression in these gates, caught and closed.** The shared exclusion logic missed two kinds of test code, so **test code was being counted as production** in *both* gates: a bare `#[test] fn` body in a non-`cfg(test)` module, and a file pulled in by a parent's `#[cfg(test)] mod X;` (cross-file — the file itself carries no `cfg(test)`). TDD both-directions: the two new exclusion tests failed against the unfixed logic, and **the controls passed in BOTH phases**, so the fix is proven non-vacuous. **Allowlist 216 → 215, and the reason matters more than the number: the SCANNER GOT MORE ACCURATE; the codebase did not improve.** The removed key was `fuel-memory/src/dlpack_view/tests.rs`, a parent-`cfg(test)` file miscounted as production. Stated in the commit body, because an allowlist that shrinks from a measurement correction is **not the ratchet moving** — conflating the two would make the metric lie in the flattering direction, which is the failure mode worth more vigilance than the other. **Remaining under GAP-141: `panic!(` — measured, NOT enforced, pending the gap-vs-invariant policy (GAP-016).** Design constraint: a **baseline allowlist that may only SHRINK** (snapshot today, fail on anything new, allowlist grows only by deliberate edit) so the allowlist file size *is* the ratchet metric; plus the same both-directions positive control. Expect a large initial allowlist — that is not failure. |
| GAP-142 | — | **Process rule, not a defect: a resolution inherits the scope of its FIX, not the scope of its TITLE.** Recorded because it produced two near-misses in one day. (1) The memory `quantized-llama-no-kv-decode` is marked RESOLVED; the fix was the `DecodeModel` trait *tiering* plus Llama3's persistent decode and **never reached a quantized family** — relaying "resolved" to the Lightbulb owner would have let a GGUF spec proceed on a false premise (see GAP-029). (2) The architect-bootstrap note stated a CLAUDE.md registry-pointer line "now lives in CLAUDE.md" when it existed only uncommitted in another worker's tree. The Lightbulb owner independently reported three instances of the same shape from their own side the same day (a `Completed`-means-EOS premise, a "no streaming client exists" justification, and a spec-coverage claim naming four defect sites while fixing three). **Countermeasure:** when a status reads as settled context — RESOLVED, SHIPPED, "already have", "now lives" — check it against the code before building on it or relaying it; and when closing a gap, write what the fix *covers*, not what the gap was *called*. This is the registry-side twin of "existence ≠ enforcement" (`fkc-contract-verification-gap`) and of "an audit's age is decay, not warranty". | STANDING |
| GAP-144 | — | **Process rule: an assertion that cannot fail against the defect it targets is not a test.** Two independent instances, one per project. **Lightbulb (2026-08-07):** a test asserted `prompt_tokens > 0` against a bug that computed a **whitespace word count** instead of a token count — the prompt's word count was 6, its true token count 9, so the assertion **passed against the very defect it was written to catch**, and had done since the task shipped. **Fuel (recorded earlier):** sampled-token equality is a vacuous oracle for KV-dependent behavior on `tiny_model` — greedy decoding is a fixed point and temperature swallows ~1e-3 drift, so the oracle passes whether or not the KV is right; the fix was to assert on **logits**. Both share a shape: the assertion is *true* and *about the right quantity* and still carries no information, because its threshold sits far from where the defect moves the value. **Countermeasure — the negative control is not optional:** before trusting a green test, show it goes **red** against a deliberately broken input, and confirm the recompile actually happened (a passing sabotage run with a stale binary proves nothing). This is the registry-side statement of the `sabotage-test-calibration` and `gate-must-match-change-kind` memories, filed here because both instances above were caught **downstream** of where they were cheap to catch. Pairs with GAP-142 (inherited claims) and GAP-143 (inherited rules); this one is inherited **confidence**. | STANDING |
| GAP-145 | Process — how anyone verifies a claim about what a commit CONTAINS | P | **Two instruments for "is X true on main" both failed inside one 10-minute investigation on 2026-08-07, and the second is the one the project's own memory PRESCRIBES.** Context: `bzfevnl6` reported main red (GAP-049). The architect tried to confirm it cheaply, without contending on the build lock, and reached a confident wrong conclusion twice. **Failure 1 — the shared checkout is a silent stale-read hazard.** `C:\Projects\fuel` was sitting at `efa71c39`, which *predates* the offending commit, so `Scalar` had no `F8E8M0` variant and `scalar_to_bytes` really was exhaustive **in that tree**. The architect read it and reported "consistent with your report in every particular" — **a confirmation derived from evidence incapable of producing a wrong answer.** It looks authoritative (right repo, right paths, real code) and **nothing on screen signals staleness**; the drift was 60+ commits. The control that would have caught it instantly: *does the symbol appear in this tree AT ALL?* **Failure 2 — `git show origin/main:<path>` is NON-DETERMINISTIC under concurrency, and memory prescribes it as the fix for failure 1.** Switching to it showed both arms already present, briefly implying the reporter had misdiagnosed. Cause: the peer's fix landed **between two reads**, moving `origin/main` from `dccad436` to `7b3abafd`. **This is a false NEGATIVE produced by the prescribed remedy under entirely normal conditions — more dangerous than the trap it replaces**, because it discredits a correct report. **THE RULE THAT SURVIVES BOTH: pin an explicit SHA and state it.** `git show <sha>:<path>` — never `origin/main:<path>` — whenever the claim is about *a specific commit's content*, and cite the SHA in the finding so a later reader can re-run the identical query. `origin/main:` remains fine for "what is the state right now"; it is **invalid** for "was X true at commit Y". **REFINEMENT, same day, from `bzfevnl6` getting bitten by the other side of it: a SHA is only stable once PUSHED AND REACHABLE.** They cited the GAP-015 fix as `ca53d093`, which the mandatory re-fetch-before-push **rebase orphaned**; the surviving commit is `c95d140a`. So "pin an explicit SHA" is right but **incomplete** — a hash read from `git log` on a local branch is **provisional**, and rebasing silently invalidates every citation already written into prose. **The failure is silent in the worst way: nothing ever re-checks a hash embedded in a document.** Verify with `git merge-base --is-ancestor <sha> origin/main` before citing, and re-verify after any rebase. Confirmed here in both directions — `c95d140a` reachable, `ca53d093` not. **Memory amended** (`ragged-batched-paged-decode.md`), since leaving it would keep prescribing a method now demonstrably capable of a wrong answer. Sharpened by `bzfevnl6`, who also correctly argued against filing GAP-049's lesson as a third instance of "gate must match the change kind": the correct *instrument* differs per change kind (`--all-targets` on constructing crates vs every consumer crate under every feature), so collapsing them yields "run more builds", which is unactionable. **Identify the blast radius from the change kind, THEN pick the instrument** — that is a method; "run more builds" is not. | OPEN |
| GAP-146 | Process — how a gap gets CLAIMED, and how the registry learns about it | P | **The registry showed GAP-028 available while `ep0587yw` had it implemented and gated, and the architect allocated it on that basis (2026-08-08).** No duplication occurred — but only because `bzfevnl6` **asked before starting rather than after**, and declined. **The near-miss IS the finding: the safeguard that worked was a human check against a wrong registry.** **ROOT CAUSE, from the owner, and it is sharper than "someone forgot" — the gap was UNCLAIMED-BY-CATEGORY, not by oversight.** `ep0587yw` **filed GAP-028 themselves**, as a GAP-014 follow-up, **in the same commit that closed GAP-014**, then began building. From inside, that never felt like claiming an unowned gap; it felt like continuing their own work — **there was no claim-shaped moment in the flow for a claim step to fire at.** So *"be more diligent"* would not have caught it: nobody was being un-diligent. **Any counter-measure that depends on a worker noticing a transition will miss this whole category.** **SECOND CAUSE, from `bzfevnl6`: the row cited `d90caeee`, a BORN-RED commit, as the lane state.** A born-red commit is where a lane **begins**, not where it **is**. This project deliberately creates born-red commits, so the hazard is built into a practice worth keeping. **Rule: a born-red SHA may be cited as where a lane STARTED; never as where it IS.** **VERIFIED, and it splits the problem in two:** `git ls-remote --heads origin \| grep gap-` returns `wip/gap-028-guarded-rebind` -> `d90caeee`. **The ownership claim WAS in the repo, discoverable, hours before the architect offered the gap out.** But that branch sits at the born-red commit while the real work lives on an unpushed branch that does not name the gap. **So the git signal answered "does someone own this?" CORRECTLY and "how far along are they?" WRONGLY — and the architect conflated the two.** Ownership and state are different questions and want different instruments. **ADOPTED IMMEDIATELY, no worker behavior change required: before offering ANY row, the architect checks `git ls-remote --heads origin \| grep gap-` AND asks the roster.** Both were available tonight and either alone would have caught this. **Candidate mechanisms still open, both from `ep0587yw`:** (1) **a gap filed as a follow-up defaults to its filer as owner** — free, zero worker action, correct here; (2) **ownership derived from git** — a gap-named branch or a gap id in a commit message *is* the claim, and it cannot go stale through forgetfulness because the worker must touch git to do the work at all. **Do not close this by adding a "remember to claim your gap" line to a doc** — the fix that asks for diligence is the one that already failed. | OPEN |
| GAP-147 | Process — merging two waits on the strength of a shared subject | P | **"Both are about X" is a TOPIC ADJACENCY, not a DEPENDENCY — and the architect nearly wrote one into the registry (2026-08-08).** Assigning GAP-029, the architect told its owner that *"the quantized census `bzfevnl6` is blocked on runs through this row too"* and asked them to confirm the dependency. **`ep0587yw` refused the premise instead of answering it**, because it does not resolve: searching the registry for "census" returns **only GAP-016/018/032/047 — all the production `panic!` census. There is no quantized-census row.** "Quantized census" was `bzfevnl6`'s phrase for their own lane, which the architect lifted from a peer message and re-issued as a registry-level fact. **That is relay-laundering — passing an unverified peer phrase downstream with the coordinator's authority attached — which is precisely the failure the architect has been careful about in every other direction.** **On the nearest real candidate the answer is NO**, and the reasoning is the reusable part: GAP-047 is a **type-safety** gap in how op ids pair with `FusedOpParams` at the registry layer; GAP-029 is a **trait-implementation** gap at the model layer. Both have "quantized" in the vicinity and share nothing else — either landing leaves the other exactly as reachable. **THE TEST, and it is cheap: name the ARTIFACT one produces that the other CONSUMES. If you cannot, they are adjacent, not dependent.** **⚠️ RUN THE TEST EVEN WHEN THE PREMISE RESOLVES — refinement from `ep0587yw`, who declined to let this row read as a cleaner win than it was.** They caught this premise **cheaply, and largely by luck of the failure mode**: "quantized census" resolved to nothing, so a single grep settled it. **Had the architect named a REAL row that merely was not a dependency, the grep would have SUCCEEDED** — and they would have gone straight to analysing the relationship with the architect's framing already installed as the thing to confirm or deny. **So a dependency claim that SURVIVES a grep is precisely the one that needs the artifact test; the one that fails a grep was never going to cost anything.** Recording the counter-example is the point: a rule justified only by the case that was easy to catch will not fire on the case that is not. **AND THE COORDINATOR-SIDE CHECK IS NOT THE ONE THE ARCHITECT FIRST WROTE.** Relay-laundering is **hardest to catch when the relayed thing is TRUE**. `bzfevnl6` almost certainly does have a census in their lane; the content was probably accurate. **The defect was the PROMOTION — from "another worker's phrase for their own work" to "a registry-level fact carrying the coordinator's authority" — not the truth of the claim.** So the check cannot be *"is this true?"*, which would have passed. It has to be **"does this have a row?"** A true statement with no row is still a laundered one, and the truth is what makes it slip through. **Same family as GAP-142** (a resolution inherits the scope of its FIX, not its TITLE) — here a *blocker* was inheriting the scope of its **subject area** rather than its **mechanism**. **Why it matters more than the instance:** merging two waits makes the registry stop tracking one of them, and the one that vanishes is invisible precisely because it now looks handled. | OPEN |
| GAP-148 | `fuel-dispatch/src/telemetry/structure_key_derive.rs` (Fuel's Baracuda-free sk3/sk4 deriver) | B | **sk4 (acc+mp) non-contraction precision-field WIRE ENCODING — Fuel ASSENTED 2026-08-08 to rules (a)-(e), verified against the deriver rather than from recall.** Four-party byte-match pin (Fuel, Unpopped, kiss-ref, Baracuda as token-derivers). The sk4 RFC had pinned the field's *semantics* and `/`-delimiter but **not its wire spelling**, so four derivers would have regenerated against 3+ honest readings and the byte-match would have failed **after four full corpus regens** — surfaced in review by Unpopped. **Fuel's codec is a POSITIVE PRECEDENT for the rule, not merely compatible with it:** the contraction field is already appended as an optional trailing slot only for a `Contraction` cell (`token.push('\|')`), **omitted entirely — not `-`, not empty — otherwise**; `contraction_field` already spells `acc` and `mp` explicitly even when defaulted; and (e) is **structurally unrepresentable** to violate here, since the trailing field comes from `match op { Contraction(cell) => Some(..), _ => None }`. **THE SUBTLETY FUEL ASKED KISS TO PIN AS AN EXPLICIT NON-EXAMPLE, because it is where a third deriver will go wrong:** Fuel's token carries **both** conventions at once — the **reduce field** (§6.6-0009) is **mandatory** and emits **`-`** when inapplicable (every non-`red` family does, by construction), while the **contraction field** is **optional-trailing** and is **omitted**. **The discriminator is mandatory-vs-optional-trailing, NOT applicable-vs-not.** An implementer who reads "a field emits `-` when it does not apply" — which is *literally true* of the adjacent field and stated in Fuel's own code comment — and applies it to the (acc+mp) slot emits a trailing `\|-` and breaks the byte-match. A plausible misreading, not a strawman. **FUEL-SIDE LATENT ISSUE FOUND WHILE VERIFYING, and it links to GAP-049:** a future fused contract-and-reduce op would be a new `FuelOpCategory` variant, would fall into the `_ => None` arm, and would **silently emit no precision field at all** — no error, no decline, a token quietly missing a coordinate. **That is the silent failure mode, and it is exactly the exhaustive-by-design vs extensible question GAP-049(3) is deciding.** Whoever rules on `FuelOpCategory` should know a wildcard arm here costs a wrong wire token, not just a missing branch. **ADDITIVITY CONFIRMED FROM THE CLAUSE, IN THE STRONG SENSE (2026-08-08).** Vulkane's KISS#134 generalization — **additive only when NO PRIOR TOKEN could have been affected by the field's absence** — is satisfied, not merely 'it appends'. **§6.7-0013 pins byte-stability:** every non-contraction token whose accumulator equals its compute dtype and whose `<mp>` is default is **byte-identical to the pre-sk4 codec modulo the §6.1 renames**, so the regen diff is exactly the cells that actually deviate. **§6.7-0012 pins it independently:** absence **MEANS** the default, *"so every existing token is unchanged in meaning"* — which is why a consumer holding a 9-field token still matches. **FUEL WILL ASSERT IT RATHER THAN CITE IT:** increment 2 derives a non-deviating non-contraction cell and asserts byte-identity to its increment-1 form. **A byte-identity claim between two codecs is exactly the kind that reads true and isn't** — cite the clause for intent, assert for fact. **AND THE (e) TRAP WAS REDISCOVERED INDEPENDENTLY FROM THE CLAUSE, by a different reader than the one who asked KISS to pin it.** Their statement of the danger is sharper than the original: **the mandatory reduce field is the NEAREST PRECEDENT IN THE SAME TOKEN and it teaches the OPPOSITE rule** (`-` when inapplicable, vs omit-entirely here). **A trap whose nearest analogue is wrong is worse than one with no analogue at all.** Two arrivals from different directions. **PIN COMPLETE 2026-08-08: all four token-derivers (unpopped-vocab, Fuel, kiss-ref) + KISS have assented.** Both Fuel additions were taken: **(f)** the `-`-vs-omitted non-example is pinned as an explicit clause sentence, and KISS noted that Fuel's codec **independently arriving at (a)/(b-d)/(e) makes this a two-deriver-CONVERGENT rule rather than one party's preference** — which is what the byte-match actually rests on. The fused contract-and-reduce observation is folded into the (e) caveat as a **required posture**: when that op arrives it MUST be a **loud** extension (new variant => compile break per deriver), never a `_ => None` emitting no field, so the reopening surfaces at build rather than as a wrong token. **KISS also corrected a Fuel citation against Fuel's own tree** (`dtype.rs:102` is `bf16`; the `f8e4m3` arm is `:106`). | **ASSENTED — pin complete** — Fuel-side latent issue OPEN (see GAP-076 for the same class inside `Op`) |
| GAP-149 | Process — any gate or instrument that DERIVES a set | P | **A COVERAGE INSTRUMENT'S NATURAL FAILURE MODE IS A SILENT FULL PASS, because "found nothing to check" and "everything checks out" produce IDENTICAL output. Two instances in two increments of one row (GAP-036), plus a third PREDICTED before it was built.** **(1) inc 1** — the scanner disarmed its `#[cfg(test)]` tracker one line early and reported **0 of 47 covered** while a hand count found 41 citations in a single file. It failed loudly *by luck of which direction the bug went*; the same bug shifted slightly reports **47 of 47** and nobody looks again. **(2) inc 2** — the prose parser walked a `Vec<char>` index and sliced a byte-indexed `str`, panicking on the spec's em dashes. Again loud **only by luck of the slice panicking**; a subtly different version returns an **empty clause set** and passes every totality check **vacuously**. **THE STANDING RULE, from `bzfevnl6`: any instrument that derives a set MUST assert the set is NON-TRIVIAL before trusting anything computed from it.** Both GAP-036 gates now do; **neither did by default**, which is the point — this is not a bug that gets written in, it is one that is left out. **THE CONNECTION THAT MAKES IT STRONGER, and it unifies a human rule with a machine one: this is the SAME DEFECT as `CLAUDE.md`'s most-repeated process failure.** That file records **three** separate false claims produced by treating an empty search result as evidence of absence, and its counter-measure is *"give every I-found-nothing a positive control."* **An empty derived set and an empty grep are the same failure wearing different clothes.** State them as one principle: **an emptiness result is never self-validating — it must be distinguished from a broken instrument before it is believed**, whether the instrument is a gate, a parser, a ledger query, or a person typing `grep`. **SHARPENED 2026-08-08 BY THE RULE FAILING TO CATCH A CASE ON ITS OWN — and this is the important half.** On GAP-058 the minimum-evidence assertion was pinned as instructed and **PASSED**: the parse found >=1000 contract sections and >=700 ledger entries, so the set was genuinely non-trivial. **The broken part was not the VOLUME, it was the DISCRIMINATOR** — a regex reported **794 of 794**, then **1162 of 1162**, because `max_ulp: ~` is YAML null and `[^\s#]` matched the `~`. **A count assertion alone sails straight through a predicate that matches everything.** **THE RULE NEEDS BOTH HALVES: assert the derived set is NON-TRIVIAL, and assert the predicate DISCRIMINATES WITHIN IT** — that it does not select all, and does not select none. **A perfect score (n of n) is a SMELL, not a result**, and the measurer caught this one precisely because 794-of-794 had the same shape as the 47-of-47 that burned them hours earlier. **(3) THIRD INSTANCE, 2026-08-08, AND THE SHARPEST — because EVERY CONTROL PASSED AND THE WORK WAS STILL WRONG.** On the GAP-058 flip attempt the measurer had all three controls green: **set non-trivial** (>1000 sections, >700 ledger entries) — yes; **predicate discriminates** (the null-aware fix from the same evening) — yes; **selector matches the measured population exactly** (asserted 216/578) — yes. **The flip was still wrong**, and a test gate caught what no amount of self-checking would have: a validator rule (**V-FKC-6**) coupling `audited` to *determinism*, in a file the measurement never touched. **THE LIMIT, in their words: "the controls checked my MEASUREMENT rather than my MODEL. A control can only falsify the question you thought you were asking."** **The generalisation this row should carry: SELF-CHECKING HAS A CEILING, and it is exactly the boundary of your own model.** Every control here was correct, well-built, and passed. What caught the error was an **independent mechanism encoding knowledge the author did not have** — the CI lint knew a coupling the author had never seen. **Controls validate the instrument; they cannot validate the model.** Falsifying a model needs something that does not share your assumptions: a gate someone else wrote, another consumer, an adversary, a peer. **This is the strongest argument in the registry for gates over vigilance** — not because people are careless, but because a careful person's controls are built from the same understanding as their error. **(4) FOURTH INSTANCE, GAP-077 — and it is a NEW failure mode, not another example of the old one: A DEGENERATE CORPUS CERTIFIES THE INSTRUMENT BY ACCIDENT.** The natural discrimination control for the coverage differ was its own **sabotage arm** — strip the claims and confirm a loss is reported. **It cannot work here.** The corpus is degenerate (zero bit-stable entries), so stripping 396 claims still reports **0 losses** — a result **indistinguishable from a completely broken differ**. **When the corpus itself is degenerate, no operation ON that corpus can establish that your instrument discriminates; the control must be built on CONSTRUCTED inputs.** Done here in three directions: a real loss reported, self-vs-self reporting nothing (catches stuck-at-yes), and a 2->1 reduction **not** counted as a last-candidate loss (pins the boundary). **Note this is the third distinct layer of the same lesson:** (i) assert the set is non-trivial; (ii) assert the predicate discriminates *within* it; **(iii) when the corpus cannot exhibit the phenomenon at all, establish discrimination OFF-corpus.** A control drawn from a corpus that cannot show the effect is the corpus-level twin of a positive control drawn from a stale tree — **it validates the instrument against a world where the answer is already the one you got.** **(5) FIFTH INSTANCE, 2026-08-08 — and it is a DERIVED ABSENCE OF WORK rather than a derived set, which is why it slipped a rule written about sets.** Enumerating `e5m2`'s consumers, **three consecutive `cargo check --workspace` aborts each reported `E0004: 0` with jobs still queued**: `objc2` ("only works on Apple platforms" — `fuel-metal-backend` is off `default-members` but `--workspace` re-includes it), then `onemkl-sys` (oneMKL absent), and only with both excluded did the real list appear (8 `E0004` lines / 4 sites). **An aborted enumeration and a completed one are BYTE-IDENTICAL at the top.** **The natural bug is that a toolchain gap reads as "nothing left to fix"** — the same two-states-one-output shape as every other instance in this row, but the output here is *zero remaining work*, which is the most welcome possible reading and therefore the least likely to be questioned. **Counter-measure: read the HALT LINE, never the error count.** **⚠️ AND IT UNDERMINES A RULE THE ARCHITECT HELPED LAND.** GAP-049(3) put *"an enum-variant addition must be gated workspace-scope AND all-features"* into `CLAUDE.md` — **but `--workspace` is unrunnable on this box and fails green.** The rule was itself an instrument whose failure mode is indistinguishable from success. `CLAUDE.md` now carries the exclusion list and the read-the-halt-line requirement. **(6) SIXTH INSTANCE, AND THE MOST DANGEROUS YET, BECAUSE IT WOULD HAVE CARRIED THE AUTHORITY OF A LIVE-GPU MEASUREMENT.** GAP-029's Step-0 probe reported `max_rel` **1.08e-2 at m=1 vs 8.10e-2 at m=6**. **On `max_rel` alone the finding would have been "CUDA quantized prefill is silently 8% wrong" — false, and worse than the defect being hunted.** Per-element relative error has a **tiny denominator near a zero crossing**, so its MAXIMUM grows with output count for reasons unrelated to accuracy — and **m=6 has 6x the outputs of m=1**. The metric **cannot discriminate** "systematically worse" from "more samples, bigger max"; `scale_rel` (denominator fixed by the tensor) and `median_rel` (outlier-resistant) can, and report **1.5-2.4x**. **A MEASURED NUMBER FROM A METRIC THAT CANNOT DISCRIMINATE is the same defect as a positive control drawn from a stale tree — and that is now THREE TIMES IN ONE DAY, TWICE BY THE ARCHITECT:** a process **count** offered as an ownership fact (GAP-150); **free RAM** offered as evidence a second CUDA build would fit, when `ptxas` address space was the binding resource (GAP-151); and `max_rel` offered as evidence of relative accuracy. **In all three the measurement was REAL and of the WRONG QUANTITY — which is worse than no measurement, because numbers stop the next person checking.** **Companion rule, from the same probe: A TOLERANCE IS A PROPERTY OF A NUMERICAL PATH, NOT OF AN OP.** The probe first asserted `max_rel < 1e-5`, imported from an oracle that realizes the **recipe** path (`dequant -> f32 matmul`, where the only slack is contraction order). **The CUDA path is a different algorithm**; the bound was a category error, not a tight threshold. **(7) PREDICTED, not yet built — GAP-058's successor work.** Deriving `audited_status` from a ledger covering **2.6%** of the corpus has exactly this shape, and the silent direction (*found no evidence => nothing flagged*) is the one that ships. **A minimum-evidence assertion must be pinned before the first verdict is computed.** Recorded here because it was predicted rather than discovered — which is what a rule is for. | OPEN |
| GAP-150 | Process / environment — killing a background CUDA build on this box | P | **A KILLED BACKGROUND CUDA BUILD DOES NOT KILL ITS `nvcc` GRANDCHILDREN. The process tree ORPHANS and keeps compiling — the harness reports the task dead while the box stays loaded (measured 2026-08-08: 16 `nvcc` + 9 `cicc`, 14.6 GB RSS, still deep in `baracuda-cutlass`, from a task the harness had already reported killed).** **Why it is nastier than it sounds: the load has no visible owner.** The lane that launched it sees a dead task and a frozen log with no exit code — only the PowerShell wrapper died. **Every OTHER lane sees an unexplained slow or OOM-ing build**, with a cause that is invisible from where they are standing. Two workers can each spend an hour diagnosing "my build is slow" while the actual explanation belongs to neither of them. **WHAT MADE IT SURVIVABLE — copy this, it is the whole mitigation: the owning lane BROADCAST it to the other lanes unprompted**, naming the expected duration and the symptom to expect. Without that, the orphan is an unattributable environmental fault. **RULING on kill-vs-let-finish: LET IT FINISH, and there are two reasons, only one of which is about load.** (1) The arithmetic — killing loses in-progress `baracuda-cutlass` `nvcc` work and a restart re-incurs the two heaviest crates (`cutlass` + `kernels-sys`), i.e. **strictly more total load on the box than completing.** (2) **The evidential reason, which is the one this registry cares about: killing it leaves the next reader unable to distinguish "I killed it" from "it failed" in the log they inherit** — the exact ambiguity that cost this project twice on 2026-08-08 (GAP-029's harness-ceiling kill, and the shared-checkout stale read). Letting it complete preserves a clean signal. **⚠️ THE ENTIRE ORPHAN PREMISE ABOVE WAS FALSE, AND THE WAY IT BECAME "CONFIRMED" IS THE REAL FINDING. Corrected the same hour.** Traced by **parentage**: all 16 `nvcc` shared parent `57752`, a `baracuda-kernels-sys` build script whose exe path is `C:\Projects\fuel-gap029\target\...`, under cargo `50428` running `cargo test -p fuel-core --features cuda --test gap029_step0_q4_0_cuda_prefill_probe --no-run`. **That tree was a DIFFERENT lane's LIVE, HEALTHY build.** A second cargo (`30420`, `cpu_end_to_end`) was present and unseen. **Nothing belonged to the lane that claimed the orphan — their build had died completely, leaving them waiting for a process that did not exist.** **HOW A WRONG CLAIM GOT STAMPED "CONFIRMED BY DIRECT MEASUREMENT" — the failure worth copying:** the owning lane inferred ownership from *"single cargo, start time matches my launch."* The architect ran `Get-Process cargo`, saw a count of **1**, and replied *"your read is confirmed by direct measurement."* **The measurement could not have distinguished WHOSE build it was — it verified the COUNT and ACCEPTED the ATTRIBUTION**, then presented the combination as verification. **A PROCESS COUNT IS NOT AN OWNERSHIP FACT.** **And the amplifier: two parties made the SAME CLASS of error and AGREED. Neither reading was verification alone; agreement made both look confirmed.** Relay-laundering (GAP-147) in a new dress — an unverified claim taken from a peer, "checked" with an instrument that does not test it, and re-emitted with the coordinator's authority attached. **The cost was concrete: a third lane was advised to wait for the drain, which would have had them wait for THEIR OWN BUILD to finish so they could start it.** **THE CHEAP DISCRIMINATOR, and it is not the count:** walk **parentage**, or read the **target path in the build-script argv** (`fuel-gap029` vs `fuel-gap049`). Two cargo invocations with similar command lines are **indistinguishable by count, by name, and by start time**. Note also the peer check was scoped to `C:\Projects\fuel` while workers build in isolated worktrees, so it could not have seen a second builder either way. **MARKER PRECISION, corrected by its author:** a `DONE_EXITCODE=` marker distinguishes *"the log ends"* from *"the build ended"* — it does **NOT** distinguish *"still running"* from *"died"*, because **absence is consistent with both.** That requires a **pid check paired with the marker**, emitting a distinct `ABORTED` event when the process vanishes without one. The marker alone is not a liveness test. **RECOVERY PATTERN, from the same lane:** once the orphan releases the `target` lock, do a **fast observable re-check** — `touch` the crate + `cargo check`, deps already cached — so the real `Checking <crate>` artifact is emitted and the verdict is seen rather than inferred from the orphan's frozen log. | OPEN — mitigations recorded, no fix needed |
| GAP-151 | Machine constraint — concurrent `--features cuda` builds; `gpu-run`'s coverage boundary | P | **TWO CONCURRENT `--features cuda` BUILDS DO NOT FIT ON THIS BOX, and `gpu-run` does not cover it: it serialises GPU RUNS, not BUILDS. That is precisely the hole this fell through (2026-08-08).** **Observed:** `ptxas fatal : Memory allocation failure` in `baracuda-kernels-sys`'s vendored flash-attention (`flash_fwd_hdim128_bf16_sm80.cu`) with ~22 nvcc from one lane against ~16 from another. **426 kernels compile through a single build script.** **⚠️ THE ARCHITECT MEASURED THE WRONG RESOURCE AND CLEARED THE SECOND BUILD ON THAT BASIS.** Free RAM was measured at **22.36 GB / 63.21 GB** and a second build judged to fit. **`ptxas` failed an allocation while 22 GB was free** — so the binding constraint is **per-process address space / allocator behaviour under concurrency**, not system free memory. **A measurement of the wrong resource is not weak evidence, it is no evidence**, and this is the second time in one day the architect ran a measurement that could not test the claim it was offered for (see GAP-150). **THE DISCRIMINATOR IS ONE STRING, AND THE ERROR TEXT IS ACTIVELY MISLEADING.** The failure presents as cargo **exit 101 with exactly one `error:` line** (`failed to run custom build command for baracuda-kernels-sys`), the real cause buried under harmless CUTLASS `#177-D` warnings. **Three wrong conclusions were available and all false:** *"GAP-007 confirmed"*, *"baracuda alpha.78 is broken"*, *"my probe does not compile"* — **and the first would have gone straight into Lightbulb's sequencing.** **"An allocator failure inside a code generator is not a fact about the code being generated."** **Distinct from the existing never-trust-an-exit-code rule, and nastier: there the error text is ABSENT; here it is PRESENT and WRONG.** Landed in `CLAUDE.md`. **ROOT CAUSE FOUND 2026-08-08, and it INVALIDATES the `-j 4` mitigation this row and `CLAUDE.md` first recorded.** Traced in the **reference-only** `C:\Projects\baracuda` checkout (Fuel builds crates.io **alpha.78** — re-verify before relying on it): `baracuda-kernels-sys/build.rs:215` uses `baracuda_forge::KernelBuilder`; `builder.rs:11` `use rayon::prelude::*` and `:457` `compile_jobs.par_iter().try_for_each(...)` spawns **one `nvcc` per job**; `:366`/`:619` call `init_thread_pool()`, and `parallel.rs` sizes that pool at **`thread_percentage` default 0.5 x `available_parallelism()`**. **`NUM_JOBS` IS NEVER READ** — positive-controlled (`nvcc` appears 6x in that build.rs, so the file was reached; `NUM_JOBS` appears nowhere in either sys build script or in forge). **So `cargo -j N` CANNOT cap this.** Cargo exports `NUM_JOBS` precisely so a build script can honour the user's `-j`; this one sizes from CPU count instead. **That is why two builds each took roughly half the box (~22 and ~16 nvcc) — a consumer asking for `-j 4` to be a good citizen still gets half the machine.** **RE-VERIFIED ON THE SHIPPED ARTIFACT, not the reference checkout:** `~/.cargo/registry/.../baracuda-kernels-sys-0.0.1-alpha.78` and `baracuda-forge-0.0.1-alpha.78`. `NUM_JOBS` **0 hits** in `build.rs` (positive control, same file and tool: `nvcc` **6 hits**); `KernelBuilder` at `build.rs:215`; and **TWO** `par_iter()` sites in `builder.rs` — `:457` **and `:698`** — where the architect had found only one. **The architect's trace was flagged reference-only and was then upgraded by someone checking the artifact Fuel actually compiles**, which is the correct handling of a caveated finding. **⚠️ AND THE ARCHITECT'S FIRST ASK TO BARACUDA WAS THE WRONG SHAPE: THE CAP ALREADY EXISTS.** `parallel.rs:96-118` checks **`BARACUDA_FORGE_THREADS` FIRST**, then `RAYON_NUM_THREADS` (honoured **explicitly**, not incidentally via rayon's global pool), then falls back to `thread_percentage` 0.5. `BARACUDA_FORGE_THREADS` is purpose-built and **better than `RAYON_NUM_THREADS` on the merits** — it caps only forge's pool rather than perturbing every rayon consumer in the build. **SO THE DEFECT IS DISCOVERABILITY, AND THE EVIDENCE FOR IT IS US: two people ACTIVELY SEARCHING for exactly this knob, both reading `parallel.rs` and `builder.rs`, both missed it and both proposed inventing one.** A knob nobody can find is a knob nobody uses; the observable consequence is that consumers reach for `-j`, get nothing, and conclude the build is unmanageable. **ASK ACCEPTED BY BARACUDA, WITH A REFINEMENT THAT SHOWS THE ARCHITECT'S VERSION WOULD HAVE MADE IT WORSE.** The proposal was *honour `NUM_JOBS` as a fallback* — i.e. `max_threads = NUM_JOBS.or(50%)`. **Baracuda caught that `NUM_JOBS` DEFAULTS TO LOGICAL-CPU-COUNT when `-j` is omitted**, so that form would have **raised the common no-`-j` build from 50% to 100% of the box** — doubling the oversubscription this whole row exists to stop. **Their correction: `NUM_JOBS` may only ever LOWER.** Compute the 50% default, then `calculated = calculated.min(NUM_JOBS)`. Result: `-j 4` -> 4 (the good-citizen case works), no `-j` -> 50% (unchanged), `-j 100` -> still 50% (the memory-safe heuristic survives, and explicit `BARACUDA_FORGE_THREADS`/`RAYON_NUM_THREADS` remain the way to go higher). **Shipping in the next `baracuda-forge`.** **Second time today a proposal that looked obviously right would have made things worse** (cf. GAP-095's `precision_explicit`, which was cheaper *and* more principled *and* would have broken `FKC-4.8-0001`). **The common factor: both were reasoned correctly from an incomplete model of a default.** **Still asked, and accepted:** document `BARACUDA_FORGE_THREADS` — ideally a `cargo:warning` banner when the 50% default is in effect, since that tells you at the moment it matters. **Baracuda is also taking the misleading-error half**: having the forge builder detect `ptxas fatal` / `Memory allocation failure` and re-surface it as the headline, so the next oversubscription is **self-diagnosing** rather than presenting as a broken dependency. That is the fix at the source for the trap that nearly became a false *"alpha.78 is broken"* bug report. **DESIGN POINT RATIFIED BY BOTH SIDES: two resources, two answers — GPU contention SERIALIZES (mutex), HOST BUILD contention COEXISTS (cooperative `NUM_JOBS`). `gpu-run` must NOT grow a build mode**, and Baracuda's xtask compiling UNLOCKED is deliberately correct for the GPU axis. Complementary, not overlapping. **Architect's design position: `gpu-run` should NOT grow a build mode.** A lock makes two builds take 2T; honouring `NUM_JOBS` lets them coexist at half speed each. **Builds are long and five projects share this box, so the build-side answer is COOPERATIVE, not a mutex** — and `gpu-run`'s scope boundary (runs, not compiles) is correct as it stands. **⚠️ THE CAP RULE'S FIRST PHRASING WAS WRONG AND EXEMPTED THE TRIGGERING INVOCATION (caught by `bzfevnl6`, same hour it was broadcast).** It read *"on any `--features cuda` build"*. **`baracuda-kernels-sys`'s build script forges kernels UNCONDITIONALLY — it never consults the `cuda` feature — and `--workspace` pulls in `fuel-cuda-backend`, so `cargo check --workspace --all-targets` with DEFAULT FEATURES is a CUDA build.** That is where ~32 nvcc came from on a lane whose command line said CUDA nowhere and whose feature flags said the opposite. **Corrected to: any `--features cuda` build OR any `--workspace` invocation.** **The bind worth naming: the workspace-scope gate that enum-variant additions are REQUIRED to run (GAP-049(3)) is itself one of these builds** — so the rule that exists to catch dtype consumers is also the one most likely to take the box down, and all three lost builds today were that shape. **AND A CONFLATION WORTH KEEPING, self-corrected by the same worker: "doesn't touch the GPU" and "is cheap on this box" are INDEPENDENT PROPERTIES.** They had called the e5m2 enumeration *"CPU-cheap, no GPU"* — device-wise true (`gpu-run` not needed, the 4070 untouched), machine-wise it was the heaviest thing running. **⚠️ THE COEXISTENCE EXPERIMENT WAS VOIDED BEFORE IT RAN, and the catch prevented a FALSE POSITIVE on its own question.** The architect asked for one capped build to run concurrently with another lane's. `bzfevnl6` had already killed theirs and **verified the box drained to 0 nvcc / 0 cargo** — so the capped build would have run **alone**, and a green would have been read as *"capped builds coexist"* and promoted to a standing rule on evidence that never tested it. **Their build was also UNCAPPED, making it the wrong arm regardless.** The configuration the standing rule actually asserts is safe is **two CAPPED builds concurrently**; that must be scheduled deliberately, not stumbled into. **BARACUDA HAS FIXED ALL THREE UPSTREAM (`62182fce`) — AND THE FIX IS ON A BRANCH, NOT PUBLISHED, SO FUEL'S CONSTRAINT IS UNCHANGED TODAY.** Recorded that way deliberately: *"Baracuda fixed it"* is exactly the sentence that reaches a worker as *"the problem is gone"* and gets the cap relaxed. **`BARACUDA_FORGE_THREADS=6` plus drain-wait stay MANDATORY until a published version is pinned AND measured.** What landed upstream: **(1)** `ptxas fatal` / `Memory allocation failure` re-surfaced out from under the CUTLASS `#177-D` wall as **both a `cargo:warning` and the LEADING line of the build error**, with a *"usually host oversubscription, not a code defect"* hint — **cache-independent, fires every occurrence**; **(2)** `NUM_JOBS` as an **upper cap** via a pure, unit-tested `resolve_thread_count` (`-j 4`->4, no-`-j`->50%, `-j 100`->50%, `-j 1`->floored) — **the precedence order is where the architect's own proposal went wrong**, so pinning it as a pure fn with the cases tested is the right shape; **(3)** the discoverability warning naming all three knobs and the failure they prevent, **plus a doc line stating that cargo caches the warning so its absence is not evidence a cap is set.** **ANTICIPATED, then NARROWED by Baracuda: the STRING is unchanged, only its POSITION moves.** `ptxas fatal` is still emitted **verbatim** — the fix *adds* it as the leading line and a `cargo:warning` rather than rewriting it. **So `grep ptxas fatal` matches before and after and the `CLAUDE.md` rule is strictly preserved**; only the symptom moves up the log. Worth workers knowing the string is identical, so a before/after comparison does not read as a new failure. **VERIFICATION PLANNED, NOT ASSUMED:** the measurable claim is `min(50%, NUM_JOBS)`, so `-j 4` should yield **4 nvcc** — against the **6-vs-~16** already measured under `BARACUDA_FORGE_THREADS=6` on this 32-CPU box. A clean before/after with a known baseline. **Measure it; do not take the changelog.** **COEXISTENCE CELL: STRONG EVIDENCE, NOT DIRECTLY OBSERVED — recorded at that grade deliberately, on the finder's insistence.** Two capped builds appear to have overlapped **~56 minutes (08:30-09:26), both at `BARACUDA_FORGE_THREADS=6`, neither hitting `ptxas fatal`**. **But nobody observed both nvcc trees simultaneously:** the one in-window measurement was `mine_tagged=6, total=16`, and **6 nvcc each spawning `cicc`/`ptxas` children accounts for ~16 unaided**, so it cannot discriminate "all mine" from "mine plus theirs". The overlap is reconstructed from the *other* lane's reported finish time. **One inferential step better than a code read, one worse than a measurement.** **⚠️ AND IT POINTS THE WAY THE ARCHITECT ALREADY WANTED IT TO POINT, which is a reason to hold it to a HIGHER bar.** The architect argued coexistence over serialization; a reconstruction supporting coexistence is precisely the evidence most likely to be over-credited. **The finder declining to promote it protected the architect's ruling from the architect's own prior.** **THE MEASUREMENT THAT WOULD SETTLE IT:** two capped builds started deliberately together, with a sampler logging `nvcc` counts **tagged by worktree path** every 30s across the window. **⚠️ THE DIRECT NUMBER INVERTS THE ARCHITECT'S DESIGN ARGUMENT, and the arithmetic is recorded because the ruling rested on it.** The coexist-over-serialize case was *"a lock makes two builds take 2T; coexistence runs both at half speed"* — **which assumed the cap costs ~2x. Measured, it costs 3-4x: the kernel phase is ~56 min at cap 6 versus ~15 min uncapped.** So: **coexist-capped => both lanes done at ~56 min (mean 56); serialize-uncapped => ~15 and ~30 (mean 22.5). On these numbers SERIALIZATION WINS.** **BUT THE FOOTPRINT ARITHMETIC EXPOSES A THIRD OPTION NOBODY COSTED: cap 6 x 2 lanes = 12 concurrent nvcc, while ONE UNCAPPED build is ~16.** Two capped builds currently load the box **LESS than one uncapped build** — we chose a cap *safer than proven-safe* and paid 3-4x for the margin. **16 is the proven-safe footprint** (single uncapped builds succeed repeatedly); the failure was at ~38; **everything between 16 and 38 is untested.** **RULED: cap stays 6 for anything running now** — a live safety margin does not move on arithmetic. **The sampler run happens at `BARACUDA_FORGE_THREADS=8`** — two lanes x 8 = the 16-nvcc footprint the box is known to survive, which is the configuration actually worth adopting rather than the conservative one we defaulted to, and it has a principled target instead of an arbitrary one. **MITIGATIONS:** **drain-wait is the control that actually works** — wait for non-own CUDA processes to exit (**ownership by command line / target path, never by count**); **`RAYON_NUM_THREADS=4` is the knob that reaches this pool** (untested, worth one run); emit a completion marker either way; carry a deadline so the wait cannot hang. **And they declined an immediate retry on the grounds that it would have put ~22 more nvcc onto a box that had just failed an allocation, plausibly killing a peer's build — *"their run is not mine to spend."*** **THE BOUNDARY QUESTION, ANSWERED 2026-08-08 (design; taken to Baracuda `do1s8hlh` at CireSnave's direction, THEIR INPUT PENDING, NOTHING BUILT): the answer is a PRIMITIVE we chose wrong, not a policy we need to tighten.** **(1) `gpu-run.ps1` STATES ITS OWN SCOPE AND THE JUSTIFICATION IS FALSE — lines 25-28:** *"wrap GPU-TOUCHING runs only… **Do NOT wrap compile-only (cargo check / clippy / build) — those never touch the device and serializing them only wastes wall-clock**."* **The premise is TRUE — `nvcc`/`ptxas` genuinely never touch the device — and the conclusion is FALSE, because THE BINDING RESOURCE WAS NEVER THE DEVICE.** It is host address space consumed by K concurrent `ptxas`, contended by **builds only** — *exactly the population the rule excludes.* **A coverage hole with a stated, plausible, wrong justification is worse than an unstated one: it argues the next reader out of noticing.** Same shape as GAP-157's `tri_backend` author, who documented the hazard precisely and still had no control for it. **(2) EXTENDING THE MUTEX IS ALSO WRONG, AND BARACUDA'S OWN MEASUREMENT IS WHAT SHOWS IT: the resource is DIVISIBLE.** The box survives **2 lanes x 8 forge threads = 16 concurrent `nvcc`** and failed an allocation near 22. **That is a BUDGET, not an exclusive.** CUDA builds run 10-30 min here; serializing them behind `Global\gpu-run` would make a 3-lane project **1-lane for hours** to protect a resource that demonstrably admits two holders — **paying exclusion prices for a semaphore problem.** **(3) THE TRAP IN THE OBVIOUS FIX, AND IT IS THE REAL CONTENT OF THIS ENTRY: a named `Semaphore(2)` would QUIETLY DESTROY THE ONE PROPERTY `gpu-run` IS BUILT ON.** **Windows `Mutex` has abandoned-holder recovery; `Semaphore` has NONE.** The wrapper's entire robustness story — validated by Baracuda's shakedown through a real hard-kill, and designed to survive a bugcheck — is `AbandonedMutexException`. **A hard-killed build holding a semaphore slot leaks that count PERMANENTLY; after N kills every CUDA build on the machine blocks forever, with no stale-state signal and nothing to reclaim.** That **fails closed and silent — STRICTLY WORSE THAN NO COORDINATION**, since today's failure is at least a loud `ptxas` error. **The tidier-looking primitive is the one that throws away the property the whole design rests on.** **PROPOSED: N NAMED MUTEXES AS SLOTS** (`Global\cuda-build-slot-0`, `-slot-1`; `WaitOne(0)` each in turn, take the first free, sliced blocking wait if none) — **counted admission that KEEPS per-slot abandonment recovery**, reusing the already-validated property instead of trading it away. **Additive: a new `cuda-build.ps1` sibling, ZERO changes to the proven `gpu-run.ps1` path** — the working lock is not to be risked to fix an adjacent problem. **OPEN QUESTIONS PUT TO BARACUDA (their call, and (c) may make this a Baracuda-side fix in a Fuel-side costume): (a)** is `BARACUDA_FORGE_THREADS` the right knob or does `RAYON_NUM_THREADS` reach the pool more reliably, and does a wrapper setting `FORGE_THREADS=8` fight their new `calculated.min(NUM_JOBS)`? **(b)** does *8-per-lane x 2* hold generally, or was 16 the ceiling only for Fuel's specific kernel set — **if the footprint is kernel-dependent, a fixed slot count is a FICTION and the real answer is a memory-aware admission check.** **(c)** wrapper vs. in-crate default — **a wrapper is a CONVENTION, an in-crate default is a CONTROL, and this project's own standing rule says the control wins.** | OPEN — boundary ANSWERED as design, unbuilt, pending Baracuda |
| GAP-152 | `fuel-cuda-backend/Cargo.toml:33` (non-optional `baracuda-kernels-sys`) + root `Cargo.toml` `default-members` | B | **EVERY WORKSPACE BUILD ON THIS REPO IS A CUDA BUILD — including a bare `cargo check` at the root with no flags and no features. This is the mechanism behind GAP-151, and plausibly behind the documented ~31-minute root build.** **Manifest evidence (independent of the runtime observation):** `fuel-cuda-backend/Cargo.toml:33` declares `baracuda-kernels-sys = { workspace = true }` — **not `optional`**, not feature-gated (contrast `baracuda-cudnn` / `baracuda-nccl` on lines 35-37, which ARE optional). And root `Cargo.toml` lists `fuel-cuda-backend` in **`default-members` (line 54)** as well as `members` (line 21). **So the default build graph pulls in a crate whose build script forges 426 CUDA kernels**, and nothing on the command line says so — *the command line lies* (`d27pmruh`'s phrasing). **TWO KINDS OF EVIDENCE, DELIBERATELY DISTINGUISHED — because a relay nearly got counted as corroboration.** `bzfevnl6` **observed** ~32 nvcc from `cargo check --workspace --all-targets` at default features (the EFFECT). The manifest facts above are the **MECHANISM**, read independently. `d27pmruh` forwarded the observation and then **retracted their own "independent weight" framing**, correctly: it was one observation reported twice, which is the accidental-corroboration shape (GAP-150). They also noted their own build could not corroborate it — `-p fuel-cuda-backend` is *explicitly* CUDA, so it cannot test the surprising case. **The manifest read is the second kind of evidence they said they could not supply.** **WHY THIS IS A ROW AND NOT JUST AN EXPLANATION: it is fixable, and fixing it dissolves the class.** If the baracuda kernel deps were optional behind a `fuel-cuda-backend` feature, or `fuel-cuda-backend` left `default-members`, **plain workspace work would not forge CUDA kernels at all** — no ptxas pressure, no toolchain requirement, and the mandated workspace-scope gate (GAP-049(3)) would stop being the most dangerous command on the box. **Cost to weigh against it:** the CUDA backend would then need an explicit feature to be checked at all, so a dtype-variant enumeration would have to run that feature explicitly or silently skip the CUDA arms — **which is exactly the GAP-059/GAP-049 failure mode**, and would need a gate of its own. **Do not adopt without pricing that side.** | OPEN |
| GAP-153 | `fuel-ir` `DType::F6E2M3` / `DType::F6E3M2` — bare 6-bit storage dtypes | B | **Fuel's two bare 6-bit float dtypes exist in a form KISS has RULED cannot exist. This is a RESTRUCTURE, not a rename, and it is deliberately NOT part of the sk4 regen.** **The ruling (Eric, via the KISS hub, 2026-08-08): `f6`/`f4` are BLOCK-SCOPED ELEMENT FORMATS, not bare §6.1 storage dtypes.** The reasoning is KISS#32's and it is a byte-layout argument, not a taste one: **6 bits do not tile a byte.** Every other sub-byte KISS dtype has a clean convention (`s4`/`u4` nibble-pair, `b1` 8/byte); 6-bit has none, so `e2m3`/`e3m2` exist in practice **only inside MXFP6 blocks, where the block defines the packing**. §6.1's own rule is that *a dtype pins byte layout only* — **a bare 6-bit token has no byte layout to pin.** KISS tried the bare-storage-dtype form in **PRs #33/#34 and closed them** after a verify pass caught exactly this. **So Fuel is not merely mis-spelled here — it has a dtype whose storage semantics are undefined at the token level.** `F4` is unaffected (4 bits nibble-pair like `s4`/`u4`); `F8E8M0`/`F8E6M2` are unaffected (they are **scales**, and `f8e6m2` lands in §6.1 as one). **Only the two 6-bit element formats need the restructure.** **EXPLICITLY OUT OF SCOPE FOR THE sk4 REGEN.** The regen (GAP-097 / KISS Phase-0 PR-1) is renames + FP8 spellings + complex-flip + (acc+mp) + SCHEMA_VERSION 4 — all token-level. **This is a storage-model change and must not ride with it**, or a wire-codec regen turns into an encoding redesign mid-flight. MX arrives additively as PR-2, which is where this work belongs if it belongs anywhere near the regen at all. **Not started, unowned, and it needs a design pass before an implementation pass** — the question is what a block-scoped element format looks like in Fuel's `DType`/`SType` split, which is the self-describing-storage seam. | OPEN |
| GAP-154 | the `m>1` Q4_0 route on CUDA (baracuda MMVQ) — numerical audit | C | **The `m>1` quantized matmul route on CUDA is NUMERICALLY UNAUDITED. Filed so that "the Step-0 probe passed" cannot be read as more than it says.** That probe established a **structural** fact — the node does not hit the `total_rows` guard — and measured error against **exact dequant** at ~1e-4. **Fine for a decode step; nobody has checked what it does across a long prefill.** **Explicitly NOT established: that baracuda's MMVQ path is numerically correct.** Auditing it against a **quantization-aware** reference is a different question the probe was not built for. **The finder asked that this cell read EMPTY rather than be filled by a probe built for something else** — the right instinct, and the reason this is a row rather than a footnote. Numbers to build on: `m=1` `scale_rel` 1.97e-4 / `median_rel` 1.74e-4; `m=6` 4.79e-4 / 2.55e-4 — **same accuracy class, 1.5-2.4x across the step.** Nothing alarming, and nothing verified. | OPEN |
| GAP-155 | `fuel-ir` `DType` — the reserved `f8e4m3fnuz` / `f8e5m2fnuz` spellings | C | **Fuel cannot distinguish a RESERVED KISS dtype from an UNKNOWN one, which is a real KISS-Classify conformance failure — ruled NOT a byte-match blocker by the KISS architect, and Fuel's to register honestly rather than scope away.** **§6.1-0001 is explicit:** a reader **MUST recognise** a reserved spelling **and distinguish it from an unknown token**, answering with a typed decline pinned as `reserved_fnuz_dtypes_typed_decline`. **Fuel has no variant for either, so an unknown token and a reserved token are the same case to it.** Insofar as Fuel claims KISS-Classify conformance, it fails that clause today. **NOT a blocker for sk4:** the four-way byte-match is a **producer** exercise — four implementations regenerate `structure_key` tokens and compare bytes on the intersection each implements. Fuel's **read**-path gap is not exercised by it. **⚠️ RULED OUT OF THE sk4 REGEN, AGAINST THE KISS ARCHITECT'S RECOMMENDATION, and the reasoning is the row's real content.** They recommended folding it in since sk4 respells both anyway and the code is already open. **Declined: adding these variants is NOT a token respelling — it is a DTYPE ADDITION, which ripples through every exhaustive match in every crate** (the `e5m2` increment ran to 19 sites across 6 crates for one dtype). **The regen's entire value is being mechanically checkable against a frozen table; a multi-crate ripple inside it destroys that** — a byte mismatch would stop being a lookup and become a bisect. **Same principle that split `(acc+mp)` into its own increment: do not mix a change verifiable against a frozen table with a change that ripples through cross-project assent.** **Cheap in isolation, which is why it should stay isolated:** both variants exist **only to be declined** — no kernels, no FDX code, no safetensors mapping, no compute semantics at this schema version. **~~Next GAP-097 increment after `e5m2`.~~** **RULED 2026-08-08 (architect), AND THE RULING REVERSES THIS ROW'S OWN ASSUMPTION: the fix is a TYPED DECLINE AT THE PARSE SITES, *NOT* two new `DType` variants.** The row above argues the variants are "cheap in isolation" and merely deferred; that framing is withdrawn. **WHY THE VARIANTS ARE THE WRONG SHAPE: §6.1-0001 is a constraint on the READER, and it is satisfied one layer lower than the type system.** The clause asks that a reserved spelling be *recognised and distinguished from an unknown token* — that is a fact the PARSER can state. **A `DType::F8E4M3FNUZ` variant states something much stronger: that Fuel HAS the dtype.** Every exhaustive match in every crate then has to answer *"what do I do with this one?"*, and the honest answer at every single site is *decline* — so the variant's whole purpose is to be refused ~20 sites x 2 variants, and it **forces the invention of a payload type for a thing with no encoding, no kernel, no FDX mapping and no compute semantics.** The `e5m2` increment measured the ripple at **20 sites across 7 crates for ONE dtype that Fuel actually supports**; paying that twice over for two dtypes it deliberately does not support inverts the cost/benefit. **Enum-completeness is this project's usual first instinct and it is right most of the time — it is wrong here specifically because the enum is a CAPABILITY vocabulary, not a NAME registry.** **SHAPE OF THE FIX (allocated to the GAP-155 lane): (1)** both parse paths — `fuel-ir/src/dtype.rs:169` (`impl std::str::FromStr for DType`, `_ => Err(DTypeParseError(s))`) and `fuel-dispatch/src/fkc/lower.rs` (`_ => None` at **289, 393, 450, 1343**) — recognise the reserved spellings and decline them **DISTINGUISHABLY**. **(2) The distinction must be MATCHABLE BY A CALLER — an error kind/variant, NOT a message string.** A decline a consumer can only tell apart by `format!`-ing and substring-matching does not satisfy §6.1-0001 in any useful sense; it just moves the ambiguity into prose. **(3) The reserved-token list is derived from ONE place**, or the two parse paths drift and the second one silently reverts to treating reserved as unknown. **(4) BORN-RED TEST: `"f8e4m3fnuz"` must decline DIFFERENTLY from `"asdf"`.** Today they are **byte-identical outcomes**, which is exactly the failure — so the test fails before the fix and that failure IS the conformance gap, reproduced. **⚠️ WATCH THE DELIMITER TRAP: `e4m3fn` is a PREFIX OF `f8e4m3fnuz`** — a naive `contains`/`starts_with` check will classify the reserved token as the supported one and the test will pass for the wrong reason (CLAUDE.md, delimiter-trap-has-two-ends). Match the **whole token**. **ESCALATED TO CIRESNAVE rather than just executed, because the two readings differ in what `DType` MEANS and that is his call, not mine:** if he intends `DType` to enumerate every KISS-NAMED dtype as vocabulary — whether or not Fuel can represent it — then the variants are correct and the multi-crate ripple is simply the price. **He should choose knowing it costs a payload-type invention for an unencodable dtype, not thinking it is plumbing.** ~~Until he says otherwise the typed-decline ruling stands and the lane builds that.~~ **⚠️⚠️ RE-RULED 2026-08-12 — THIS ROW'S PREMISE IS WRONG, AND THE ESCALATION I SENT CIRESNAVE RESTED ON THE WRONG SURFACE. BOTH ARE MINE.** The row asserts *"Fuel cannot distinguish a RESERVED KISS dtype from an UNKNOWN one, which is a real KISS-Classify conformance failure."* **The distinguishing part is true and the conformance part does not apply, because FUEL HAS NO TOKEN-RECOGNITION SURFACE AT ALL.** **MEASURED, positive-controlled:** `telemetry/baracuda_provider.rs:7-9` states it in the code's own words — *"Fuel NEVER derives or parses the token (K1 opacity): the string comes back from Baracuda and is wrapped verbatim."* `KernelEntry.structure_key: String` (`fuel-kernel-seam/src/lib.rs:111`) is carried opaquely, stored and compared, never decomposed — its fixtures hold `"elementwise:f32"`, not even a sk4 token. **Zero** dtype-token parse arms across `fuel-dispatch` / `fuel-kernel-seam` / `fuel-kernel-seam-types`, against a **positive control of 116 op-token parse arms in `fkc/lower.rs`** — so the query shape finds parse tables where they exist. **KISS ARCHITECT RULING (2026-08-12, from the merged sk4 text, not preference): §6.1-0001 binds the TOKEN-RECOGNITION SURFACE, not an implementation's internal type enum.** Three grounds: the reserved paragraph frames the duty as *a reader answering a `structure_key`* ("an enum variant recognizes nothing"); §6.1-0004 scopes spelling to *"wherever it appears in a `structure_key` token or an operand descriptor"*; §6.7-0011 makes the token the only wire form. **No clause pins an implementation's data structures.** **CONSEQUENCE — THE FIX WAS HOURS FROM LANDING AT A SITE THE CLAUSE DOES NOT GOVERN.** `fuel-ir/src/dtype.rs::from_str` parses **Fuel's INTERNAL vocabulary** (`"f8e4m3"`, NOT sk4's `"f8e4m3fn"`), reached only by `runtime_fused.rs:339/:473` deserializing Fuel's OWN graph attrs — **`"f8e4m3fnuz"` cannot arrive there.** A correct, single-sourced, tested reserved list at that site is **INERT BY CONSTRUCTION** — the [[gap-077-flip-is-inert]] shape — and its green would have been cited as conformance evidence. **HONEST REPLACEMENT CLAIM, which is sharper than the one it retires: Fuel is NOT nonconformant with §6.1-0001's reader duty — FUEL IS NOT A KISS-CLASSIFY READER AT ALL. It is an EMITTER, and every Fuel conformance statement must be scoped to the emit surface in the statement itself.** (The KISS architect is drafting that as an RFC — a conformance claim MUST name the roles it covers, and an unoccupied role is an explicit exclusion, not an omission — with Fuel as cosignatory since this measurement is the motivating instance.) **sk4 FACTS for whoever picks this up:** sk4 is MERGED on KISS main (`19c3ad7`, `SCHEMA_VERSION = 4`). The reserved spellings are **RESPELLED, NOT RETIRED** — `e4m3fnuz` → **`f8e4m3fnuz`**, `e5m2fnuz` → **`f8e5m2fnuz`** (`origin/main:conformance/src/structure_key.rs:49`). **A relay saying sk4 "retires" them reached two other projects and was corrected; had Fuel acted on it, the correct-looking action was to DELETE this work, and the failure would have been silent — a reserved list holding the sk3 spellings declines nothing and every test still passes.** ⚠️ Read KISS via `git show origin/main:` — the shared `C:\Projects\KISS` worktree is checked out at `1c0c9a6` (**sk3**: 22 tokens, `s8`, `e4m3fn`, `c32`/`c64`) and I computed a whole divergence finding off it before catching the staleness. **REPOINTED, NOT CANCELLED → the emit surface.** `telemetry/structure_key_derive.rs::dtype_token(DType) -> Option<&'static str>` returns **ONE `None` for three different reasons**, the distinction written in the comment directly above it — the same defect GAP-161 found in `storage.rs:3023-3039`. The four-way taxonomy (**`supported` · `recognized-but-unsupported` · `reserved` · `unknown`**) is the KISS architect's, and it must be spelled as **two axes, not one enum** (*what kind* vs *why*, see GAP-161). **`u32` and `i16` have a `DType` AND a legal sk4 token AND are still declined on the operand-descriptor path** (`map_element_kind` → `None`), so *has a token* and *can be derived* already diverge inside Fuel's 14. **The misplaced-instrument lesson is carved out as GAP-167.** **KIND-AXIS TYPE RULED 2026-08-12, AND THE LANE'S PARTITION REPLACED MINE — I HAD SMUGGLED A BACKEND FACT INTO A GLOBAL TYPE.** I assigned `U32`/`I16`/`F8E8M0`/`F8E6M2` to `recognized-but-unsupported` **while also ruling KIND carries no backend field**, which is incoherent: **those four are GLOBALLY SUPPORTED** — Fuel has the `DType` and `dtype_token` emits a legal sk4 token — and are unsupported only *by baracuda's `ElementKind`*, which is the WHY axis (GAP-161). **My version would have put a per-backend answer inside a backend-independent type, and the first Vulkan or CPU consumer would have found it wrong.** **RULED PARTITION (total over sk4's 24 tokens): 14 `Supported`** (Fuel has the `DType` AND emits a token) **· 8 `RecognizedUnsupported`** (`u16`, `u64`, `bool`, `i4`, `u4`, `b1`, `c64`, `c128` — in the vocabulary, **no Fuel `DType` at all**) **· 2 `Reserved`** (the fnuz pair) **· everything else `Unknown`. NO BACKEND FIELD ANYWHERE.** **⚠️ THE PROPERTY THAT MAKES THIS BETTER THAN A TAXONOMY THAT MERELY READS WELL: 14 + 8 + 2 = 24 EXACTLY, so it is a TOTAL, TESTABLE PARTITION.** Asserted against KISS's own `DTYPES: [&str; 24]`, **a new sk4 token OR a new Fuel `DType` breaks the count and fails a test rather than silently landing in `Unknown`** — the build-time instrument this project prefers, which my version did not have. The test must assert **count AND membership**, not merely that every token maps somewhere. **AND THE LANE'S REASON THE TWO AXES CANNOT MERGE IS A TYPE-LEVEL IMPOSSIBILITY, NOT A DISCIPLINE — it supersedes the architect's "they compose, they don't collapse" framing: DIFFERENT DOMAINS.** KIND classifies a `&str` token (including strings that are not `DType`s at all); WHY classifies a `DType` already in the enum. **`StorageStatus` literally CANNOT be called on `"f8e4m3fnuz"`.** So the no-`Reserved`-variant-inside-`StorageStatus` rule is enforced by the signature rather than by vigilance. Both types land in `fuel-ir`; pair as `{ kind, why: Option<StorageStatus> }` if an umbrella is wanted, **never a merged enum.** **BOTH PARSE SITES LANDED `4133df43`** — site 2 (`fkc/lower.rs`) on its own merits (`.fkc.md` contracts are external and hand-authored, so a reserved spelling really can be typed there and today returns "bad dtype token", sending the author after a typo that does not exist), site 1 as internal ergonomics, **with the commit body stating what it is and is NOT — no §6.1-0001 claim.** The now-false doc comment on `dtype_token` (which asserted the parse side "is a real conformance gap, tracked separately") was replaced in the same commit. | OPEN — KIND partition ruled; both parse sites landed `4133df43` |

Fuel co-authors; the architect writes Fuel's half (RFC work is the architect's lane, so it does NOT consume a worker or compete with the sk4 regen).** **⚠️ FUEL'S ORIGINAL CITATION WAS AN OVERREACH AND IS WITHDRAWN.** The architect said KISS violated its own §6.1-0004. **It does not reach `classify.md` §6.1 or `ops.md` §6.16 — it binds the TRACEABILITY MATRIX only.** The KISS architect's framing is narrower and worse for KISS in the way that matters: **the failure mode was identified, a normative rule was written against it for the ONE artifact where it had already bitten, and every other duplicated normative fact was left hand-maintained. The principle was adopted locally and never generalized.** That argues for a **clause**; a violation claim would only have argued for a script. **AND FUEL'S OWN SUGGESTION WAS OVER-PROMISED, corrected by the same reply: GENERATION WOULD NOT HAVE CAUGHT TODAY'S DEFECT.** The sk4 drift was entirely in hand-written prose *around* the tables (`6.1-0002`, `6.1-0003`, `6.1-0012`, the complex family across five `ops.md` sites). **It prevents the NEXT class, not THIS class** — and a hardening item that over-promises is the one that gets trusted past its scope. **THE RFC'S CORE CONTRIBUTION FROM FUEL: THESE ARE TWO PATTERNS, NOT ONE, AND ONE CLAUSE WOULD ADDRESS ONE WHILE CLAIMING BOTH.** **PATTERN A — a normative fact duplicated across N artifacts, one of which is checked.** Fuel: GAP-141 (gate enforced 14 patterns, rule named 5), GAP-058 (794 `audited: true`, no ledger row), GAP-076 (fused destructiveness hardcoded away from its registry), GAP-152 (doc comment asserting unestablished wiring). KISS: three dtype tables with one linted; `kiss_trace` printing orphaned tests informationally while §6.1-0003 calls an orphan a build-fail. **Fix: DERIVE the duplicates from the SSOT — drift becomes unrepresentable.** **PATTERN B — an instrument whose failure mode is indistinguishable from success.** KISS: `discover_tests` keying by bare test name tree-wide, so same-named tests silently overwrite and **the instrument under-reports itself**. Fuel enforces it internally with no standard requiring it. **If B is out of scope, A alone still carries the RFC.** | OPEN — drafting Fuel's half |

sweep that reads 9 of 10 directories -> count still above minimum -> **green, with 10% of the corpus invisible.** **So the rule needs a THIRD clause: non-trivial, discriminating, and COMPLETE — the enumeration must be able to detect that it enumerated less than everything.** For a directory sweep the fix is small and total: **propagate the error instead of `return`/`continue`. A discovery step that can fail should FAIL, not SHRINK.** The general case is harder and belongs in the RFC as a stated limit rather than a clause. **Fuel's total-failure catch is DESIGNED where KISS's is INCIDENTAL** (a staleness assert that happens to require non-empty) — **the only difference between the two, and the smaller half. A component whose tests take no environment input is IMMUNE, not lucky.** Their zero is a *positive result* — 364 tests run, 4 `#[ignore]`d and accounted for — not an unfalsified absence. **A document that cannot say where a pattern STOPS is overclaiming.** **SCOPED 2026-08-08 — the row's "blocked on KISS ratifying a convention" was TOO COARSE. Most of it is blocked; a real subset is not, and the split is the first deliverable.** **(a) `#[ignore]`-gated AND runtime-skipping — FIXABLE NOW, no convention needed.** The `#[ignore]` **is** the declared gate: a human had to pass `--ignored` to run it at all. **So the runtime skip is redundant, and worse — you asked for this test explicitly and it decided not to run.** Those should **fail hard**, not skip. The population is *every `#[test]` that can reach a `return` before an assert*. **If that cannot be enumerated, saying so IS a valid deliverable** under GAP-159's standing check — *"I could not enumerate it; here is what I swept for"* beats a number whose scope is unstated. **ENUMERATED 2026-08-08 BY CONTROL FLOW — THE POPULATION IS ~435, NOT 33.
