| GAP-158 | `fuel-cuda-backend` `storage.rs` (tuple matches) + `cast.rs` — `DType` wildcards | B | **GAP-075's fabricating-wildcard class in the ONE crate that sweep never reached — filed BEFORE the CUDA verification leg returns, deliberately, so a green leg cannot imply these sites were examined.** **The asymmetry is the row's point: a compile check answers "what BREAKS"; it cannot answer "what QUIETLY ACCEPTS."** `device.rs` and `pinned.rs` carry **exhaustive** `match dtype` blocks with no wildcard, so adding `DType::F8E5M2` produces `E0004` there and the compiler enumerates them. **`storage.rs`'s tuple matches and `cast.rs` carry WILDCARDS over `DType`, so E5M2 falls into them and is SILENTLY MISHANDLED rather than rejected — invisible to the compiler by construction.** Those two questions need different instruments and only the first is being run. **Identified textually by the GAP-097 lane, who explicitly declined to treat the scan as the answer** (*"a textual scan is exactly the instrument that has been wrong all day"*) — so treat the site list as a starting point, not a survey. **Fix follows GAP-075's ruling**: decline loudly or make the match exhaustive; **do not fabricate**. Note GAP-075 closed as complete for the sites it swept, which did not include this crate — **the row was accurate about its own scope and this is outside it**, not a reopening. **RESOLVED 2026-08-08 — THE HYPOTHESIS IS FALSE. NOT-A-DEFECT, credited to `d27pmruh` via GAP-075.** Every `DType`-scrutinee wildcard in `fuel-cuda-backend` is a **typed decline**: `cast.rs`'s `baracuda_dtype_tag` — the site both the architect and the mover named — is `_ => return None`; `storage.rs`'s where-kernel tuple table is `_ => None`. **E5M2 is not silently mishandled there; it declines.** **THE METHOD IS WORTH MORE THAN THE RESULT, AND THE FIRST ATTEMPT IS THE REASON.** The mover's initial grep — *"DType wildcards that return a value"* — came back **EMPTY**, and **its positive control proved the pattern COULD NOT have matched a value-returning arm even if one existed.** Reporting that as *"no fabrication found"* would have been **the right conclusion reached by an instrument incapable of finding the counterexample** — a true statement with **no evidential content**, which is the failure mode nothing downstream can detect. **The replacement is now the standard shape for this class of sweep: classify EVERY wildcard arm by RHS, then check each arm's SCRUTINEE.** 59 arms: 18 `_ => None`, 15 `_ => Err/bail!`, 3 `_ => return Err(UnsupportedDtype)` (36 declines), **12 `_ => unreachable!` (panics)**, 1 `_ => false`, 1 `_ => "unknown status code"`, 2 blocks. **The scrutinee step is the one most people skip and it is what decides the row:** all 12 `unreachable!` arms match on `&cos.slice`/`&sin.slice` (`CudaStorageSlice`), **and E5M2 has no `CudaStorageSlice` variant, so it cannot construct one to reach them**; `_ => false` matches `dims()`; the blocks match `inputs.len()` and a graph-update result. **None are dtype-reachable.** Coverage control: `find` reports 46 `.rs` files, the glob covered **46/46**, and the complement contains no wildcard arms. **THE GENERAL LESSON, AND IT RETIRES AN INSTRUMENT THIS REGISTRY USED TWICE: the presence of a wildcard says NOTHING until you read what it does.** GAP-075 and GAP-158 were **both scoped off wildcard counts**; GAP-075's count was mostly real, GAP-158's was **entirely artifact**, and **nothing about the count could distinguish them.** **AND THE SYMMETRIC ERROR, NAMED:** the mover had earlier reported *"`cast.rs` silently accepts E5M2 — GAP-158 confirmed rather than predicted,"* inferred from the **compiler not firing there** — when the actual reason it did not fire is that the arm **declines**. **Not-firing is consistent with both fabricate-silently and decline-correctly, and the alarming reading was picked without reading the arm.** Same defect as that morning's empty-grep, in the **opposite direction**. ***An uninformative signal does not become informative by being read pessimistically.*** **The positive-control rule as everyone carries it is one-sided — *an empty grep is not evidence of absence* — which quietly implies the safe move is to assume the defect is there. It is not.** Absence-as-proof-of-absence and absence-as-proof-of-defect are the **same error**, and in a registry the pessimistic one is **more expensive**: a wrong OPEN row costs a lane a day *and looks responsible while doing it*. **`fuel-metal-backend` is untouched by this analysis and is now GAP-160.** | RESOLVED — NOT-A-DEFECT |
| GAP-159 | Process — reporting a row FIXED, and reporting a finding "filed" | P | **A STATE DERIVED FROM AN ARTIFACT'S EXISTENCE IS NOT A STATE DERIVED FROM ITS CONTENT — and the architect's own reporting has the defect.** Adopted 2026-08-08 from Baracuda via the KISS architect, whose instance is the miniature: **`gh pr list` showed `reviews=1` on five PRs, which reads as *reviewed*; there were 12 findings across them and 11 unaddressed.** Their line: ***"it has a review" is not "the review was read."*** **FUEL'S VERSION, and it is the architect's:** this registry holds **143 rows, most OPEN with a proposed fix**. **The rows are honest — the status column says OPEN. The SUMMARIES are the instrument that is not:** *"filed as GAP-157"* reads as *handled*. **A row nobody acts on is indistinguishable from a finding that was resolved, at the reporting layer** — exactly the `reviews=1` failure, one layer up. **STANDING CHECK ADOPTED: no row is reported FIXED without stating what it does NOT cover.** That is KISS's proposed §6.1-0011 (*a claim MUST carry the method that produced it and what that method did not examine*) applied to registry rows rather than to conformance claims. **The architect has been doing this INCONSISTENTLY** — GAP-095's Vulkan-gated pickup was stated, others were not — **and inconsistent is the same as absent for anyone relying on it.** **The general form covers a family already recorded here:** `reviews=1`; **`E0004: 0` from an aborted build** (GAP-149(5)); **`running 0 tests … ok`** (GAP-157); **a scoped check's `Checking` lines that never reached the target crate** (GAP-150); and **a `FIXED` row with live residuals**. **METHOD NOTE worth more than the rule — from the §8 objection that produced it: *"what produced this?" is a stronger question than "is this true?"*** Asking whether a claim is true invites checking it against **the same understanding that accepted it**; asking what produced it forces you to the **instrument**, whose limits are visible where the claim's are not. **The RFC's §8 objection was about ATTRIBUTION and surfaced FALSITY** — a backing row claiming a clause was backed when the tool still credits unexecuted backing. **Five instances found by the authors; the sixth found by one reader asking one question.** | OPEN — standing check in force |

| GAP-161 | Typed declines have NO EXPIRY — correct-today becomes wrong-tomorrow, silently | A | **FOUND AS A RESIDUAL OF GAP-158's CLEAN NEGATIVE, AND IT IS THE MORE IMPORTANT HALF.** GAP-158 established that `fuel-cuda-backend` **fabricates nothing** — 36 wildcard arms decline honestly. **It did NOT establish that the declines are SEMANTICALLY RIGHT**, and the reason they are currently right is the problem: **E5M2 has no CUDA storage at all, so declining is the only correct behaviour AVAILABLE.** **That makes them correct FOR A REASON THAT EXPIRES.** The moment `F8E5M2` gains CUDA storage, **every one of those arms keeps declining** and nothing marks the transition: **the compiler will not fire** (the wildcards still typecheck), **the tests will not fire** (they decline or skip), and **the capability will simply be absent while every instrument reports health.** **Same shape as GAP-157's runtime skips — a REQUIREMENT THAT IS REAL BUT UNDECLARED — one level up.** GAP-157 is about a test whose device requirement is undeclared; this is about a **decline whose JUSTIFICATION is undeclared.** In both cases the code is locally correct and the system-level claim it supports is false. **Generalizes well beyond E5M2:** the same argument applies to every `_ => None` added because a backend lacked a capability at the time of writing. **PROPOSED MECHANISM (not yet designed — bring a design before building):** a decline should be able to name the capability whose absence justifies it, e.g. `decline_pending!(F8E5M2, reason = "no CudaStorageSlice variant")`, so that **the arrival of the capability can be made to FAIL the build or a gate** rather than silently satisfying nothing. **The KISS-side analogue is #140/#141's declarable gates**, and this should be checked against them before Fuel invents a private form — **a per-project decline idiom would be the same defect one level up**, which is the exact note already standing on GAP-157. **DO NOT convert this into a sweep-and-annotate task on sight:** the population is every decline arm in the workspace, and **GAP-158 just retired wildcard-counting as an instrument.** Scope it by *capability*, not by *arm*. **ESCALATED by the GAP-158 mover, and they are right that it is worse than filed: A STALE DECLINE DOES NOT MERELY FAIL SILENTLY — IT ACTIVELY MISDIRECTS, AND IT GETS MORE CONVINCING THE LONGER IT STANDS.** When E5M2 gains CUDA storage those arms do not become *loud* wrongness; they keep declining a dtype that now works. **The symptom is *"E5M2 is unsupported on CUDA"* — which is INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM THE TRUE STATE TODAY** — so the natural diagnosis is *"storage isn't wired yet,"* **pointing the investigator at the one thing that was just fixed.** **AND THE DISTINCTION THAT MAKES THIS A REAL DEFECT CLASS RATHER THAN HOUSEKEEPING: this is NOT a stale TODO. A TODO is inert text. These are LOAD-BEARING CONTROL FLOW whose correctness is TIME-DEPENDENT and whose expiry is invisible to the compiler, to the tests, AND to the person reading the error message.** That third one is what a TODO can never do — **a stale comment misleads a reader who happens to read it; a stale decline manufactures evidence for a false diagnosis.** **DESIGN 2026-08-08 (architect). The row proposed a `decline_pending!` macro; that was the wrong shape and is withdrawn.** **THE KEY OBSERVATION: an expiring decline is a CROSS-TYPE COUPLING, which is exactly why no single exhaustive match catches it.** The arm matches on `DType`; the justification is a fact about `CudaStorageSlice`. **Making the `DType` match exhaustive — this project's own first-choice remedy — does NOT help, because `DType` is not the enum that changes when the capability arrives.** The event we need to trap is *"`CudaStorageSlice` grew a variant"*, and it happens in a different file from the decline it invalidates. **That is the whole difficulty, and it is why the obvious fixes miss.** **REJECTED — `decline_pending!(F8E5M2, reason = …)`:** a macro can record the reason but **cannot observe the capability arriving**, so it produces better documentation of a hazard, which GAP-157 already establishes is not a control for it. **Strictly worse than the current state, because it would LOOK like a mechanism.** **PROPOSED — A CANARY TEST OVER A DECLARED PAIR SET, which is the cheapest thing that actually FIRES:** one list of `(backend, dtype)` pairs that decline **because unimplemented** (distinct from declining because *impossible* — that distinction is the design's real content), and a test asserting each pair still has no storage/kernel. **When someone lands the capability, the assertion INVERTS and the test goes RED, naming the pair and pointing at the decline arms to revisit.** No macro machinery, no new vocabulary, and it fails in the direction that costs a build rather than a wrong answer. **THE TWO REASONS A DECLINE EXISTS MUST BE SEPARATED, AND THIS IS THE PART TO GET RIGHT:** *impossible* (F4 has no meaningful CUDA GEMM — will never be implemented; a permanent decline, correctly a plain wildcard) versus *unimplemented* (F8E5M2 has no storage YET — a temporary decline that expires). **Today both are spelled `_ => None` and NOTHING distinguishes them, which is why the expiry is invisible.** Even without the canary, **spelling them differently is most of the value** — a reader can then tell a settled decline from a queue item. **STILL OPEN, and it is a real design question rather than a detail: does the pair set get DERIVED or DECLARED?** Declared is cheap and rots (someone adds a decline and forgets the list — the registry's own recurring defect, one level up). Derived needs a single authority answering *"does backend B support dtype D"*, which Fuel does not have as one thing today. **Recommend DECLARED for increment 1 with the canary, then derive it if a capability table ever lands** — a declared list that FIRES beats a derived one that does not exist. **Check against KISS #140/#141 declarable gates before building: a per-project decline idiom would be the same defect one level up**, which is the note already standing on GAP-157 and it applies here unchanged. **NOT ALLOCATED — bring a design review before any code.** **⚠️ RE-RULED 2026-08-12 — THE REVIEW CAME BACK AND REFUTED MY DESIGN. THE CANARY-OVER-A-DECLARED-PAIR-LIST IS WITHDRAWN; THE LANE'S SINGLE-DECISION-POINT FORM IS ADOPTED.** Recorded as a refutation rather than an evolution, because the review found the design fails the standard *I cited to reject the alternative*. **(i) MY CANARY IS THE PRE-#141 STATE.** I rejected a `decline_pending!` macro for being "better documentation of a hazard, which GAP-157 establishes is not a control" — then proposed a design that puts the decline (the arm in `storage.rs`) and its declaration (a hand list in a test) in **two separate acts**, which is exactly what KISS #141 abolished: *"the skip mechanism and the declaration are now the same act — a test cannot decline invisibly."* **I proposed the thing I would have rejected in review.** (Also: my instruction to "check against KISS #140/#141's declarable gates" MIS-CITED them — they are the KISS side of GAP-157, and no pre-existing declarable gate for expiring *capability* declines exists to borrow. The lane extracted the principle anyway and used it against the design, which is the better move than noting the mis-citation.) **(ii) THE MEASURED KILL SHOT: THE CANARY'S PREDICATE CANNOT OBSERVE THE EVENT.** `F4`/`F8E8M0`/`F6E2M3`/`F6E3M2` already exist as **raw-byte `CudaSlice<u8>`** variants (`storage.rs:134-137`), so if F8E5M2 storage arrives by that same dummy-byte route, a canary asserting "typed variant absent" **never moves.** A control that reports coverage while being structurally unable to see the event is worse than none. **(iii) MY KEY WAS ILL-TYPED.** `F4` is **present** at `transfer_to_device` (~`:2947`) and **declines** at the compute op (~`:3365`) — same dtype, opposite answers — so storage and kernel are two populations and `(backend, dtype)` cannot express it; it needs `(backend, dtype, OP)`, multiplying the hand-maintained list. **(iv) CORRECTION TO THIS ROW'S OWN CLAIM: "making the `DType` match exhaustive does NOT help" IS AN OVERCLAIM.** True that a `DType`-exhaustive match does not re-fire when an *existing* dtype gains storage later — but it **does** force every dtype to be classified and **does** fire on dtype-add. **It is HALF the mechanism, complementary to the expiry trap, not a failed alternative**, and as written this row would send the next reader past the half that works. **(v) THE REFRAMING THAT MAKES THIS A SMALLER ROW THAN I FILED: the distinction I called the design's real content IS ALREADY IN THE CODE — as prose the type erases.** `storage.rs:3023-3025` writes *"declines because no variant exists — NOT because the dtype lacks a representation… Distinct from F8E6M2 below, whose encoding is unauthored (GAP-045)"* and then `:3026-3039` returns the **identical** `UnsupportedDtype` for all of them. Three reasons spelled out, collapsed to one value. **GAP-161 is therefore "stop erasing a distinction the author already made," not "invent a mechanism" — and my declared list would have been the SIXTH restatement of a decision already made five times, coupled to none of them.** **ADOPTED INCREMENT 1:** `StorageStatus` + `cuda_storage_status(DType)` as the ONE storage-existence authority, wildcard-free, typed reasons (`Present` / `Impossible` / `UnimplementedYet` / `UndefinedEncoding`); gate **`transfer_to_device` only** — the one site whose present-set genuinely equals storage existence; at the other sites single-source **the REASON** via a shared helper. **⚠️ THE LANE CAUGHT A WIDENING-BY-REMEDY I HAD APPROVED: `zeros`/`alloc_uninit`/`rand_*` decline the dummy-byte types as an OP decline layered on top of storage existence (you can transfer existing dummy-byte storage but not zero-init it), so gating those whole sites on storage-status would have wrongly ALLOWED zeros for dummy types — and no current test asks for that, so a green suite would not have caught it.** **ARCHITECT AMENDMENTS: (1) the `StorageStatus` TYPE lives in a SHARED home from day one** (`fuel-ir` — a fact about a `DType`, zero new dep edges) **even though only CUDA has a status fn in increment 1**: per-backend *functions* should differ, the *type* and the reason-is-typed rule must not, or Vulkan and CPU each invent their own and it becomes three copies instead of one type-move ([[fix-the-generator-not-the-output]] applied to the remedy). **(2) ⚠️ TWO AXES, NOT ONE ENUM (KISS architect's caution, and it supersedes my own "match lane A's reason strings" nudge, which pushed toward the collapse): *WHY* a decline exists (`Impossible`/`UnimplementedYet`/`UndefinedEncoding` — only the middle one EXPIRES) is a different axis from *WHAT KIND* it is (`reserved`/`recognized-but-unsupported`/`unknown` — a fact about vocabulary membership). `f8e4m3fnuz` is *reserved* in kind AND *expiring* in why; `F4` on a CUDA GEMM is *recognized-but-unsupported* in kind and *Impossible* in why. They COMBINE; they do not collapse. Merging them yields a taxonomy that answers neither cleanly — wanting a `Reserved` variant inside `StorageStatus` IS the collapse happening.** **(3) STATED LIMIT, recorded in the doc comment on the function and not only in a message: without a macro co-generating the `CudaStorageSlice` variants and the `Present` arms from one list, someone can wire F8E5M2 storage via a non-gated site without flipping the central classification. Strong, not airtight; the macro form is increment-2+.** Gates: both sabotages with confirmed recompilation, and the consistency canary must be SHOWN CAPABLE OF FAILING ("near-tautological, one place" is its virtue and its risk). **THE PRECISE NAME FOR THIS DEFECT, from the KISS architect 2026-08-12, and it is better than the one this row has been using: THE REASON→VALUE MAPPING IS NOT INJECTIVE.** Three distinct reasons at `storage.rs:3023-3039` return the **identical** `UnsupportedDtype`. **"Reasons erased into prose" describes the symptom; "not injective" names the instrument that catches it.** **AND IT PLACES THIS ROW IN A CLASS WITH A KNOWN CONSEQUENCE: exhaustiveness (`E0004`) buys COMPLETENESS — a new variant falling through a catch-all is loud once you look — while TWO VARIANTS MAPPED TO ONE OUTPUT IS SILENT ALL THE WAY DOWN: green build, complete report, and a consumer that AGREES WITH A REFERENCE FOR THE WRONG REASON. Strictly worse than a disagreement, because a disagreement gets investigated and a false agreement gets FILED.** **⚠️ SCOPE THE DEMAND, because a universal injectivity rule would be wrong: INJECTIVITY IS MANDATORY WHERE THE OUTPUT IS AN IDENTITY, OPTIONAL WHERE IT IS A CLASSIFICATION.** `sk4_token` is an identity (a collision means two operand shapes share a `structure_key`); **`map_element_kind` is a classification — two Fuel dtypes legitimately COULD map to one backend kind if the backend does not distinguish them, and forbidding that would encode a false constraint.** So `StorageStatus`'s reasons must be distinguishable **because a consumer branches on them**, not because distinctness is a general virtue. **CROSS-PROJECT: the same gap exists in the KISS reference — `decline_wire()` is exhaustive over all 20 `KeyDecline` variants with no catch-all and there is NO test asserting the 20 output strings are distinct.** Their Lane A is adding an injectivity test with a pinned count and a seeded-duplicate proof. **Fuel's `sk4_token` injectivity turns out to be covered INCIDENTALLY** — a token `BTreeSet` of cardinality 14 asserted against a dtype population of 14 — **but nothing names it as an injectivity check, so a refactor can delete the coverage unknowingly. Name the property the assertion protects.** **⚠️ NAME CORRECTED AGAIN, AND THIS ONE IS RIGHT: NOT "non-injective" — A DISTINCTION STATED IN PROSE MUST BE CARRIED IN THE TYPE** (KISS architect, KISS #167, third case). **A decline reason is neither an identity nor a classification: many causes legitimately share one verdict.** What is illegitimate is that **the author knew the distinctions, WROTE THEM DOWN, and shipped an interface that cannot express them — the comment claims a resolution the type does not have.** **THE GREPPABLE INSTRUMENT, RUN ON FUEL 2026-08-12: comments matching `distinct from` / `NOT because` / `as opposed to` / `unlike` in production Rust = 137 candidates; narrowed to within 5 lines of a decline return = 6 hits / 5 distinct sites; POSITIVE CONTROL (`storage.rs:3027`) SURVIVES the narrowing; 96% exclusion (discriminating, not eating the population). ⚠️ THE 5-LINE WINDOW IS A LOWER BOUND — this row's own arms span ~15 lines, so a comment preceding a long match can have its collapse out of reach.** **⚠️⚠️ AND READING THE ONE HIT THAT LOOKED LIKE A SECOND INSTANCE PRODUCED A REFINEMENT THAT STOPS THE INSTRUMENT FLAGGING CORRECT CODE: `fuel-metal-backend/src/storage.rs:128` joins SIX dtypes into one decline arm and IS CORRECT.** Its comment anticipates the objection — *"the arm below is dtype-parameterised, so joining it names the dtype and asserts nothing false about the format"* — and `Err(UnsupportedDTypeForOp(self.dtype, op))` really is parameterised, so six joined arms yield six distinct values. **SO "A DISTINCTION IN PROSE + A SINGLE RETURN" IS NOT THE TEST. THE TEST IS: IS THE *DIMENSION THE COMMENT DISTINGUISHES* CARRIED AS A FIELD IN THE RETURNED VALUE?** Measured: CUDA's `UnsupportedDtype { dtype: DType, op: &'static str }` and Metal's `UnsupportedDTypeForOp(dtype, op)` **both carry `dtype` and `op`; NEITHER carries a reason** — and this row's comment distinguishes **why** (unimplemented-yet / undefined-encoding / impossible). **The defect is exactly: the distinguished dimension has no field, while an undistinguished one does.** **LOSSY-AND-HONEST IS FINE (Metal claims only that it asserts nothing false, the weaker and correct claim); LOSSY-WHILE-ASSERTING-OTHERWISE IS THE DEFECT.** **CROSS-PROJECT, unhedged by them: the KISS reference has the adjacent gap — `decline_wire()` exhaustive over 20 `KeyDecline` variants with NO test that the 20 output strings are distinct.** Fuel's `sk4_token` injectivity is covered but **INCIDENTALLY** — a token `BTreeSet` of cardinality 14 asserted against a dtype population of 14 — **an assertion simultaneously load-bearing and INVISIBLE, so naming it is the entire fix: there is no code to write, only a name to attach.** **INCREMENT 1 LANDED `625c6b4d`** — `StorageStatus` in `fuel-ir` (zero new dep edges, re-verified on current `origin/main` rather than the lane's base: all four backends already dep `fuel-ir`) + `cuda_storage_status` as the ONE storage-existence authority, wildcard-free, typed reasons; `transfer_to_device` gated on it; the other sites single-sourcing the REASON through a shared `storage_unavailable` helper; new `CudaError::StorageUnavailable`. **Gates, per-phase and unaggregated:** `fuel-ir --all-targets` 58 lib passed + prose-hedge gate ok (the new prose adds 0 hedges); phase 1 `storage_status` unit tests **6 passed / 0 failed** with artifact `Compiling fuel-cuda-backend` and a `GAP161_ALL_GREEN` terminal marker; phase 2 `--all-targets --no-run` finished in 11.41s, **so the new `CudaError` variant broke no exhaustive match anywhere.** Re-verified on the MERGED tree after a `fuel-ir` doc change forced a `fuel-cuda-backend` recompile — *clean rebase is not compatibility*, applied unprompted. **BOTH SABOTAGES RED-CONFIRMED, AND IN THE DISCRIMINATING FORM — each reddened EXACTLY the intended tests with the others staying green** (a sabotage that reddens everything proves the suite runs, not that the guard works): **(1)** marking `F32` — which HAS a constructor — `UnimplementedYet` reddened exactly `present_set_is_exactly_the_storage_backed_dtypes` and `storage_unavailable_on_a_present_dtype_is_a_loud_error`, other 4 green; **(2)** dropping the typed `StorageUnavailable` path in the shared helper reddened exactly `storage_unavailable_on_an_absent_dtype_carries_the_reason`, other 5 green. Both `exit 101` **with a DONE marker present**, distinguishing *finished-failing* from *killed* — the distinction that cost two builds tonight to learn. **⚠️ SCOPE ON SABOTAGE-2, RECORDED RATHER THAN CLOSED, BECAUSE "SABOTAGE-VALIDATED" WOULD OTHERWISE BE READ AS COVERING MORE THAN IT DOES: THE MECHANISM IS SABOTAGE-VALIDATED; THE FOUR SITES' LIVE RUNTIME PATHS ARE NOT.** They are device-gated, so no pure test exercises them. **What IS established about the sites: (a) phase-2 `--all-targets` COMPILED them calling `storage_unavailable(dt, "op")` and the old hardcoded errors are GONE — so they demonstrably route through the tested mechanism, proven by the COMPILER rather than an assertion; (b) sabotage-1 corrupts the exact classification `transfer_to_device`'s gate derives from, transitively covering that repoint.** **ARCHITECT RULED AGAINST closing the gap with a live GPU test, and the reason generalises: it would add a FRAGILE Display-through-`fuel_ir::Error` assertion, and a fragile assertion is a LIABILITY not coverage — it breaks on unrelated message edits, gets weakened by whoever hits it next, then reads as coverage while asserting almost nothing. A WEAK TEST WHERE A COMPILE-LEVEL PROOF ALREADY EXISTS IS WORSE THAN NO TEST THERE.** The lane proposed running it anyway and was told not to. **DOC CROSS-LINKS BOTH WAYS, two fields never merged: `StorageStatus` uses `F8E5M2` as its worked example — KIND=`Supported` x WHY=`UnimplementedYet`, both axes applying and DISAGREEING, which is a LIVE proof the split was necessary** — where lane A's `f8e4m3fnuz` is KIND-only and cannot demonstrate it. **INCREMENT 2+ recorded in the function's own doc comment, not built:** the airtight co-generating macro; kernel-level declines; per-backend `<backend>_storage_status` reusing the shared TYPE; and the **external-trigger canary form — a wildcard-free match over a non-`#[non_exhaustive]` external enum, which fires at COMPILE time on a dependency bump** (settled on GAP-171 after the lane's first `try_from("U32")` sketch was found not to compile). | **CLOSED** `625c6b4d` (increment 1); increment 2+ recorded in-code as a stated limit |
| GAP-162 | (B) the shared device-guard helper — the refactor that removes GAP-157's reproduction mechanism | B | **SCHEDULED (fresh session — a 40-file refactor at the tail of a long day is the half-migrated-trait shape to avoid). Filed in the mover's words; the FACTS live on GAP-157, this row is the DESIGN, which is the non-mechanical part.** **GATING SHAPE — THE ONE PLACE (B) CAN GO WRONG:** the shared helper must be **ABSENT when its feature is off, never STUBBED to a no-op.** A test-support module (shared `tests/common/mod.rs` per live-test crate, or a small always-compiled `fuel-test-support` crate) exposing `cuda_dev_or_fail() -> CudaDevice` under `#[cfg(feature="cuda")]` and `vulkan_backend_or_fail() -> VulkanBackend` under `#[cfg(feature="vulkan")]`, **each PANICKING on a missing device — never returning `Option`, so there is no silent-`None` path to inherit.** **FORBIDDEN BY CONSTRUCTION: a helper behind `#[cfg(any(cuda,vulkan))]` that on a plain build resolves to an empty/no-op stub — that is the gap eating its own fix.** A shared decision point that silently compiles out is **GAP-157's undeclared requirement at the fixture layer**, and **GAP-161's decline-whose-justification-expires one level up** — the same class. **Rule: NO no-feature arm. A miswired caller (references `cuda_dev_or_fail` without `--features cuda`) must be a COMPILE ERROR (fn absent), not a silent no-op** — caller's `#![cfg]` and helper's `#[cfg]` then match by construction. **REPOINT ORDER:** one crate at a time (`fuel-dispatch` -> `fuel-core` -> `fuel-cuda-backend` tests + the 5 in-module `baracuda/` copies -> `fuel-vulkan-backend` -> `fuel-parallel`), each verified under its feature build, **reusing the warm baracuda cache from the tri_backend exemplar build (no cold `ptxas` re-pay).** **`tri_backend` stays FILE-LOCAL** — its Vulkan iGPU-by-name / refuse-NVIDIA selection is unique and unshareable; the shared helper covers only the ~45 identical `dev_or_skip`/`backend_or_skip` copies. **WHAT VERIFIES IT — THREE CHECKS, BECAUSE POLARITY AND GATING ARE SEPARATE RISKS: (1)** each converted file compiles under its feature (mechanical, cache-warm); **(2)** the shared helper is **ABSENT, not stubbed**, with no features — a `#[cfg(not(any(feature="cuda",feature="vulkan")))]` **`compile_fail`** check that referencing it without the feature does **not** compile; **(3)** polarity is intended — a device-less `--ignored` run **FAILS**. **Check (2) is the one most refactors would omit, and it is the only one that tests the gating rather than the mechanics.** **HALF-MIGRATION MARKER:** the `tri_backend` exemplar comment **stays until** the shared helper is the single source and all 40 files use it — so the intermediate state is self-describing rather than looking like an inconsistency. | SCHEDULED |
| GAP-163 | A STANDING aarch64 cross-check — the gate that would have caught GAP-160's `I8` gap in May | P | **ASSIGNED to `bzfevnl6`. This is the fix for the CLASS, not for Metal: `DType::I8` went uncovered for ~3 months for exactly one reason — NOTHING COMPILES THAT CODE.** A check that compiles it is what would have caught it on day one. **THE CAPABILITY, discovered 2026-08-08 while disproving GAP-160's premise:** `cargo check --target aarch64-apple-darwin` on this x86 Windows box **compiles the entire aarch64 dependency chain** — `fuel-cpu-kernels` (17 warnings, `neon.rs` x21 sites), `fuel-quantized` (4, `neon.rs` x5), `fuel-cpu-backend` (2). **These are NEON code paths this machine has NEVER compiled, and they are now compiler-adjudicated for free: no GPU, no Mac, no CI, no device.** **SCOPE IT TO THE CRATES THAT ARE GREEN TODAY, AND EXPLICITLY EXCLUDE `fuel-metal-backend`** — it is 26 errors deep (GAP-160), and **a standing gate that is RED ON ARRIVAL gets disabled within a week**, which is worse than not having one. Excluding it is a declared limit, not an oversight. **DELIVERABLE: a runnable check plus its documented invocation — and, applying GAP-160's own rule to this gate, A STATEMENT OF WHICH PHASES IT REACHES.** If it only reaches type-check on those crates, the gate must say so; **a gate that does not declare its phase coverage is the same defect it exists to catch.** **CHASE THE ONE SUBSTANTIVE WARNING SPECIFICALLY: `falling back to 'f32' as the trait bound 'f32: From<f64>' is not satisfied`.** *"The numeric path silently degrades on aarch64"* would be a real defect **hiding inside a warning nobody has ever read** — and unlike the other 22, it is about behaviour rather than style. **DELIVERED 2026-08-08 @ `72638aa5` — `pwsh scripts/aarch64-cross-check.ps1`.** Scoped to the three green crates, `fuel-metal-backend` excluded as directed. **The gate states its own phase coverage in BOTH directions, in its PASS message rather than only its docs:** REACHES parse / macro expansion / name resolution / type-check / **match exhaustiveness (E0004)** / borrow-check; **DOES NOT reach** monomorphization of uninstantiated generics, codegen, **LINKING**, runtime. **So it cannot say the aarch64 build links, and it cannot say the NEON kernels are numerically correct — compiling a NEON path and that path computing the right answer are different claims, and only hardware settles the second.** **TWO BUGS IN THE GATE, BOTH FOUND BY TESTING IT RATHER THAN TRUSTING IT — and the second is the more valuable finding of the day.** **(1) FALSE RED ON A WARM CACHE:** the first draft grepped for `Checking <crate>` lines, and **cargo does not reprint those when every unit is fresh**, so the gate failed on a cached run. **That is the architect's own red-on-arrival warning arriving from an unanticipated direction — a gate nobody trusts, via a mechanism unrelated to the code under test.** Now parses `--message-format json` for `compiler-artifact`. **(2) RIGHT VERDICT, WRONG DIAGNOSIS:** a **failed** compile and a **never-attempted** compile both produce no artifact, so checking artifacts first reported a genuine `E0004` in `fuel-quantized` as *"the compiler did not reach these crates"* — **true about artifacts, and a wrong diagnosis pointing the reader at the harness instead of at their own code.** Cargo's exit is now checked first. ***THE RULE THIS ESTABLISHES, IN THE MOVER'S WORDS: the sabotage's job is usually to prove the gate goes RED. Here it went red for the RIGHT REASON and said the WRONG THING — which a pass/fail-only check could never surface. VALIDATING A GATE MEANS READING ITS MESSAGE, NOT JUST ITS EXIT CODE.*** Both directions validated: planted non-exhaustive `DType` match -> exit 101, `E0004 seen: 6`; reverted -> exit 0. **⚠️ THE ARCHITECT'S `f32: From<f64>` HYPOTHESIS WAS WRONG AND IS WITHDRAWN.** It was called *"the one substantive item… behaviour rather than style"* **from the warning STRING, without checking whether it was aarch64-specific.** **It is not — it appears identically on x86**, in a log already on hand. The macro instantiates at `f32` and `f64`; for `f32` rustc falls back and produces the **correct value**. **It is a future-compat lint, not silent numeric degradation.** *"The numeric path silently degrades on aarch64"* would have been a real defect had it been true — **and it is exactly the sort of claim that becomes fact once it is in a registry.** The mover tested before chasing, which is the only reason it did not land here as one. Fixed anyway (`1.0_f32` satisfies both instantiations); **the change is to the lint, not the math.** **THE GATE JUSTIFIED ITSELF ON DAY ONE: 21 x `E0133` `unsafe_op_in_unsafe_fn` warnings** (`rust_2024_compatibility`) across the NEON paths — **real future-compat debt in code this box had never compiled until today**, surfaced by the gate's first run. Out of the gate's scope to fix; carved out here as its own follow-up. **`fuel-metal-backend` JOINED THE GATE 2026-08-08 @ `cbb775d0`, after being repaired to green (GAP-160) — never before, per the red-on-arrival rule. The gate now covers FOUR crates, PASS exit 0.** And it carries the Metal limit in its own PASS output, not merely its docs: *"COMPILE-correct is not KERNEL-correct. No Metal hardware here and no Metal runtime test exists, so this says its types line up — NOT that any shader computes the right answer."* **Read a PASS as "the rot is gone", never as "Metal is supported."** | DELIVERED |
| GAP-164 | `.cargo/config.toml` — `[build] rustflags = ["-C", "target-cpu=native"]` leaks the HOST cpu into every CROSS-compile | P | **Found by the GAP-163 gate on its first run, and reported rather than fixed because it is a repo-wide codegen-flag decision affecting every lane's builds, not a lane's call.** `[build] rustflags` applies to **all** targets, so an aarch64 cross-check is handed the host's `znver4`, producing **~20 KB of "not a recognized processor/feature" chatter that would BURY any real diagnostic.** Ignored by LLVM and harmless to correctness — **but a host cpu-tuning flag is meaningless for a cross-target, and burying diagnostics is how a gate stops being read.** The gate overrides it for its own target; **the repo-wide question is open.** **THE OBVIOUS FIX HAS A SUBTLETY THAT MUST NOT BE SKIPPED — this is why it is not a one-line change.** Moving to `[target.x86_64-pc-windows-msvc] rustflags` host-scopes it, and cargo does **not merge** the two: when a `[target.<triple>]` entry matches, `[build] rustflags` is **ignored entirely**, so the host build sees the same flags and the cross build sees none. **BUT `[target.<triple>].rustflags` does NOT apply to build scripts or proc-macros** (which compile for the host), whereas `[build].rustflags` **does**. **So the move is behaviour-preserving for the crate graph and a REAL CHANGE for build scripts** — they would lose `target-cpu=native`. **That is almost certainly fine and possibly desirable, but it is a codegen change to every build script in the workspace and it must be stated, not discovered.** **~~RECOMMENDATION: host-scope it, and say in the commit that build scripts lose `target-cpu=native`.~~ SUPERSEDED — the real config made this both smaller and different, and the recommendation was written without reading it.** **FIXED 2026-08-08 by the architect. The file ALREADY uses per-target overrides TWICE** (`wasm32-unknown-unknown`, `x86_64-apple-darwin`) — **so `aarch64-apple-darwin` was not a design flaw needing `[build]` restructured, it was a MISSING ENTRY in a pattern the file already establishes.** Adding one touches no other target, no host build, and no build script — **strictly safer than the host-scoping move recommended above, which would have changed build-script codegen flags for a problem that didn't require it.** **⚠️ AND THE FIRST FIX WAS A NO-OP THAT LOOKED LIKE A FIX — caught ONLY by measuring it.** `rustflags = []` **does NOT displace `[build] rustflags`: cargo treats an empty list as UNSET and falls back**, so the host flag returns silently. Measured with the empty override in place and correctly on disk: **still 4816 `not a recognized processor` lines.** The entry must be **non-empty** to displace. **A config edit that lands correctly, reads correctly, and does nothing is exactly the class this registry keeps recording — and the only reason it did not ship is that the change was re-measured instead of reasoned about.** **SHIPPED: `[target.aarch64-apple-darwin] rustflags = ["-C", "target-cpu=generic"]`.** `generic` rather than a specific Apple core because **this target is type-checked and never executed, so any tuning choice is unverifiable** — while `native` is an outright lie, naming an x86 core to an ARM target. **NEON is baseline-mandatory in ARMv8, so `generic` still compiles the NEON paths the gate exists to reach.** **RESULT: 4816 -> 0 chatter lines; total log 5083 -> 292.** `[build]` now carries a comment stating that any NEW cross-target needs its own entry or it silently inherits the host flag. | FIXED |
| GAP-165 | T5 / Whisper — encoder-decoder decode is a DIFFERENT contract from `DecodeModel` | C | **A TRACKED SUB-SCOPE OF GAP-029, NOT A DECLINE. CireSnave scoped GAP-029 to all 11 families as one lane, so this must read the way GAP-078/098 read: carved out, numbered, owned, VISIBLE.** Ruled 2026-08-08 on the gap-029 lane's recommendation. **THE REASON, stated so the carve-out is defensible rather than convenient: cross-attention KV has DIFFERENT LIFETIME SEMANTICS.** Self-attention KV is **appended every decode step**; cross-attention KV is **computed ONCE from the encoder and then read-only for the entire generation.** **A trait method meaning *"extend the cache with this step's keys"* has NO MEANING for cross-attn — that is not a parameter difference, it is a different contract.** Folding it into `DecodeModel` would either **distort those methods for the 9 decoder-only families** or push encoder-decoder through a shape that lies about what it does. **Neither is better than a second row.** **SEQUENCED AFTER the 7-family trait lands** (GAP-029), because the decoder-only contract is the thing this one has to be designed *against* — designing both at once means designing the shared part twice with no evidence. | OPEN — sub-scope of GAP-029 |
| GAP-166 | `fuel-inference/src/multi_session.rs:77` — `DecodeModel::n_kv_heads()` / `head_dim()` vs `fuel-core/src/lazy_deepseek2.rs:563` | C | **`DecodeModel`'s geometry methods ASSERT a KV cache shape that MLA does not have — and the failure is SHIPPED, not prospective.** Found by the GAP-029 lane 2026-08-12 while designing the trait *against* the describe-do-not-assert constraint; they found the constraint already violated on `main` in the present tense. **Filed as its own row rather than a line on GAP-098 because GAP-098 is *LFM2 is not built*, and this is *a shipped trait invites a wrong allocation* — different defects.** **MEASURED (all verified by the architect against `origin/main`, not relayed):** the trait requires `n_kv_heads()` and `head_dim()`, both documented *"(cache geometry)"*. `DeepSeek2Model` is decode-capable **today** — `decode_shape_key` (`lazy_deepseek2.rs:499`), `rebind_and_realize_prebuilt_mla` (`:1373`) — and has neither, because MLA's decode state is a `LazyLatentCache`, not a `KvCache`: per layer, slot 0 is the post-norm compressed latent trailing `[kv_lora_rank]`, slot 1 the post-RoPE `k_pe`. **TWO INDEPENDENT AXES OF MISMATCH, and the second is the one that gets missed because it is not about geometry at all: (1) STATE KIND** (`LazyLatentCache` vs `KvCache`); **(2) OWNERSHIP SHAPE** — `forward_with_latent_cache(&self, tokens, cache: LazyLatentCache) -> Result<(LazyTensor, LazyLatentCache)>` threads the cache **BY VALUE AND RETURNS IT**, where the trait takes `&mut KvCache`. **⚠️ THE SHARP PART IS NOT THAT DEEPSEEK2 CANNOT IMPLEMENT THE TRAIT — IT IS THAT IT CAN, AND WRONGLY.** `DeepSeek2Config` has `num_attention_heads` (`:70`) and `v_head_dim` (`:90`), so **both methods are syntactically returnable.** *(FACT: the fields exist, verified. CONSEQUENCE, which is REASONING FROM THE TRAIT'S STATED CONTRACT AND WAS NOT RUN: a scheduler that trusts them allocates a standard `KvCache` of `[n_kv_heads, head_dim] x n_layers` for a model whose decode path never reads a `KvCache` at all — it type-checks, it runs, and it allocates the wrong state.* The lane marked that split itself; it is preserved rather than smoothed over.)* **This is the LFM2 failure mode GAP-098 flags as prospective, except already shipped.** **WHERE THE CONFLATION LIVES — ONE SENTENCE, and it is the generalisable part.** `n_layers`' doc says the geometry is *"the KV cache geometry the scheduler builds a session's private cache against (**all sessions share one model, so this is uniform**)"*. That parenthetical is a **TRUE justification for uniformity ACROSS SESSIONS** — and the methods then use it to assert uniformity **ACROSS LAYERS and ACROSS STATE KINDS**, which it does not establish. **A true reason attached to a wider claim than it supports: the citation is real, so the claim reads as sourced, and the scope gap is invisible unless you ask what the reason actually licenses.** See CLAUDE.md. **SCOPE: DeepSeek2 is OUTSIDE GAP-029's 11** (positive-controlled — the 11 `lazy_quantized_*.rs` files are gemma3, glm4, lfm2, llama, phi3, qwen2, qwen3, qwen3_moe, smollm3, t5, whisper; there is no `lazy_quantized_deepseek2.rs`). **NOT a port and NOT a widening of GAP-029.** **FALSIFICATION ATTEMPT RUN 2026-08-12 (GAP-029 lane, spec §3.3 @ `6c6e64aa`) — IT SPLIT, AND NEITHER HALF WAS THE EXPECTED ANSWER. The value of the exercise is that it did not come back "yes it fits" on both axes, which would have been the same artifact as not having tried.** **AXIS 1 (state kind) — EXPRESSIBLE, and the vocabulary DID NOT HAVE TO BE INVENTED: it is shipped TWICE.** `LazyLatentCache::new(…, slot_trailing: Vec<Vec<usize>>, …)` (`lazy_latent_cache.rs:74`, readers `n_slots()` `:226` / `slot_trailing(slot)` `:228`) and `LatentKvCache::with_capacity(…, slot_trailing, …)` (`inference_context.rs:651`) — the device-resident twin, **in the same file as `KvCache`**. Concretely: KV layer = `vec![vec![n_kv_heads, head_dim]; 2]`, MLA layer = `vec![vec![kv_lora_rank], vec![qk_rope_head_dim]]`. **So `KvCache` is the 2-slot SPECIAL CASE, and `(n_kv_heads, head_dim)` is not a more primitive fact than `slot_trailing` — it is one inhabitant of it.** That upgrades the describing spelling from *defensible generality* to *the generalization the tree already made twice and `DecodeModel` never adopted.* **⚠️ AND THE CATCH, WHICH IS WHY ATTEMPTING BEATS CONCLUDING: `slot_trailing` is ONE list applied to EVERY layer** (`new()` loops `for _ in 0..n_layers`). **It describes state KIND generically while still ASSERTING uniformity across LAYERS — adopted as-is it buys one of the two dimensions and FEELS like both.** So the vocabulary must be `slot_trailing` **indexed by layer**. Note that lands on the independently-derived *"`layer_idx` is a real parameter, not a loop counter"* from the opposite direction. **AXIS 2 (ownership) — DOES NOT CARRY OVER, and the architect's stated REASON was wrong in both halves.** I predicted ownership would slip and guessed by-value was the stronger guarantee. Measured: **all six validation errors in `forward_with_latent_cache_impl` fire BEFORE any mutation, so MLA's all-or-nothing comes from EARLY VALIDATION — a property independent of the threading**, and by-value is carrying a persistent-lazy-rebuild idiom (cf. `advance_by(mut self) -> Self` `:200`), not an error-semantics choice. **THE REAL CONSEQUENCE, which is not obvious: under by-value, on `Err` the cache is CONSUMED AND DROPPED — so there is nothing left for `advance_batched` to route to the serial arm**, which is precisely what the batched arm's "before mutating any cache" contract relies on. `&mut Self::State` can serve MLA via `mem::replace`, **but it CHANGES MLA's error contract and must be written deliberately.** Ruled: `&mut Self::State`, **recorded as a choice with a named cost** — an inherited default and a deliberate choice are indistinguishable in the code and completely different in the next reader's hands. **BOTH FINDINGS WERE INVISIBLE TO THE EXISTING CORPUS: eight uniform-KV models satisfy either spelling, returning a clean 8-of-8 that certifies nothing** ([[control-design-corpus-traps]], [[justification-scope-mismatch]]). | OPEN — vocabulary ruled per-layer + `&mut` with named cost; GAP-170 carries the allocator half |
| GAP-167 | METHOD — a test at the WRONG SITE, not a weak test | C | **A CORRECT, SINGLE-SOURCED, TESTED, GREEN FIX CAN BE EVIDENCE ABOUT A DIFFERENT QUESTION THAN THE ONE IT IS LABELLED WITH.** Carved out of GAP-155 on the KISS architect's framing, kept as its own row because it generalises past dtypes and past KISS. **THE LINE, theirs verbatim: *"A test at the wrong site is not weaker evidence than a test at the right one; it is evidence about a different question wearing the right label."*** **THE INSTANCE:** GAP-155 was hours from landing a reserved-KISS-dtype recognition fix — single source of truth, matchable typed error, three tests — on `fuel-ir/src/dtype.rs::from_str`, **a function that parses Fuel's INTERNAL token vocabulary** (`"f8e4m3"`), reachable only by Fuel's own graph-attr deserialization. The tokens it was written to recognise (`"f8e4m3fnuz"`) **cannot arrive there.** It would have gone green, and **its greenness would have been cited as KISS-Classify conformance evidence.** **HOW THIS DIFFERS FROM THE ROWS AROUND IT, which is why it is not a duplicate:** [[self-checking-has-a-ceiling]] and [[control-design-corpus-traps]] are about an instrument that **cannot observe** the property (degenerate corpus, non-discriminating predicate). **This is the adjacent failure — an instrument that works PERFECTLY and is aimed where the property does not live.** No control on the instrument's sensitivity catches it, because the instrument is fine. **THE CHECK THAT DOES CATCH IT, and it is one question: before writing the test, ask WHICH SURFACE THE OBLIGATION BINDS, and prove the trigger can REACH that surface.** For a conformance obligation: whose act is it (reader? emitter? provider?), and does the input the clause describes physically arrive at the code under test? **In this case the answer was a positive-controlled absence — Fuel has NO structure_key token reader at all (zero dtype-token parse arms vs 116 op-token arms in `fkc/lower.rs`), so the obligation had no site in Fuel to land on.** **Family: [[gap-077-flip-is-inert]] (a correct audited flip with 0 live triggers), [[enumerate-the-population-not-the-strings]] (counting the wrong construct). The shared root: the artifact was verified, and what it was verified ABOUT was assumed.** **Standing rule, now in CLAUDE.md.** | **CLOSED** — recorded as method; rule in CLAUDE.md |

Not whether we have a `DType::<name>`, but whether we have the code to do things with it.* Plus: *declining a dtype we could easily support is acceptable ONLY as a short-term workaround until the code exists.*** So this row is a **sequenced build program**, not a compliance checklist, and a decline here is a scheduled debt rather than an answer. **MEASURED against sk4 (`origin/main:conformance/src/structure_key.rs`, `SCHEMA_VERSION = 4`, `DTYPES: [&str; 24]`): 14 of 24 have a Fuel `DType`; 2 are reserved and correctly have none (GAP-155); 8 HAVE NONE — `u16`, `u64`, `bool`, `i4`, `u4`, `b1`, `c64`, `c128`.** **⚠️ THEY ARE NOT 8 EQUIVALENT LINE ITEMS, AND REPORTING THEM AS ONE NUMBER IS THE MISTAKE TO AVOID — four different sizes of job, one of which is not a dtype addition at all:** **(a) `u16`, `u64` — MECHANICAL.** Pure additions; the measured cost is the `e5m2` ripple (~20 sites / 7 crates per dtype). No design questions. **(b) `i4`, `u4` — REUSES EXISTING MACHINERY.** KISS specifies packed-pair byte, low nibble = even index, sign- vs zero-extended on read — the layout Fuel already implements for `F4`. **(c) `bool` — A SEMANTICS DECISION, NOT PLUMBING, and it needs CireSnave's ruling because it changes an existing public surface.** Measured: **every Fuel comparison returns a `U8` MASK** (`fuel-graph/src/lib.rs:385-386`; `fuel-ir/src/dispatch.rs:222-233` *"output is a `U8` mask (`1` where equal, `0` otherwise)"*), and `grad.rs:146` records the mask as non-differentiable. Only `u16` and `u64` have NO `ElementKind` at all.** So **the descriptor/seam boundary is not the wall for six of the eight.** **⚠️⚠️ AND THE CAVEAT IS THE WHOLE POINT, because it is the exact error this row exists to prevent, one remove out: THIS IS EVIDENCE ABOUT VOCABULARY, NOT CAPABILITY. sk3/sk4's rule is that **byte-incompatible hardware variants MUST NOT share a token** and *"the ambiguous bare `e4m3` is gone."* **FIX: rename to `F8E4M3FN` / `"f8e4m3fn"` and put the bias/max/inf/NaN facts in the doc.** Three reasons: **(1)** Fuel's `F8E5M2` **already** matches sk4's `f8e5m2` exactly, so the divergence exists **only** on e4m3 — an artifact, not a policy; it does not find `"f8e4m3"` inside captured/replayed graph attributes on disk. **`runtime_fused.rs:339/:473` deserialize `cast_dtype` through `DType::from_str`, so an existing capture containing `"f8e4m3"` silently fails to deserialize after the rename.** Either accept the old spelling on parse (and say so) or migrate the captures — **decide before renaming, not after a replay breaks.** **RELATED, same class, settle before KISS PR-2 merges:** Fuel spells its 4-bit type bare **`f4`** (*"4-bit float (MX4 format)"*). Neither yields "18 spaces mid-sentence, no newline."** So the cause may be something else (adjacent-literal concatenation? `concat!`? a continuation whose next line is indented past the strip?), **and if it is, the discriminator being designed against is the wrong one.** **JOB: open 4–5 confirmed-genuine sites and read the SOURCE BYTES, not the string value. And `baracuda-kernels-sys` build output exists in `C:\Projects\fuel` ONLY (20 out-dirs); `fuel-bool`, `fuel-gap077` and `fuel-coord` have ZERO.** **THEREFORE A CUDA BUILD IN ANY WORKTREE COLD-STARTS THE ~56-MINUTE FORGE UNCONDITIONALLY, INDEPENDENT OF THE LOCKFILE.** The 14s peer run was either in a tree holding warm output or ran a command that never reaches the forge (`--features telemetry` pulls no baracuda dependency at all — see GAP-181). **So the lane's copied-lockfile workaround never had a mechanism to work through, and the row's refusal to assert *"committing the lockfile fixes cold forge starts"* was correct — the claim would have been FALSE, not merely unproven.** **⚠️ THE FIX IS NOT OBVIOUS AND MUST NOT BE APPLIED REFLEXIVELY — THE CHEAP-LOOKING ONE TRADES AN HOUR FOR A BOTTLENECK. If it hashes RAW TEXT it proves nothing here** and the round-trip must be built by hand: parse every checked-in `.fkc.md` under both parsers and diff the **lowered contracts**, not the YAML. **EXPLICIT NON-FIX: do NOT solve this by feature-gating FKC. The instinct about the hole was right; the explanation was right for that case and wrong for floats.** **CORRECTLY REFUSED AND WORTH KEEPING: routing `X -> Bool` through `cast_*_u8` would TRUNCATE (`5.7 -> 5`) and fabricate a non-canonical Bool. Declining beats shipping a plausible arm.** **BLAST RADIUS TODAY IS ZERO** — nothing in Fuel produces a U8 mask any more (swept: zero numeric-mask uses) — **but "zero today" is a measurement, not a guarantee, and under CireSnave's standing instruction (*declining a dtype we can easily support is acceptable only as a short-term workaround*) the FLOAT half is exactly the kind of decline that instruction forbids leaving permanent.** **⚠️ CONFIRMED INDEPENDENTLY BY THE LANE RATHER THAN TAKEN ON RELAY, WHICH IS THE RIGHT RESPONSE TO A COORDINATOR CORRECTION: `cuda/compare.fkc.md` ships exactly FOUR `ne` kernels (`ne_{f32,f64,f16,bf16}_u8`) and ZERO integer compare kernels (`grep -cE '^kernel: (eq\|ne\|lt\|le\|gt\|ge)_(u8\|i8\|u32\|i16\|i32\|i64)_'` -> 0). The split holds in both directions.** **⚠️⚠️ AND THE LANE WENT PAST THE CORRECTION IT WAS GIVEN, CORRECTLY — I ASKED FOR THE COMMENT TO BE FIXED, WHICH WOULD HAVE LEFT THE DEFECT INTACT.** A corrected comment over one returned value is **still** GAP-161's shape, merely better worded. `src_dtype` is in scope, so **the two reasons are now carried in the VALUE**: float source -> *"not wired — the `!= 0` kernels exist but are unreachable from a storage-level cast; needs a graph-level `x.ne(0)`"*; integer source -> *"no CUDA compare kernel at all"*. **A caller can now distinguish a wiring gap from an absence, which is the whole point and is exactly what a prose fix cannot deliver.** **That also sharpens the remaining work: the float kernels are UNREACHABLE FROM A STORAGE-LEVEL CAST, so the fix is a GRAPH-LEVEL lowering of `Cast(float -> Bool)` to `Ne(x, 0)` — not a storage-layer wiring change.** Truncation routing stays refused. **SHIPPED AND MERGED `f1c40270` — `Cast(float -> Bool)` now lowers to `Ne(x, x*0)` in `optimize()`, beside the identity-Cast rule, which is where the codebase already puts Cast rewrites. Born-red observed (*"float -> Bool must lower to Op::Ne, got Cast(Bool)"*), then green. IEEE behaviour pinned by a value test on the three inputs that discriminate: `-0.0 -> FALSE`, `NaN -> TRUE`, `±inf -> TRUE`.** Integer half untouched, truncation refusal untouched, two-reason decline value ships as-is. **⚠️ THE TRADEOFF, RULED RATHER THAN BURIED: the rewrite is UNCONDITIONAL, so CPU's 4 float->Bool cast kernels are bypassed on the `to_dtype` path (the 7 int->Bool ones are not). The lane flagged it instead of hiding it and did NOT delete the kernels.** **RULED KEEP, on three findings the lane did not have:** **(1) THE ESCAPE ROUTE DOES NOT EXIST — coordinator-verified with a positive control: Fuel has NO comparison-against-scalar op; the `*Scalar` family is only the affine pair `AddScalar`/`MulScalar`. So `Ne(x, x*0)` is the available spelling and the second pass is genuine, not an artifact of how it was written.** **(2) THE COST IS FAR SMALLER THAN IT SOUNDS, AND THIS SIZES IT: after GAP-168(c), comparisons produce `Bool` DIRECTLY (`fuel-graph/src/shape.rs:65`), so THE FRAMEWORK NO LONGER GENERATES `Cast(float -> Bool)` ANYWHERE — it is reachable only via an explicit user `to_dtype(Bool)`. The bypassed kernels sit on a path the framework itself stopped using when comparisons migrated.** The lane had reported the cost as if it were on every framework-built mask and **self-corrected the scope error**. **(3) UNCONDITIONALITY IS WHAT THE RECIPE PRINCIPLE DEMANDS, NOT A CONCESSION TO LAYERING. A `decompose` is TOTAL; that totality is what makes the base map a fixpoint and the cover step meaningful, so a capability-conditional decompose would break the thing the optimizer rests on.** The rejected alternative — hardcoding `== Cuda` in `optimize()` — would have inverted the constitution from the other side, putting a backend identity in the layer that is supposed to decide. **The lane refused it and was right.** **⚠️ A FALSE WARNING THE LANE WROTE AND THEN RETRACTED, kept because the retraction is the lesson: a draft comment claimed a future `x*0 => zeros` fold would *"silently turn NaN into FALSE"*. IT IS WRONG — `NaN != NaN` and `NaN != 0` are BOTH true, as are both inf cases, so the two spellings agree everywhere and the fold is SAFE. Exposed by writing the VALUE test, after reasoning about the sentence had already produced the wrong answer once.** **A plausible hazard that does not exist is still a false claim, and it is the DURABLE kind: nobody disproves a warning, they obey it — a future optimizer author would have skipped a safe fold forever on the strength of one sentence.** Corrected in place so the intuitive-but-wrong version cannot return. Same rule as *a guard must not encode a false sentence*, pointed at a comment. **Also correct: `Op::ZeroFill` was checked and rejected as the zeros source because it is DESTRUCTIVE (writes through input 0).** **NOT VERIFIED, AS STATED BY THE LANE: no live-GPU run. That makes this an internal inconsistency in v1's own definition for this case, not a feature request.** **FUEL IS THE MOTIVATING INSTANCE: `fuel-cuda-backend/src/baracuda/quant_w4a16.rs` ships THREE 4-bit weight-only GEMM families behind separate cargo features — Marlin (symmetric int4, GPTQ-derived), AWQ (asymmetric int4), NF4 (bitsandbytes). MARLIN AND NF4 ARE BOTH SYMMETRIC 4-BIT, so they are one key and two kernels.** **⚠️ AND THIS PAIR IS WORSE THAN "different kernel", WHICH IS THE WHOLE POINT: NF4 IS A CODEBOOK (16 non-uniform values via lookup); MARLIN IS LINEAR (`q * scale`). THERE IS NO SCALE FOR WHICH A LINEAR DEQUANT REPRODUCES NF4 — the difference is the INTERPRETATION OF IDENTICAL BITS, which is exactly why dtype does not separate them.** So a cache keyed on `structure_key` alone does not pick a slower kernel; **it picks a NUMERICALLY WRONG one, silently.** Fuel's own `nf4_matmul` decompose — *nibble-unpack + indicator-sum codebook + per-block scale* — has no linear spelling, which is the same fact from the recipe side. **⚠️ STATUS IS LATENT, NOT LIVE, AND THAT QUALIFIER WAS REPORTED BEFORE THE SEVERITY: `Encoding::AffineBlock` has ZERO non-test, non-defining consumers — declared, not wired — so FUEL IS NOT EMITTING COLLIDING KEYS TODAY.** The `Encoding` enum also reserves `AffineInt` / `AffineFloat` *"for LATER (do NOT implement now)"*, **so the second family that triggers the collision is already named in Fuel's own source as planned work.** KISS's note on the ordering is worth keeping: *reporting "not a live wrong answer today" BEFORE the severity is what makes the severity believable; the opposite order would have been a stronger-sounding report and a weaker one.* **INDEPENDENT CORROBORATION THAT PREDATES THE CONVERSATION: `fuel-ir/src/stype.rs:47` already read *"FDX has no distinct NF4 code in v1 — see plan deferred list"*. Someone reached the same gap FROM THE ENCODING DIRECTION before the op-family question existed — two independent routes to one finding, worth more than either alone.** **FUEL-SIDE OBLIGATION, WHICH DOES NOT DEPEND ON KISS ACTING: when a SECOND `AffineBlock` family is wired, kernel selection MUST NOT key on `structure_key` alone. PRESCRIBED INSTRUMENT — a witness asserting that exactly ONE quant family produces `AffineBlock` today, failing when a second lands, with a message naming this row.** Same shape as GAP-169's expiry witness: **assert the PREMISE (one family today), not the conclusion**, so the deferral expires on a checkpoint that will occur rather than on someone remembering. **OPEN QUESTION KISS DELIBERATELY DID NOT ASSERT, recorded because the discipline is the point: whether v1's exclusion was decided KNOWING a codebook format exists whose bits are indistinguishable from a linear one. If it was, #189 is a v2 pointer against a known cost; if it was not, the case list was incomplete. The decision record is unrecoverable and guessing would be the exact failure both projects have spent two days cataloguing.** **⚠️ WITNESS LANDED AND MERGED `3d2f907a`: `gap190_affine_block_cannot_distinguish_codebook_from_linear` (`fuel-ir/src/stype.rs`) asserts THE PREMISE — Marlin (linear `q*scale`) and NF4 (codebook) produce the SAME `Encoding::AffineBlock` and collide under `Hash` too, which is what a plan cache actually keys on. AWQ is asymmetric and therefore separable, which is why this row names Marlin/NF4 and not AWQ — stated in the test.** **EXPIRES AT COMPILE TIME, not only by assertion: the descriptor is built FIELD-BY-FIELD, so the day the variant gains a discriminator it STOPS COMPILING and the author must decide what each family sets; if the arms are instead made to differ, the assert fails. TWO WAYS TO TRIP, BOTH ON CHECKPOINTS THAT WILL OCCUR.** **Born-red by PREDICATE PERTURBATION (block extent 64->32 -> RED), so the expensive control — a fake variant on an EXHAUSTIVE-BY-DESIGN enum — was not paid for; and THE MESSAGE WAS READ, not just the verdict. Non-vacuity asserted in-test.** Premise control DISCRIMINATES: `AffineBlock` has zero production constructors while sibling `GgmlBlock` IS found in production, so the query demonstrably finds wired variants when they exist. **⚠️⚠️ THE LANE STATED THE WITNESS'S BOUND RATHER THAN LETTING THE GREEN STAND FOR MORE: `fuel-ir` CANNOT SEE ITS OWN CONSUMERS, so this fires on the DESCRIPTOR'S SHAPE and NOT on the event the obligation is actually about — a second quant family getting WIRED in `fuel-cuda-backend`. Marlin wired as `AffineBlock { packed: F4, block_shape: [64], zero_point: None }` — THE LIKELY CASE — SLIPS PAST ENTIRELY.** The invariant writable at that layer is *"the type cannot distinguish them"*, **not** *"only one family exists"*. Written into the test's own doc where the next reader meets it. **RESIDUAL SPLIT OUT AS GAP-192 rather than left as a footnote on a closed row.** | **WITNESS LANDED `3d2f907a`** — type-level collision pinned and compile-expiring; **the WIRING trigger is NOT covered and is now GAP-192**; upstream KISS #189 |
| GAP-191 | A compositional op family emits N cells where a fused implementation emits 1 — so a future `gat` byte-match positive would MISMATCH while both sides are correct | C | **FILED 2026-08-13 from a question the KISS architect raised on #193 and deliberately did NOT answer, put to the family table's maintainers instead.** Fuel expresses `gat` compositionally (`une` x `bin`: `gate.silu().mul(&up)`), so **Fuel emits TWO structure-key cells where an implementation with a fused SwiGLU kernel emits ONE. Two implementations agreeing on SEMANTICS disagree on the NUMBER OF CELLS** — a byte-match question, not a vocabulary one. **NOT BITING TODAY, stated before the consequence: Fuel's byte-match is 20/20 over the PUBLISHED positives and NONE of them is a `gat` vector.** This is a **known future divergence**, filed so that if a `gat` positive is ever published, **a cell-count mismatch is not read as a Fuel defect** by whoever hits it. **FUEL'S ARCHITECTURE OFFERS A CANDIDATE ANSWER, OFFERED UPSTREAM AS A HYPOTHESIS AND EXPLICITLY NOT AS A PROPOSAL: every fused op ships BOTH a `decompose` and a `pattern`, and optimization is lower-to-base-map then find-best-cover — so a backend WITH a fused kernel covers the subgraph and emits one cell, a backend WITHOUT one emits two, FROM THE SAME GRAPH. If that generalises, cell count is a fact about THE BACKEND'S KERNEL INVENTORY, not about the family vocabulary — and it can differ between two backends of the SAME implementation.** **⚠️ THREE BOUNDS STATED WITH IT, because the hypothesis is not free: (i) it assumes the key describes the graph AFTER cover, which is Fuel's arrangement and may not be every consumer's; (ii) FUEL HAS NO FUSED ACTIVATION OP TODAY, so this is ARCHITECTURE, NOT OBSERVATION — Fuel is unconditionally the two-cells case right now; (iii) it says nothing about whether a family exists precisely to REQUIRE a fused implementation for numerics or performance, and IF SOME DO, THE COVER STEP IS EXACTLY THE WRONG PLACE TO DECIDE IT.** **KISS'S HONEST LIMIT, recorded because it bounds Fuel's own table: one consumer's op inventory is ONE DATA POINT — evidence a family CAN be expressed compositionally, not that it always is or must be. Two independent consumers agreeing would be a materially stronger basis.** | OPEN — unallocated; **not biting (no `gat` positive published)**; upstream KISS #193; **Fuel's job is to not let a future cell-count mismatch be misread as a defect** |
| GAP-192 | GAP-190's obligation has NO detector for the event it is about — "a second quant family got WIRED" cannot be seen from `fuel-ir` | C | **SPLIT OUT OF GAP-190 2026-08-13, on the implementing lane's own stated bound rather than on review.** GAP-190's witness pins that `Encoding::AffineBlock` **cannot distinguish** a codebook family from a linear one, and expires at compile time if the descriptor gains a discriminator. **What it CANNOT do is fire when a second family is wired using the SAME descriptor shape — which is the likely case and the actual hazard.** **WHY IT MATTERS: Marlin (linear `q*scale`) and NF4 (CODEBOOK) collide on a v1 `structure_key` because `quant` is carried for binding and excluded from the admissibility key. THERE IS NO SCALE FOR WHICH A LINEAR DEQUANT REPRODUCES NF4 — so a cache keyed on the key alone does not pick a slower kernel, IT PICKS A NUMERICALLY WRONG ONE, SILENTLY.** **LATENT TODAY, and the control discriminates: `Encoding::AffineBlock` has ZERO production constructors, while sibling `GgmlBlock` IS found in production — so the query finds wired variants when they exist.** Fuel ships the three kernels (Marlin, AWQ, NF4) that make it bite; only the `Encoding` plumbing is absent. **THE QUESTION, NOT A PREDETERMINED ANSWER: where can a detector for "a second family is now wired" live, and what does each option cost?** Candidates to price and to REJECT WITH REASONS — a workspace-scope scan; a test in the crate where wiring happens; a registry-side obligation with no automation (**honest but weakest, and if that is the answer it must be SAID as the weaker thing**); or something structural making a second family's wiring a compile error. **⚠️ DO NOT WEAKEN GAP-190'S WITNESS — it is CORRECT for what it asserts, and its value is precisely that it refused to assert more.** A finding that no good detector exists is a legitimate outcome. **⚠️⚠️ OPTION MAP DELIVERED 2026-08-13, AND ITS CORE FINDING CORRECTS GAP-190's STATED OBLIGATION — VERIFIED BY THE ARCHITECT AT HEAD BEFORE BEING ACCEPTED, per the *a correction is a claim* rule: `KernelBindingTable` is keyed on `(BindingKey, KernelDTypes, BackendId)` (`kernel.rs:946`), NOT on `structure_key`; and `plan.rs:1044` computes the key AFTER the winner is chosen, recording it into the telemetry record at `:1066` (*"Fuel CALLS the provider, never derives"*).** **SO FUEL DOES NOT SELECT ON `structure_key` AT ALL, AND GAP-190's OBLIGATION AS THE ARCHITECT WROTE IT — *"kernel selection must not key on `structure_key` alone"* — WAS AIMED AT THE WRONG ACTOR. The mis-selection happens in BARACUDA'S cache. Fuel's real obligation is narrower and different in kind: DO NOT EMIT A KEY THAT CANNOT DISTINGUISH, AND DETECT THE MOMENT FUEL WOULD START.** The collision can only be RESOLVED upstream (KISS #189, a family discriminator on `AFFINE_BLOCK`); everything Fuel-side is DETECTION. **RULED: BUILD (A) + KEEP (C).** **(A) WORKSPACE-SCOPE SCAN asserting ZERO production `Encoding::AffineBlock {` constructors — and THE LANE'S ARGUMENT IS BETTER THAN THE ROW IT WAS GIVEN: this row specified a detector for the SECOND family; the right trigger is the FIRST, because the first `AffineBlock` wiring is the moment someone must decide the whole strategy including whether to push KISS #189. A second-family detector fires one decision too late.** Strong LOCAL PRECEDENT: same shape as `FUEL_SK4_SPELLINGS`, a pinned baseline kept *"so a change is a decision with a diff"* — which caught a real red today. **A legitimate NF4 wiring bumping 0->1 and failing IS THE REVIEW, not a false positive.** REQUIREMENTS: count the CONSTRUCT not a string; **positive control IN THE TEST** (`GgmlBlock` IS found in production, so zero is falsifiable); exclude tests and say how; and **the failure message must say WHAT TO DECIDE — that Marlin (linear) and NF4 (codebook) collide, that no scale makes a linear dequant reproduce NF4, that the resolution is upstream — because a tripwire whose message is "the count changed" gets baselined away.** **(B) TEST-IN-THE-WIRING-CRATE REJECTED, and the reason is recorded so nobody proposes it again: THERE IS NO FUEL-SIDE `structure_key`-KEYED SELECTION REGISTRY to attach duplicate-detection to, and building one would re-implement the discriminator Fuel does not have.** **(D) STRUCTURAL COMPILE-ERROR (a choke-point taking a `QuantFamily` enum + exhaustive match) REJECTED AS PREMATURE — speculative infra before the first wiring exists, with zero constructors today. ⚠️ BUT RECORDED AS THE SHAPE TO REACH FOR IF (A) FIRES, because at that moment it stops being speculative.** Its TRUE form is the upstream discriminator, not available to Fuel alone without diverging from the v1 key. **⚠️ DO NOT WEAKEN GAP-190's `fuel-ir` WITNESS — it asserts the SHAPE-level collision and expires at compile time; (A) complements it at the WIRING level.** | **ALLOCATED (Fuel 1) — BUILD (A) + keep (C)**; B and D rejected WITH REASONS; **GAP-190's obligation corrected: Fuel emits, baracuda selects** |
| GAP-143 | — | **Process rule: a documented rule does not generalize itself — the durable form is mechanical, not remembered.** Filed by the decode owner against their own work, which is why it's credible. `ec471d2b` documented that *an exit code of either polarity is evidence about the harness, not the code*, derived from a `cmd /c` invocation that compiled nothing and exited 0. **~2 hours later** the same author wrote `git rebase … \| tail -3 && git push`, where `tail`'s exit 0 masked a **conflicted rebase** and the push went out with the main commit unapplied. Same defect class, new syntax; knowing the rule did not fire. **Durable form: never place a pipe between an operation and an `&&` that depends on its success** — capture to a file, test `$?`, then branch. Same shape as the `--all-targets` lesson (`gate-must-match-change-kind`): the fix is to **derive the check from the operation**, not to remember harder. Pairs with GAP-142 — 142 is about inherited claims, 143 is about inherited *rules*. | STANDING |
